# Retail divergence backlog

Behavioural differences between this reconstruction and the retail
`gladiator.dll`, recovered by a routine-by-routine audit of all 33 original
translation units against `dev_tools/gladiator.dll.bndb_hlil.txt`, with
`ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/` as a cross-check. Every entry below
survived an independent adversarial re-check against the HLIL; claims that
did not survive are not listed.

| | |
| --- | ---: |
| Retail routines compared | 756 |
| Raw findings | 109 |
| Confirmed | 80 |
| Refuted on re-check | 28 |
| High / medium / low | 13 / 38 / 29 |
| Applied / reverted / open | 24 / 1 / 55 |

`applied` entries are fixed in the tree and covered by the suite. `reverted`
entries were implemented, found to move test outcomes that need separate
triage, and backed out with the evidence retained here. Everything else is
open.

Read a routine's HLIL with the line span given for each entry, e.g.
`Read dev_tools/gladiator.dll.bndb_hlil.txt offset=16382 limit=60`.
See [retail_function_map.md](retail_function_map.md) for the full
address-to-translation-unit index.

## `src/botlib/aas/aas_local.h`

### MEDIUM Weapon-jump area flag is 0x20 instead of retail's 0x2000

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_SetWeaponJumpAreaFlags / AAS_Reachability_WeaponJump` @ `0x10017a9b (set), 0x10017cbc (test)`
- HLIL: 19268 `ecx_2:1.b = (*(eax_91 + (edx_3 << 2) + 4)):1.b | 0x20` — OR of 0x20 into byte 1 of areaflags, i.e. `areaflags |= 0x2000`; 20288-20289 `((*(data_10066954 + arg2 * 0x1c + 4)).w:1.b & 0x20) != 0` — test of areaflags bit 13
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:2590 `aasworld.areasettings[areanum].areaflags |= 0x2000u;` and :2632
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_local.h:317`

**Divergence.** `src/botlib/aas/aas_local.h:317` defines `AAS_AREA_WEAPONJUMP` as 32 (bit 5); retail uses 0x2000 (bit 13). HLIL 0x10017a9b line 19268 `ecx_2:1.b = (*(eax_91 + (edx_3 << 2) + 4)):1.b | 0x20` ORs 0x20 into byte 1 of `areasettings[areanum].areaflags` (stride 0x1c, field offset 4), i.e. `areaflags |= 0x2000`; HLIL 0x10017cbc line 20289 `((*(data_10066954 + arg2 * 0x1c + 4)).w:1.b & 0x20) != 0` tests the same bit 13. Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:2590 and :2632, and Q3 `AREA_WEAPONJUMP 8192`. The divergence is NOT observable in bot behaviour. Our setter (aas_reach.c:4037) and tester (aas_reach.c:4081-4082) use the same constant and always run in the same reachability-generation pass, and no other retail areaflags read uses a mask beyond `& 1`/`& 2`/`& 4`, so the marked-area set and the generated weapon-jump reachabilities are identical to retail. The observable divergence is confined to the emitted .aas file: AAS_WriteAASFile (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:9390-9394) writes `aasworld.areasettings` verbatim into Q2_AAS_LUMP_AREASETTINGS, and that path is reached whenever `aasworld.saveFile` is set (aas_reach.c:270 -> aas_main.c:173-182). For any map carrying a weapon-jump item classname, our areasettings lump therefore has bit 5 set where retail has bit 13. Bit 5 is not a retail areaflag at all, so the lump is not byte-compatible with retail output, and any later generation pass over such a file begins from a different pre-seeded flag state.

**Failure scenario.** Generate reachability for any map with a rocketlauncher/railgun/quad: retail writes 0x2000 into the areaflags of those areas and the .aas on disk carries bit 13; our .aas carries bit 5 instead. A retail gladiator.dll loading our .aas finds no weapon-jump areas and generates zero rocket-jump reachabilities, and conversely a retail-generated .aas read by us has no bit 5 set. Bit 5 is also not a retail areaflag, so the emitted file is not byte-compatible in the areasettings lump.

**Fix.** In `src/botlib/aas/aas_local.h:317`, change #define AAS_AREA_WEAPONJUMP 32 to #define AAS_AREA_WEAPONJUMP 8192 /* 0x2000, retail: or ch, 0x20 at 0x10017a9b */ No other define moves: `AAS_AREA_GROUNDED 1`, `AAS_AREA_LADDER 2`, `AAS_AREA_LIQUID 4`, `AAS_AREA_DISABLED 8` and `AAS_AREA_BRIDGE 16` already match retail/Q3 and do not collide with bit 13. The two use sites (`src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:4037` and `:4081-4082`) need no edit since they reference the macro. `tests/aas/test_aas_map.c:4676-4678` asserts through the macro as well and will follow automatically, but should be re-run to confirm.

## `src/botlib/aas/aas_main.c`

### MEDIUM AAS_ContinueInit skips AAS_Optimize/AAS_WriteAASFile when the AAS filename is empty

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_ContinueInit` @ `0x1000df30`
- HLIL: 12420-12437
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_main.c:175`

**Divergence.** src/botlib/aas/aas_main.c:175 gates the optimize+write block on `shouldwrite && aasworld.aasFilePath[0] != '\0'`, but retail's AAS_ContinueInit (0x1000df30, HLIL 12423 `if (data_100667e8 != 0 || eax_2 != 0)`) gates it on `aasworld.savefile || (int)LibVarGetValue("forcewrite")` alone, with no test on aasworld.filename. Retail never clears aasworld.filename anywhere — data_100667f0 has only two writes in the whole DLL, both in BotLibLoadMap's loose/pak branch (HLIL 12982, 12985) — and the aasN.zip fallback returns BLERR_NOERROR before reaching them (HLIL 12991-12994), so a zip-sourced AAS runs AAS_ContinueInit with a stale (previous map's) or zero-filled filename. We instead force that buffer empty in two places retail has no counterpart for: AAS_ClearAASData (aas_map.c:8252; retail's AAS_DumpAASData clears savefile but not filename) and the zipped branch of AAS_LoadMap (aas_map.c:9130). Consequently, whenever a zip-sourced AAS is combined with savefile (set by AAS_InitClustering when numclusters < 1 or forceclustering/forcereachability is set, or by AAS_InitReachability) or with `forcewrite 1`, retail runs AAS_Optimize — which swaps compacted vertex/edge/face arrays into aasworld and thereby changes every subsequent sample/reachability/route result — and then calls AAS_WriteAASFile on the stale/empty name, printing "%s written succesfully\n" at PRT_MESSAGE or "couldn't write %s\n" at PRT_ERROR. We skip the optimize, skip the write, and print nothing.

**Failure scenario.** Map's .aas is pulled out of aas0.zip and `forcewrite 1` (or the loader set aasworld.savefile). Retail: runs AAS_Optimize(), which rewrites the in-memory vertex/edge/face/area lumps that routing and reachability then use, and then calls AAS_WriteAASFile on the stale/empty filename, printing `couldn't write %s\n` at PRT_ERROR. Ours: neither optimizes nor writes and prints nothing, so the in-memory AAS data (and every route computed from it) differs from retail's for the rest of the level.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/aas/aas_main.c:175 drop the `&& aasworld.aasFilePath[0] != '\0'` conjunct so the block is gated only by `aasworld.saveFile || forcewrite`, matching HLIL 12423. That alone is not sufficient: also delete both invented clears of the buffer so it can carry a stale value the way retail's does — `aasworld.aasFilePath[0] = '\0';` at aas_map.c:8252 in AAS_ClearAASData (retail's AAS_DumpAASData never touches data_100667f0) and the zipped branch at aas_map.c:9128-9131, collapsing that if/else down to the unconditional archived ? logicalPath : physicalPath copy that mirrors HLIL 12981-12985. After this, aasFilePath is written only where retail writes aasworld.filename, and AAS_WriteAASFile must be able to take an empty or stale path and fail cleanly so the PRT_ERROR "couldn't write %s\n" diagnostic is emitted as retail emits it.

### LOW Invented AAS_ContinueInit failure diagnostic and early return retail has no counterpart for

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_ContinueInit` @ `0x1000df30`
- HLIL: 12437-12440
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_main.c:196`

**Divergence.** src/botlib/aas/aas_main.c:196-201 adds a failure exit and a PRT_ERROR diagnostic that retail's AAS_ContinueInit (sub_1000df30) does not have. HLIL 12439-12440 shows retail's tail after the optional write block is unconditional: `j_sub_10019520()` (AAS_InitRouting) followed by `return j_sub_1000df00() __tailcall` (AAS_SetInitialized -> data_100667e4 = 1 and PRT_MESSAGE "AAS initialized.\n", HLIL 12399-12400). Retail's only early exit in the routine is the `if (result == 0)` guard on `j_sub_10018920()` = AAS_ContinueInitReachability (HLIL 12414/12416), which our line 166 already reproduces. Retail references no error string in this routine at all. Impact is limited, not the every-frame spam loop the claim describes for ordinary play: AAS_PrepareReachability has already been executed successfully on the same data before line 196 is reached — at src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:9211 on the .aas-file load path (a failure there aborts the map load) and at src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:1457 inside AAS_StoreReachability on the in-game reachability-generation path (where it is deliberately non-fatal, only warning). The line 196 call is therefore a redundant rebuild of the same tables, and its data-driven failure branches (missing areasettings, firstreachablearea+numreachableareas > numreachability, the reachability-areas index bound) cannot fire there. Only a calloc failure can trigger it, and under OOM retail's GetClearedMemory-based AAS_CreateReversedReachability would fault rather than print. So the concrete divergence is: an error string not present in the retail binary, plus an extra exit that leaves aasworld.initialized clear where retail always sets it and prints "AAS initialized.\n".

**Failure scenario.** If AAS_PrepareReachability ever returns non-zero (e.g. a calloc failure in aas_reach.c:4792/4798/4807 on a large map, or the missing-areasettings branch), retail would still call AAS_InitRouting and AAS_SetInitialized and print 'AAS initialized.'; ours prints a bot-library error string that does not exist in the retail image and returns with initialized still false, so AAS_StartFrame re-enters AAS_ContinueInit every frame and re-runs AAS_InitClustering/AAS_Optimize and re-prints the error each frame forever.

**Fix.** Keep the AAS_PrepareReachability call at aas_main.c:196 — that position corresponds to retail's AAS_CreateReversedReachability (sub_10018df0), invoked from AAS_InitRouting (sub_10019520, HLIL 21458-21463; ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_route.c:318-328) — but drop the early return and the invented text, so the routine has retail's single straight-line exit: (void)AAS_PrepareReachability(); (void)AAS_InitRetailRoutingCaches(); aasworld.initialized = qtrue; BotLib_Print(PRT_MESSAGE, "AAS initialized.\n"); Do not take the claim's first option (deleting the call because "aas_map.c:9211 already does it"): on the in-game generation path aas_map.c:9211 runs while aasworld.reachability is still empty and returns immediately; it is aas_reach.c:1457 inside AAS_StoreReachability that covers that path. Deleting the call would leave the reversed-reachability rebuild depending solely on that site, which is a larger change than parity requires. Separately, the sibling PRT_ERROR at src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:1458-1461 ("AAS_StoreReachability: failed to prepare generated reachability\n") is another string with no retail counterpart in this call chain and is worth auditing under its own claim.

## `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c`

### HIGH SOLID_BBOX start-solid test uses an expanded box; retail uses a box shrunk by 0.5

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_EntityCollision` @ `0x10003680`
- HLIL: 4999-5040 (100037bd..10003853)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_bspq2.c:417-423
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:890`

**Divergence.** src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:886-896 (AAS_TraceEntityBBox) inverts the sign and the strictness of retail's SOLID_BBOX start-solid margin. Retail AAS_EntityCollision @ 0x10003680 (HLIL 4995-5040, deciding lines 100037c2/100037cf `expandedmins[i] + 0.5 < start[i]` and 100037d6/100037e3 `expandedmaxs[i] - 0.5 <= start[i] -> bail`) reports start-solid only when, on all three axes, `expandedmins[i] + 0.5 < start[i] < expandedmaxs[i] - 0.5` — the Minkowski-expanded box SHRUNK by 0.5 per side, strict interior — and only when entdata.solid == 2 (HLIL 4995; ref be_aas_bspq2.c:412-423). Our loop keeps startsinside whenever `expandedmins[i] - 0.5 <= start[i] <= expandedmaxs[i] + 0.5`, i.e. the box GROWN by 0.5 per side, inclusive. Our start-solid region is therefore 1.0 unit wider per face on every axis and a strict superset of retail's: a bot whose box merely touches (or sits up to 0.5 units clear of) a SOLID_BBOX entity gets startsolid = allsolid = 1, fraction = 0, endpos = start written straight into the caller's bsp_trace_t at aas_map.c:913, which AAS_AreaEntityCollision propagates at aas_map.c:2449-2451, while retail breaks out of the loop and falls through to the axial sweep at 1000385b. Additionally, our `(entity->solid == SOLID_BBOX) ? 0.5f : 0.0f` still runs the start-solid test for SOLID_BSP entities that reach AAS_TraceEntityBBox (invalid model number path from AAS_EntityCollision at aas_map.c:2004-2031); retail runs no start-solid test at all when entdata.solid == 3.

**Failure scenario.** A bot standing flush against a SOLID_BBOX entity (item, player, monster): the bot origin lands exactly on `expandedmins[axis]` for one axis (bot box face touching the entity box face). Retail: `start <= v41 + 0.5` breaks the loop, so no start-solid; it falls through to the sweep and returns an ordinary hit with a plane normal and a positive fraction. Ours: start is within `expandedmins - 0.5`, so `startsinside` stays true and we write `startsolid = allsolid = 1, fraction = 0, endpos = start` straight into the caller's trace. AAS_AreaEntityCollision then propagates `trace->startsolid = 1` (aas_map.c:2449) into AAS_TraceClientBBox, so the bot is reported stuck-in-solid whenever it merely touches another entity.

**Fix.** Gate the block on SOLID_BBOX and flip both the margin sign and the strictness, matching HLIL 100037bd-100037f3 / ref be_aas_bspq2.c:417-423: qboolean startsinside = qfalse; if (entity->solid == SOLID_BBOX) { startsinside = qtrue; for (int axis = 0; axis < 3; ++axis) { if (start[axis] <= expandedmins[axis] + 0.5f || start[axis] >= expandedmaxs[axis] - 0.5f) { startsinside = qfalse; break; } } } Drop the `startsolidmargin` variable — the 0.5 is unconditional inside the SOLID_BBOX branch, and SOLID_BSP must skip the test entirely rather than run it with a 0.0 margin. The write block that follows (startsolid/allsolid = 1, fraction = 0, contents = 0, sidenum = -1, zeroed plane, endpos = start, ent = entnum, unconditional `*trace = local`) already matches HLIL 10003805-10003853 and needs no change. Note retail's constant is a QWORD 0.5 (`fadd qword`), but 0.5 is exactly representable so float vs double is not observable here.

### HIGH BSP brush plane expansion is applied with the wrong sign (brush is shrunk by the trace box instead of expanded)

- status: **applied**
- retail: `CM_TraceThroughBrush` @ `0x10003c90`
- HLIL: 5386-5425 (10003eef..10003fa2), 5432-5452 (10003e5b..10003ea3)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_bspq2.c:693-722 (vec[]/dir[] selection and `v20 = v19 + v38`)
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:1211`

**Divergence.** src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:1201-1222 (AAS_BSPTracePlaneOffset), used at :1340 and :1558 — the sign of the BSP brush/node plane box-expansion is inverted. Retail CM_TraceThroughBrush (0x10003C90..0x100041BC) forms the expanded plane distance as `d' = planedist - DotProduct(offset, normal)`, where `offset[k] = normal[k] > 0 ? mins[k] : maxs[k]` and `planedist` already carries the +/-DotProduct(normal, modelorigin) term. Proof, general path: HLIL 10003f28 selects `normal[k] <= 0 ? maxs[k] : mins[k]` into vec[]; 10003f30 `var_3c = fneg(var_54)` plus 10003f74 `x87_r7_29 = var_28*fneg(var_4c) + var_2c*fneg(var_50) + var_30*var_3c` gives `-DotProduct(vec, normal)`; 10003f80 `x87_r6_6 = x87_r7_29 + var_74_1`. Axial path: 10003e77-10003e84 yields `normal[type] > 0 ? -mins[type] : +maxs[type]`, added at 10003ea3 — consistent with the general formula. Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_bspq2.c:662-722. Our AAS_BSPTracePlaneOffset uses the same offset selection but returns `+DotProduct(offset, normal)`, and both call sites add it (`expandedDist = planedist + offset`). Because that dot product is non-positive for any normal when mins<=0<=maxs, our expanded planes move inward: every BSP brush is shrunk by the trace box instead of grown by it. The wrong-signed value is also stored into trace->exp_dist (:1399). Effect: with a mover face {normal=(1,0,0), dist=16} and a bot box mins=(-16,-16,-24)/maxs=(16,16,32), retail's expanded plane sits at x=32 and blocks a sweep whose centre is at x=20; ours sits at x=0, startdist=+20>0 on that side, and the brush reports no hit. Brushes thinner than the presence box invert entirely and can never be hit. Reachable via AAS_EntityCollision's SOLID_BSP branch (aas_map.c:2004-2016), i.e. all door/plat/mover collision inside AAS_TraceClientBBox. Correction to the claim as filed: line :1558 is not simply the same expression in retail. Retail's node walker sub_100044F0 computes TWO offsets — HLIL 5968-6006: `arg_74` = mins-selected `-dot(...)` (axial `-boxmins[type]`) and `arg_78` = maxs-selected `-dot(...)` (axial `-boxmaxs[type]`), ref be_aas_bspq2.c:1122-1151 v133/v134 — and subtracts each from the raw front/back distances to drive a paired side-flag test (HLIL 10004b5b-10004ba2). Our node walker collapses that to a single expanded plane, which is a separate structural divergence on top of the sign error.

**Failure scenario.** AAS_TraceBSPModel is reachable only from AAS_EntityCollision's SOLID_BSP branch (HLIL 5202, j_sub_100044f0 called from 10003a8d), i.e. door/plat/mover collision during AAS_TraceClientBBox. Take a mover brush spanning x in [-16,16] with a right face plane {normal=(1,0,0), dist=16} and a bot presence box mins=(-16,-16,-24)/maxs=(16,16,32). Retail's expanded plane is at x = 16 - (1*-16) = 32, so a bot centre at x=20 is inside the brush and the sweep is blocked. Our expanded plane is at x = 16 + (-16) = 0, so a bot centre at x=20 is 20 units in front of the plane, `startdist > 0` on that side, and the brush reports no hit — the bot walks through the mover. Symmetrically, brushes thinner than the presence box invert entirely and can never be hit.

**Fix.** Negate the accumulation inside AAS_BSPTracePlaneOffset so it returns `-DotProduct(offsetvec, normal)`, leaving both call sites as `planedist + offset`: if (normal[component] < 0.0f) offset -= normal[component] * ((maxs != NULL) ? maxs[component] : 0.0f); else offset -= normal[component] * ((mins != NULL) ? mins[component] : 0.0f); Axial sanity check against HLIL 10003e77-10003e84: for normal = +1 on an axis the returned offset must be `-mins[axis]`; for normal = -1 it must be `+maxs[axis]`. Both then satisfy `expandedDist = planedist + offset`, matching retail 10003ea3 / 10003f80. Do not instead flip the sign at the two call sites unless you also update trace->exp_dist at :1399, which retail stores as the same `v19` offset value (ref be_aas_bspq2.c:733 `v43 = v19`, HLIL 10004029 `var_64 = fconvert.s(x87_r6_6)`) — note retail stores the *offset* there in the ref rendering versus the full expanded dist in HLIL, so re-check that field separately. Separately (out of scope of this one-line fix, file as its own finding): aas_map.c:1558 should be reworked to retail's two-offset node descent — evaluate the split plane against both the mins-selected and the maxs-selected offset and combine the resulting side-flag pair, per HLIL 5968-6006 / 10004b5b-10004ba2.

### HIGH BSP node descent classifies the box against one expanded plane; retail uses both box-side offsets

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_TraceBSPModel` @ `0x100044f0`
- HLIL: 6013-6015 (100049af..100049bb), 6018-6060 (10004b4d..10004be9)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_bspq2.c:1122-1123, 1132-1151, 1153-1185
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:1568`

**Divergence.** AAS_TraceBSPModel (sub_100044f0 @ 0x100044f0) keeps TWO plane offsets per node in the boxed path and gates the two children independently; we keep one and make the two gates mutually exclusive. Retail (HLIL lines 6005-6006, 6007-6070, 6075, 6098): - `100049af arg_74 = -boxmins[type]`, `100049bb arg_78 = -boxmaxs[type]` (axial); the non-axial arm at 10004a8d..10004b3c selects boxmins/boxmaxs per component by the sign of the normal into v148/v149 and sets arg_74 = -dot(v148,n) = -minSupport, arg_78 = -dot(v149,n) = -maxSupport. - The loop 10004b5b..10004be9 subtracts BOTH offsets from both endpoint distances and writes four flags: arg_64/arg_68 for the boxmins offset, arg_6c/arg_70 for the boxmaxs offset (index 2*pair + role; role 0 = both dists > -0.005 "in front", role 1 = both dists < 0.005 "behind"). - `10004c03 if ((&arg_64)[side] != 0 || (&arg_64)[side+2] != 0)` pushes children[side] with the FULL unclipped segment; `10004c78` repeats it for the opposite role/child. Both children can be pushed. Only when both offsets agree (10004c59 / 10004cdd) is the sibling skipped. - Since frontA implies frontB and behindB implies behindA, retail's effective gates are: descend children[0] iff d + maxSupport > -0.005 at both endpoints; descend children[1] iff d + minSupport < 0.005 at both endpoints, with d(p) = DotProduct(p, normal) - planedist, minSupport = dot of the box corner minimising dot(corner, n), maxSupport = the maximising corner. Ours (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:1558): one `expandedDist = planedist + AAS_BSPTracePlaneOffset(normal, mins, maxs)`, and AAS_BSPTracePlaneOffset (aas_map.c:1201) returns minSupport, so front/back = d - minSupport = d + |minSupport|. Line 1568 uses it for the children[0] test and line 1583 reuses the same value for the children[1] test, and the first branch `continue`s so they can never both fire. Consequence: we skip children[1] whenever d > minSupport at both endpoints (d > -16 for x/y planes, d > -24 for +z with the {-16,-16,-24}/{16,16,32} presence box), where retail skips it only when d > ~+16 / +24. That is a 32-unit (x/y) to 56-unit (z) band around every split plane in which we prune a subtree retail traverses. Secondarily, our single offset -minSupport equals retail's front offset +maxSupport only for origin-symmetric boxes, so the children[0] gate also differs on z-ish planes (d+24 vs retail's d+32 for n=(0,0,1)). Corrected failure scenario (the one in the claim is inverted — with the centre line at x=-10 our front value is +6, so we push children[0], the same front child retail visits): plane {normal=(1,0,0), dist=0}, presence box mins(-16,-16,-24)/maxs(16,16,32), segment (8,-100,0) -> (8,100,0), with a solid mover brush at x in [-8,-4] in the back child. - Ours: expandedDist = 0 + mins[0] = -16, front = back = 8 + 16 = 24 > -0.005 -> push children[0] only; children[1] never visited, brush never clipped, trace reports fraction 1. - Retail: frontA = 8-16 = -8 (false), frontB = 8+16 = 24 (true) -> children[0] pushed; frontA && frontB false so it does not skip the sibling; behindA = -8 < 0.005 (true) -> children[1] pushed with the full segment and the brush is clipped. The box swept from x=-8 to x=24 genuinely overlaps the brush, so we return a clear trace through solid mover geometry.

**Failure scenario.** Trace a 32x32x56 presence box through a mover whose splitting plane {normal=(1,0,0), dist=0} separates a leaf containing a brush at x in [0,8] (front child) from empty space behind. With the trace centre line running at x=-10 from start to end, the correctly-offset front test rejects the front child, and our back test (using the same front offset) also passes, so only the back child is pushed. Retail's second offset (`-boxmaxs[0] = -16`) puts the box's +x face at x=+6, so it also descends the front child and clips against the brush. Result: our trace passes through a mover brush the bot's box actually overlaps.

**Fix.** In AAS_TraceBSPModelTree (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:1554-1639) carry both supports per node instead of one, mirroring 100049af/100049bb: - minSupport = sum_k normal[k] * (normal[k] > 0 ? mins[k] : maxs[k]) — this is exactly what AAS_BSPTracePlaneOffset already returns, so keep it. - maxSupport = sum_k normal[k] * (normal[k] > 0 ? maxs[k] : mins[k]) — add the complementary selector (retail builds it in the same v148/v149 pass, matching the `normal[k] > 0.0f` strictness; the k with normal[k]==0 contributes 0 either way). With d(p) = DotProduct(p, normal) - planedist, replace the mutually exclusive 3-way split with two independent gates: - frontStart = d(current.start) + maxSupport; frontEnd = d(current.end) + maxSupport; push children[0] iff frontStart > -0.005f && frontEnd > -0.005f. - backStart = d(current.start) + minSupport; backEnd = d(current.end) + minSupport; push children[1] iff backStart < 0.005f && backEnd < 0.005f. Both gates may fire; when they do, retail hands each child the ORIGINAL unclipped start/end (10004c0c and 10004c86 copy i_11[0..5] / i_7[0..5] straight from the parent entry), so do not clip in that case. Retail only computes a split point in the residual case where, for the pair belonging to that child, neither "wholly in front" nor "wholly behind" holds (10004cf7 / 10004d68), and it derives the fraction from that pair's own adjusted distances: `arg_a4[i] / (arg_a4[i] - arg_b0[i])`, i.e. frontStart/(frontStart-frontEnd) for the front child and backStart/(backStart-backEnd) for the back child — not from a single shared `front/(front-back)` as at line 1599. Related, and worth checking as a separate item while touching this: the brush clipper at aas_map.c:1340 uses the same `planedist + minSupport`, but retail's CM_TraceThroughBrush (ref be_aas_bspq2.c:662-723, gladiator.dll 10004310..1000448D) computes `v19 = -boxmins[type]` (axial, normal>0) / `v19 = -dot(vec, normal) = -minSupport` (non-axial) and then `v20 = v19 + plane->dist`, i.e. planedist MINUS minSupport — the opposite sign, which expands the brush by the box instead of shrinking it.

### HIGH AAS entity area-links skip the presence-type bbox expansion (AAS_LinkEntityClientBBox is not implemented)

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_LinkEntityClientBBox` @ `0x1001c620`
- HLIL: 23914-23929 (plus caller at 10432-10433: `1000ab14 esi[0x1f] = j_sub_1001c620(&var_c, &var_18, ebx, 2)`)
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:9988`

**Divergence.** AAS_LinkEntityClientBBox (retail sub_1001c620 @ 0x1001c620, HLIL 23914-23929) is not implemented in src/. Retail's AAS_UpdateEntity (sub_1000a920) links every changed entity into the AAS area tree through it — `1000ab14 esi[0x1f] = j_sub_1001c620(&var_c, &var_18, ebx, 2)` — and that routine first fetches the presence-type box via AAS_PresenceTypeBoundingBox (sub_1000dda0) and forms newabsmins = absmins - maxs (1001c649), newabsmaxs = absmaxs - mins (1001c66d) before calling AAS_AASLinkEntity (sub_1001c460, 1001c696). With retail's presencetype literal 2, sub_1000dda0 selects table index 2, which is mins {-16,-16,-24} and maxs {16,16,8} — note that in this DLL the literal 4 selects the TALL box {16,16,32} and 2 selects the SHORT box {16,16,8}, the opposite of Q3 (our aas_map.c:556-604 already gets this right). So retail actually links each entity over absmins-(16,16,8) .. absmaxs+(16,16,24): grown 16 units horizontally, 8 units down and 24 units up. (The earlier statement of "16 horizontally, 32 down, 24 up" used the presencetype-4 row and is wrong.) That expansion is exactly the set of client origins p for which the swept presence box [p+mins, p+maxs] touches the entity, so arealinkedentities[A] in retail contains every entity a client standing anywhere in area A could collide with. Our AAS_UpdateEntity (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:10086-10092) feeds the raw origin+mins/origin+maxs box into AAS_LinkEntityToComputedAreas, which calls AAS_BBoxAreas with those raw bounds at src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:9988. Neither AAS_BBoxAreas (:2333) nor AAS_LinkEntityToArea (:9666) expands, AAS_LinkEntityToComputedAreas has only that one caller, and AAS_PresenceTypeBoundingBox is never called on any linking path. The single consumer of the resulting lists, AAS_AreaEntityCollision (aas_map.c:2402-2442, retail sub_1001b130 @ 1001b130, called from AAS_TraceClientBBox / sub_1001b260 at 1001b351), therefore sees a short list: a bot in area A whose swept presence box would clip a door whose raw box lies wholly in the adjacent area B finds nothing, where retail reports the hit with a proper fraction. Net effect: entity-aware client traces (obstacle avoidance, door/mover handling, movement prediction blockers) miss movers within 16 units horizontally / 8-24 units vertically of an area boundary, and first see them only once the segment is already inside the mover's own area (startsolid / fraction 0).

**Failure scenario.** A func_door bmodel sits with its absolute bounds entirely inside AAS area B, 20 units past the A/B boundary plane. Retail links the door into every area touched by the box grown by 16 units horizontally, 32 down and 24 up - which includes area A. A bot in area A calls AAS_TraceClientBBox(PRESENCE_NORMAL): while the segment is still inside A, AAS_AreaEntityCollision walks arealinkedentities[A], finds the door link, sweeps the +/-16 presence box and reports the hit with the correct fraction. In our build the door is linked only to B, so the A segment tests against an empty list; the trace either passes through the door's approach entirely, or first sees it when the segment is already inside B and the swept box is already overlapping the door, producing bsptrace.startsolid / fraction 0 instead of a proper fraction. Net effect: entity-aware client traces (obstacle avoidance, door handling, AAS_ClientMovementPrediction blockers) miss movers within ~16-32 units of an area boundary.

**Fix.** Add AAS_LinkEntityClientBBox(const vec3_t absmins, const vec3_t absmaxs, int entnum, int presencetype) in src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c next to the existing link helpers (no new file): call AAS_PresenceTypeBoundingBox(presencetype, mins, maxs), then VectorSubtract(absmins, maxs, newabsmins) and VectorSubtract(absmaxs, mins, newabsmaxs), and link with those. In practice fold it into the existing structure: have AAS_LinkEntityToComputedAreas take the presencetype and apply the two subtractions before the AAS_BBoxAreas call at aas_map.c:9988, keeping AAS_LinkEntityToArea/AAS_SetEntityAreaBit unchanged. Call it from AAS_UpdateEntity's area-link path (aas_map.c:10092) with the bare literal 2, matching `1000ab14 j_sub_1001c620(&var_c, &var_18, ebx, 2)` — use the literal, not PRESENCE_NORMAL, because this DLL's AAS_PresenceTypeBoundingBox maps 2 to the SHORT box {-16,-16,-24}/{16,16,8}; naming it PRESENCE_NORMAL would invite someone to "fix" it to 4 and change behaviour. Add a comment citing 1001c620/1000ab14 and noting the 4-vs-2 table inversion documented at aas_map.c:565-570. Do NOT expand inside AAS_BBoxAreas itself — it is a public query used elsewhere and retail's sub_1001c460 does no expansion. Leave the BSP-leaf link untouched: `1000ab38 esi[0x20] = j_sub_10006210(&var_c, &var_18, ebx, 0)` passes the unexpanded box, so AAS_LinkEntityToBSPLeaves (aas_map.c:10098) must keep receiving the raw absmins/absmaxs. AAS_ClampMinsMaxs stays harmless since expansion only grows the box.

### HIGH AAS_UpdateEntity links entities to areas without the client-bbox expansion

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_UpdateEntity` @ `0x1000a920`
- HLIL: 10432-10435 (call); 23914-23929 (sub_1001c620 AAS_LinkEntityClientBBox); 12345-12385 (sub_1000dda0 AAS_PresenceTypeBoundingBox)
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:9988`

**Divergence.** Retail AAS_UpdateEntity (0x1000a920) relinks a moved entity into the AAS area tree through AAS_LinkEntityClientBBox with presencetype 2 — HLIL 10433 `esi[0x1f] = j_sub_1001c620(&var_c /*absmins*/, &var_18 /*absmaxs*/, ebx /*entnum*/, 2)` — and sub_1001c620 (HLIL 23914-23929) expands the box before linking: it calls AAS_PresenceTypeBoundingBox(2, mins, maxs) (sub_1000dda0, HLIL 12345-12385; presencetype 2 selects table index 2 -> mins {-16,-16,-24}, maxs {16,16,8}) and then links `absmins - maxs` .. `absmaxs - mins`, i.e. [absmins-(16,16,8), absmaxs+(16,16,24)] — wider by 16 in -x/-y, 8 in -z, 16 in +x/+y and 24 in +z. The BSP-leaf link on the very next line (HLIL 10435, sub_10006210 with arg4 = 0) correctly takes the raw box, so only the AAS-area link is expanded. Our AAS_UpdateEntity (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:10083-10099) computes the raw absmins/absmaxs and hands the identical pair to both AAS_LinkEntityToComputedAreas and AAS_LinkEntityToBSPLeaves. AAS_LinkEntityToComputedAreas calls AAS_BBoxAreas(absmins, absmaxs, ...) at src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:9988, and AAS_BBoxAreas (aas_map.c:2333-2361) only clamps and walks the node tree — no presence expansion anywhere. Nothing upstream supplies it either: BotUpdateEntity (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:3244-3277) and TranslateEntityUpdate (src/q2bridge/aas_translation.c:193-241) both pass the raw entity bounds. Our AAS_PresenceTypeBoundingBox (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:556-605) already reproduces retail's table and selector exactly; it is simply never invoked on the entity-link path. Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_sample.c:927-936 (AAS_LinkEntityClientBBox) and be_aas_entity.c:105/112 (the only call sites, presencetype 2). Effect: every entity is under-linked to AAS areas by up to 16 units horizontally and 24 units vertically. Since aasworld.areaEntityLists is read only by AAS_AreaEntityCollision (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2402-2426), reached from AAS_TraceClientBBox (aas_map.c:2545) when passent >= 0, a door or mover sitting within that margin of an area boundary is not found when a bot traces its client box through the neighbouring area: retail reports the entity as blocking, we return fraction 1.0 and treat the path as clear.

**Failure scenario.** A func_door whose absolute bbox lies wholly inside AAS area A but whose faces are 10 units from the boundary with area B. Retail links it to both A and B (its link box reaches 16 units further in x/y and 24 units further in +z). A bot then runs AAS_TraceClientBBox through area B; AAS_AreaEntityCollision walks aasworld.areaEntityLists[B] and finds the door, so the trace reports the door as blocking. In our build the door is linked to A only, areaEntityLists[B] is empty, the trace returns fraction 1.0, and the bot treats the reachability as clear -- it walks into the closed door and, for movers, AAS_EntityCollision is never consulted at all for the neighbouring area.

**Fix.** Apply the expansion only on the AAS-area link inside AAS_UpdateEntity (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c, at the relink block around line 10083-10099), leaving the BSP-leaf link on the raw box exactly as retail does at HLIL 10435: vec3_t absmins; vec3_t absmaxs; VectorAdd(entity->origin, entity->mins, absmins); VectorAdd(entity->origin, entity->maxs, absmaxs); AAS_ClampMinsMaxs(absmins, absmaxs); vec3_t pmins; vec3_t pmaxs; vec3_t linkmins; vec3_t linkmaxs; /* Retail 1000ab14: j_sub_1001c620(absmins, absmaxs, entnum, 2). */ AAS_PresenceTypeBoundingBox(PRESENCE_NORMAL, pmins, pmaxs); VectorSubtract(absmins, pmaxs, linkmins); VectorSubtract(absmaxs, pmins, linkmaxs); int linkStatus = AAS_LinkEntityToComputedAreas(entity, linkmins, linkmaxs); ... linkStatus = AAS_LinkEntityToBSPLeaves(entity, absmins, absmaxs); PRESENCE_NORMAL is 2 in src/botlib/aas/aas_local.h:285, matching retail's literal. Keep the existing AAS_ClampMinsMaxs on the raw box and apply the expansion after it (the expansion only widens, so the clamp stays a no-op on the link box). Do NOT move the expansion into AAS_BBoxAreas: in this DLL AAS_LinkEntityClientBBox is called only from the entity relink path (ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_entity.c:105/112 are its only call sites), so the reach-side AAS_BBoxAreas users in src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:1186 and :3446 must keep receiving unexpanded boxes.

### MEDIUM AAS_BSPModelMinsMaxsOrigin enumerates all 8 model corners; retail enumerates only 7 distinct ones

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_BSPModelMinsMaxsOrigin` @ `0x10005e60`
- HLIL: 6976-6995 (10005eef..10005f54)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_bspq2.c:1747-1756
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:1162`

**Divergence.** AAS_BSPModelMinsMaxsOrigin (retail 0x10005e60, HLIL 6976-6995) enumerates only 7 distinct AABB corners, not 8. Verified against the retail bytes in dev_tools/assets/gladiator.dll (file offset == RVA): the selectors are x: 0x10005eec `83 fb 04` cmp ebx,4 / 0x10005eef `7d 0a` jge -> i >= 4 ? maxs[0] : mins[0] y: 0x10005f03 `f6 c3 01` test bl,1 / 0x10005f06 `74 0a` jz -> (i & 1) ? mins[1] : maxs[1] z: 0x10005f1a `83 fb 02` cmp ebx,2 / `7c 0f` jl 0x10005f1f `83 fb 06` cmp ebx,6 / 0x10005f22 `7f 0a` jg -> (i < 2 || i > 6) ? mins[2] : maxs[2] The z immediate is literally 6 (a correct all-8-corner walk would need 5), so i=6 re-emits i=4's corner (maxs0,maxs1,maxs2) and the corner (maxs[0], maxs[1], mins[2]) is never generated. Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_bspq2.c:1751-1753. Our src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:1165-1167 uses the textbook (corner&1)/(corner&2)/(corner&4) selection and visits all 8 distinct corners. The rotation is v' = M*v with M row-major from AnglesToAxis (ref be_aas_bspq2.c:261-272), so for output component k our maxs[k] is strictly larger than retail's whenever row k of the axis matrix has sign pattern (+,+,-), and our mins[k] is strictly smaller whenever it has (-,-,+). Both require a genuine compound rotation: any single-axis rotation leaves a zero in one column, which makes the missing corner tie with one retail does visit, so pure pitch / pure yaw / pure roll are bit-identical. Gladiator's AnglesToAxis row 0 is (cp*cy, cp*sy, -sp), so pitch and yaw both in (0,90) hits the (+,+,-) case immediately. CORRECTION to the claim's blast radius: the divergence is NOT observable at aas_reach.c:3540 or bot_move.c:218/265/470. Those call sites pass an all-zero angles vector in our code and in retail alike (retail be_ai_move.c:193-204/256/401, be_aas_reach.c:2209, be_ai2_dmq2.c:1781-1784 all zero `angles` immediately before the call), and with zero angles retail's 7 corners still cover mins and maxs on all three axes, so both implementations return the model bounds exactly. The single reachable divergence path is AAS_UpdateEntity -- retail ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_entity.c:85 calls AAS_BSPModelMinsMaxsOrigin(ent->modelindex-1, ent->angles, ent->mins, ent->maxs, NULL) with the entity's live angles, mirrored by our src/q2bridge/aas_translation.c:223. So the divergence shows up as different cached per-entity mins/maxs (and hence different entity size/link extents) for SOLID_BSP movers carrying a compound rotation, e.g. a func_rotating whose map-authored `angles` is non-axis-aligned (SP_func_rotating never calls G_SetMovedir, so the map angles persist and combine with the spin axis).

**Failure scenario.** A mover with a compound rotation (any `angles` whose rotation matrix has a row with sign pattern (+,+,-), e.g. a func_rotating with both pitch and yaw), height-asymmetric bounds. Retail's returned maxs on that axis is short by the contribution of the missing corner; ours is correct/larger. AAS_BSPModelMinsMaxsOrigin feeds mover bounds in aas_reach.c:3540, bot_move.c:218/265/470 and q2bridge/aas_translation.c:223, so our bots compute a different stand-on/ride bbox for rotating brush models than retail. (For zero angles both implementations return the model's mins/maxs exactly, so only rotated models diverge.)

**Fix.** In src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c, replace the corner selection at lines 1165-1167 with the shipped selection, byte-for-byte in intent: local[0] = (corner < 4) ? model->mins[0] : model->maxs[0]; local[1] = (corner & 1) ? model->mins[1] : model->maxs[1]; local[2] = (corner < 2 || corner > 6) ? model->mins[2] : model->maxs[2]; Add a comment recording that the `> 6` is a retail defect being reproduced deliberately (the all-8-corner form would be `> 5`), otherwise a later cleanup pass will "fix" it back. The zero-angles short-circuit at aas_map.c:1138-1149 may stay: with the identity matrix retail's 7 corners still yield exactly model->mins / model->maxs, so the fast path is numerically identical. Removing it for literalness is optional, not required. Likewise the 999999.0f seeds at aas_map.c:1158-1159 vs retail's ClearBounds constants are unobservable for BSP-range coordinates and need no change. Regression test: with a compound rotation such as angles = (45, 45, 0) on a height-asymmetric model (e.g. mins = (-64,-64,-8), maxs = (64,64,8)), the returned maxs[0] must equal the retail value -- i.e. it must NOT include the contribution of the (maxs[0], maxs[1], mins[2]) corner. Drive it through the AAS_UpdateEntity path (src/q2bridge/aas_translation.c:223) with solid == SOLID_BSP, since that is the only call site that can pass nonzero angles.

### MEDIUM AAS entity linking drops AAS_AASLinkEntity's axial BoxOnPlaneSide fast path

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_AASLinkEntity` @ `0x1001c460`
- HLIL: 23873-23897
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2302`

**Divergence.** Retail AAS_AASLinkEntity (0x1001c460) inlines Quake's axial BoxOnPlaneSide fast path and uses the generic sub_1001c2e0 only as a fallback, selected by the signed test at 1001c541 `if (edx_10 s>= 3)` on plane->type (aas_plane_t +0x10). Decoded x87 masks: 1001c555 `(ah & 0x41) != 0` is C0|C3, i.e. `plane->dist <= absmins[type] -> side = 1`; 1001c56a `(ah & 1) != 0` is C0 alone, i.e. `plane->dist < absmaxs[type] -> side = 3`, else `side = 2`. Corroborated verbatim by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_sample.c:895-910 (the claim's "ref cite: none" is wrong). Our AAS_CollectBBoxAreasFromTree (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2302) always calls AAS_BoxOnPlaneSide with no plane->type branch. AAS_BoxOnPlaneSide (aas_map.c:2189-2218) is itself a faithful port of sub_1001c2e0 (front bit on max-corner dist >= 0, back bit on min-corner dist < 0), so the defect is the missing axial branch, not the generic routine. The two paths disagree on exactly one input class: `absmins[type] < plane->dist == absmaxs[type]`. Retail returns side = 2 (descend children[1] only); we compute maxdist = absmaxs[type] - dist = 0.0f exactly (normal is axial, so the DotProduct is exact), which is >= 0 and sets bit 1, plus mindist < 0 sets bit 2, giving side = 3 and an extra descent into children[0]. At the other boundary (dist == absmins[type]) both paths return 1, and for a zero-extent box (absmins[type] == absmaxs[type] == dist) both return 1, so this is the sole divergence. Consequence: the entity is additionally linked into the areas of the front subtree that lie flush against the plane — i.e. the areas face-adjacent to the entity's +axis face. This reaches the entity-link path via AAS_BBoxAreas -> AAS_LinkEntityToComputedAreas (aas_map.c:9988), and also affects the AAS_BBoxAreas callers in aas_reach.c:1186 and :3446. Any consumer that enumerates aasworld.arealinkedentities for an area (AAS_AreaEntityCollision and friends) then sees a mover in areas retail never links it to. The input is routine on real maps because AAS split planes and bmodel bounds are integer-valued, and retail's AAS_LinkEntityClientBBox (0x1001c620) expands by presence-type boxes that are integral too.

**Failure scenario.** An axial node plane (plane->type < 3) with plane->dist exactly equal to the entity's absmaxs on that axis - routine, since AAS split planes and door/plat bmodel bounds both land on integer coordinates. Retail's axial branch returns side = 2 and descends only children[1] (back). Ours computes maxdist = absmaxs - dist = 0 -> front bit set, mindist < 0 -> back bit set -> side = 3, and also descends children[0]. The entity is linked into every AAS area in that whole front subtree that retail never links it to, so AAS_AreaEntityCollision in those areas tests a mover retail never considers there and can return a blocked trace where retail returns fraction 1.0.

**Fix.** In AAS_CollectBBoxAreasFromTree (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2302), replace the unconditional generic call with retail's type split, keeping retail's signed comparison: const aas_plane_t *plane = &aasworld.planes[node->planenum]; int side; if (plane->type < 3) { if (plane->dist <= absmins[plane->type]) { side = 1; } else if (plane->dist >= absmaxs[plane->type]) { side = 2; } else { side = 3; } } else { side = AAS_BoxOnPlaneSide(absmins, absmaxs, plane); } Notes on scope: - Do not change AAS_BoxOnPlaneSide itself. It is the faithful sub_1001c2e0 port and is also used by AAS_LinkEntityToBSPLeaves (aas_map.c:9888), whose retail counterpart is a different routine that must be audited on its own before its call site gets the same treatment. - Use the plain signed `plane->type < 3` and do not add a `plane->type >= 0` guard: retail's test is `edx_10 s>= 3`, so a negative type takes the axial branch in retail too, and adding a guard would be an invented divergence. - Add a parity test that builds a two-node AAS tree with an axial plane whose dist equals the box's absmaxs on that axis and asserts only the back child's areas are returned.

### MEDIUM AAS area/BSP-leaf link lists are built in the reverse of retail's traversal order

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_BestReachableArea / AAS_BestReachableLinkArea` @ `0x1000b300`
- HLIL: 10857-10859 (AAS_BestReachableArea fallback); 10657-10687 (AAS_BestReachableLinkArea walks head-first); 23899-23905 (sub_1001c460 push order: children[0] at node+4 pushed BEFORE children[1] at node+8); 23843-23849 (each visited leaf is PREPENDED); 7168-7174 (same push order in sub_10006210)
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2310`

**Divergence.** AAS_CollectBBoxAreasFromTree (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2303-2320) pushes node->children[1] before node->children[0], the mirror of retail's sub_1001c460 (HLIL 23899-23905: `if ((eax_5 & 1) != 0) *ebx = *(esi_5 + 4)` pushed BEFORE `if ((eax_5 & 2) != 0) *ebx = *(esi_5 + 8)`; corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_sample.c:916-919). Because both are LIFO stacks, our leaf visit sequence is the exact reverse of retail's. The net effect is NOT that our order is reversed everywhere -- it is that AAS_BBoxAreas' returned array already equals retail's *link-list* order (retail prepends each visited leaf at HLIL 23846/23853, so its list is the reverse of its visit order). The bug is therefore in the two consumers that reverse the array a second time: 1. LIVE DIVERGENCE -- AAS_BestReachableArea (retail 0x1000b300, HLIL 10857-10859; ref be_aas_entity.c:398-401). src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:1197 and :1207 scan areas[] backwards "to emulate the prepend", but our traversal is already reversed, so the double reversal cancels. Concretely, for an item whose link box straddles two grounded areas 57 (children[0]) and 91 (children[1]): retail's DFS visits [91,57], prepends to list [57,91], and AAS_BestReachableLinkArea (sub_1000b130, HLIL 10657-10687) returns the head, 57. Our traversal yields areas[] = {57,91}, the backwards scan takes index 1 and returns 91. The item's goal areanum, its travel time, and which bot routes to it all differ. 2. PARITY-ONLY (not observable today) -- src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:9990-10002 iterates areas[] forward while AAS_LinkEntityToArea (9688-9694) prepends, so entity->areas ends up as the reverse of retail's chain. The only order-sensitive reader, AAS_BestReachableEntityArea (0x1000b1b0), is dead in retail (ref/be_aas_entity.c:572-574) and unreferenced in our tree. 3. PARITY-ONLY -- the same push inversion in AAS_LinkEntityToBSPLeaves (aas_map.c:9891-9908) against sub_10006210 (HLIL 7168-7174) has no live observable consequence: its only order-sensitive retail consumer, sub_100063D0 / AAS_EntitiesInBox, is dead (ref/be_aas_bspq2.c:1920), and per-leaf entity lists are ordered by entity-link chronology, not by DFS order. 4. Contrary to the claim's implication, src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:3446-3447 (teleporter reachability) iterates areas[] FORWARD and therefore currently MATCHES retail's head-first link walk at ref/be_aas_reach.c:2106. Any fix must not regress it.

**Failure scenario.** An item (e.g. a health pack on the seam between two grounded areas) whose link box overlaps grounded areas 57 and 91, visited by retail's DFS as V = [57, 91]. Retail's list head is 91, so AAS_BestReachableLinkArea returns 91 and BotChooseLTGItem routes to area 91. Our traversal yields W = [91, 57], the prepend leaves head 57, and we return 57. The item's goal areanum, its travel time, and therefore which bot picks it up and by which route all differ. Same for AAS_BestReachableEntityArea and for any AAS_BestReachableArea call whose origin falls outside every area.

**Fix.** Either of two self-consistent fixes; pick one and apply it to all affected sites in the same change. Preferred (faithful traversal). In AAS_CollectBBoxAreasFromTree (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2303-2320) move the `(side & 1)` push of node->children[0] ABOVE the `(side & 2)` push of node->children[1], so the visit order matches retail's sub_1001c460. Then: - src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:1197 and :1207 (backwards scans) become correct as written -- leave them, but update the 1191-1196 comment, which currently misstates why the backwards scan is there. - src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:9990 (forward iteration + prepend in AAS_LinkEntityToArea) becomes correct -- leave it. - MUST also flip src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:3447 to iterate `for (int index = numareas - 1; index >= 0; --index)`, otherwise the teleporter-reachability loop stops matching retail's head-first walk at ref/be_aas_reach.c:2106. - Optionally apply the same push swap in AAS_LinkEntityToBSPLeaves (aas_map.c:9891-9908) for parity with sub_10006210; no live behaviour depends on it. Alternative (minimal diff, treat AAS_BBoxAreas as returning retail's link order). Leave the traversal alone and instead: - change aas_reach.c:1197 and :1207 to scan FORWARD (`for (int index = 0; index < numareas; ++index)`), and - change aas_map.c:9990 to iterate backwards (`for (int index = occupied - 1; index >= 0; --index)`) so the prepend chain in AAS_LinkEntityToArea reproduces retail's, and - leave aas_reach.c:3447 forward as-is. Whichever route is taken, note that AAS_AddUniqueArea's dedup (aas_map.c:2164-2170) has no retail counterpart -- retail creates one link per leaf visit -- so if the AAS node tree can reference the same area from two leaves the resulting counts still differ; that is a separate item.

### MEDIUM AAS_AreaEntityCollision passes the wrong contents mask to AAS_EntityCollision

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_AreaEntityCollision` @ `0x1001b130`
- HLIL: 22748-22749
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2437`

**Divergence.** AAS_AreaEntityCollision (retail 0x1001b130, HLIL 22731-22769) passes contentmask = 0x02010003 (MASK_PLAYERSOLID: CONTENTS_SOLID|CONTENTS_WINDOW|CONTENTS_PLAYERCLIP|CONTENTS_MONSTER) to AAS_EntityCollision. The deciding HLIL line is 22748-22749 at 1001b1a8, where Binary Ninja renders the integer immediate as the float 9.47742657e-38f (bit pattern 0x02010003 = 33619971); BN types the same constant as an int at HLIL 38611 and 39199, and ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_sample.c:287 spells it 33619971. Note that BN over-detects the call arity (10 operands); the real 7 arguments are the trailing ones (entnum, start, &boxmins, &boxmaxs, end, mask, &bsptrace), so the immediate does sit in the contentmask slot. src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2437 instead passes CONTENTS_SOLID | CONTENTS_PLAYERCLIP (0x00010001). No caller or callee compensates: AAS_TraceClientBBox (aas_map.c:2545) adds no mask, AAS_EntityCollision (aas_map.c:1987) forwards contentmask verbatim into AAS_TraceBSPModel on the SOLID_BSP path, and AAS_TraceThroughBSPBrush (aas_map.c:1305 — the claim called this "AAS_TraceBSPModelBrush") rejects every brush failing (brush->contents & contentmask) != 0. Brush contents come straight from the BSP lump (aas_map.c:4862), so CONTENTS_WINDOW survives to the filter. The observable loss is CONTENTS_WINDOW only. Q2 qbsp gives translucent brushes CONTENTS_WINDOW instead of CONTENTS_SOLID, so a SOLID_BSP entity built from glass brushes (breakable window, translucent func_door/func_wall) linked into a traced area is entirely skipped by our filter: AAS_TraceClientBBox returns fraction 1.0 and the bot predicts its move straight through the glass, where retail stops at it. The CONTENTS_MONSTER bit is not independently observable — Q2 never compiles it onto BSP brushes (src/shared/q_shared.h:101: "should never be on a brush, only in game") — but it belongs in the constant for exactness. Corroborating context: src/shared/q_shared.h:110 already defines MASK_PLAYERSOLID as exactly 0x02010003, and the two sibling sites matching the reference's other 33619971 calls (be_ai_move.c:230, 669) already use MASK_PLAYERSOLID at src/botlib/ai_move/bot_move.c:241 and :787, making line 2437 an isolated outlier.

**Failure scenario.** A SOLID_BSP entity built from CONTENTS_WINDOW brushes (translucent glass door / breakable window bmodel, common in Q2 maps) is linked into the area a bot is tracing through. Retail's AAS_EntityCollision -> AAS_TraceBSPModel keeps the brush because 2 is in the mask, so AAS_TraceClientBBox stops at the glass. Ours ANDs 2 against 0x10001, gets 0, skips every brush of the model, and reports fraction 1.0 - the bot walks its move prediction straight through the glass door. Same for any bmodel whose brushes carry CONTENTS_MONSTER.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c, in AAS_AreaEntityCollision, change the sixth argument of the AAS_EntityCollision call at line 2437 from CONTENTS_SOLID | CONTENTS_PLAYERCLIP, to MASK_PLAYERSOLID, which src/shared/q_shared.h:110 already defines as CONTENTS_SOLID|CONTENTS_PLAYERCLIP|CONTENTS_WINDOW|CONTENTS_MONSTER = 0x02010003, matching the immediate at 1001b1a8 and the reference's literal 33619971. Using the MASK_PLAYERSOLID spelling keeps this site consistent with src/botlib/ai_move/bot_move.c:241 and :787, which already use it for the same retail constant. Spelling out CONTENTS_SOLID | CONTENTS_WINDOW | CONTENTS_PLAYERCLIP | CONTENTS_MONSTER is behaviourally identical if the explicit form is preferred. No other change is needed — the mask flows unmodified through AAS_EntityCollision into AAS_TraceBSPModel and the AAS_TraceThroughBSPBrush contents test.

### MEDIUM PVS leaf lookup sends points exactly on a splitting plane to the wrong child

- status: **open**
- retail: `CM_PointLeafnum` @ `0x10003360`
- HLIL: 4805-4814 (100033c4..100033d6)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_bspq2.c:241-245
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:3367`

**Divergence.** Retail CM_PointLeafnum @ 0x10003360 (HLIL 4784-4814) descends the Quake II BSP with `d = DotProduct(point, plane->normal) - plane->dist`, then at 100033cf branches on `(AH & 0x41) != 0` — AH bit 0 is x87 C0 (`d < 0`) and AH bit 6 is C3 (`d == 0`), so the test is `d <= 0` — taking `children[1]` (offset +8 in the 28-byte node) and otherwise `children[0]` (+4). Points exactly on a splitting plane therefore descend into the BACK child; NaN (unordered, C0|C2|C3 set) also goes back. Corroborated verbatim by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_bspq2.c:243-246. Retail AAS_InPVS @ 0x10005B30 (HLIL 6777-6850) resolves both of its points through this routine, calling `sub_10003420(point, 0)` at 10005b92 and 10005bd7, which is `&dleafs[CM_PointLeafnum(point, 0)]` (HLIL 4832-4836). Our AAS_BSPPointLeafNumber (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:3345, sole caller AAS_InPVS at :3414, which is itself reached from the enemy-sighting gate AAS_EntityVisible at :3569) instead does `nodenum = node->children[distance < 0.0f ? 1 : 0];` at line 3367, sending `d == 0` (and NaN) to the FRONT child. Our other leaf walker, AAS_BSPModelPointLeafNum at aas_map.c:1767, already matches retail with `node->children[(distance > 0.0f) ? 0 : 1]` and its header comment even names the rule "front-on-positive, back-on-zero", so the two walkers in our tree disagree with each other. Nothing upstream compensates: neither AAS_BSPPointLeafNumber nor AAS_InPVS applies an epsilon, snap, or re-test to the point. For a point coincident with a node split plane (common with Q2's integer plane distances and grid-aligned entity/sample origins) retail reads the cluster of the back leaf and we read the front leaf's cluster; when the two leaves belong to different clusters AAS_InPVS returns a different answer and the bot's target-visibility decision flips. Not part of this finding: our walker starting at a hardcoded `nodenum = 0` instead of `bspModels[0].headnode` is unobservable, since the Q2 world model's headnode is 0.

**Failure scenario.** A bot eye position or target sample lying exactly on an axial BSP split plane — common with Q2's integer plane distances and item/spawn origins on 8- or 16-unit grids, e.g. a point at z=0 against a floor plane {normal=(0,0,1), dist=0}. Retail resolves to the leaf below the plane and reads that leaf's cluster; we resolve to the leaf above. If the two leaves have different clusters, AAS_InPVS returns a different visibility answer and the bot's target-visibility / enemy-sighting decision flips.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c, change line 3367 from nodenum = node->children[distance < 0.0f ? 1 : 0]; to nodenum = node->children[(distance > 0.0f) ? 0 : 1]; This reproduces retail's `(AH & 0x41) != 0 => children[1]` rule (`d <= 0`, and NaN, take the back child), and makes AAS_BSPPointLeafNumber agree with the already-correct AAS_BSPModelPointLeafNum at aas_map.c:1767. No other change is needed; the surrounding bounds checks and the AAS_InPVS caller are unaffected.

### MEDIUM The soundindex->soundinfo table is rebuilt on index-refresh calls and before the AAS load succeeds

- status: **open**
- retail: `sub_1001D140 (AAS_InitSoundIndexTable)` @ `0x1001d140`
- HLIL: 24437-24486
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_sound.c:470-494
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:8316`

**Divergence.** Retail builds the soundindex->soundinfo table (`data_100669c0` / count `data_100669bc`) from exactly one site: `1000ed73 j_sub_1001d140()` in `sub_1000ecd0` (AAS_LoadMap), reached only after `1000ed45 *sub_10044f86() = j_sub_1000e880(arg1)` returned 0. The `mapname == NULL` refresh branch returns at `1000ed02` having run only `j_sub_1000dcc0`, whose fill-only helper `sub_1000db40` (HLIL 12222-12265) populates previously-NULL name slots in place and touches neither `numindexes` nor `data_100669c0`/`data_100669bc`. We call the equivalent `AAS_SoundSubsystem_RegisterMapAssets` twice in `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c`: at line 8316 in the refresh branch (retail never does this) and at line 8343 on the load path, before the BSP/AAS files are located and regardless of whether the load later fails (retail does it last, after reachability init). No later site rebuilds it. Primary observable divergence — the refresh branch. The game calls `BotLib_BotLoadMap(NULL)` from `Bot_soundindex` (`ref/gladiator-bot-restored/game/bl_redirgi.c:541`) whenever a sound is precached after the library is loaded, always with the constant `MAX_SOUNDINDEXES` (`bl_main.c:344`). Retail leaves that new slot NULL in `data_100669c0`, so `sub_1001ce20` passes the `1001ce37` range check, then hits `1001ce6e if (ebx_1 == 0) return 0` and queues no sound event — bots never hear sounds precached after the map load. We rebuild the table, so the name resolves against a sounds.c `soundinfo` record and the sound starts producing events, giving bots sensory input retail's bots do not have. Secondary, weaker divergence — the load-failure path: retail keeps the previous map's mapping while we have already replaced it. This is real but lands in a degenerate state, since `BotLib_BotLoadMap` destroys every bot using the library on a non-zero return (`bl_main.c:346-362`). Note that the ordering change on the *successful* load path is by itself unobservable: nothing between aas_map.c:8343 and the function's end at 9230 reads or clears the table, and no sound events are queued during the load.

**Failure scenario.** The engine calls AAS_LoadMap(NULL, ...) to refresh configstrings mid-level after new sounds were precached. Retail leaves data_100669C0/data_100669BC untouched, so the new indexes stay unmapped and BotUpdateSound silently ignores them; we free and rebuild the table so those sounds now resolve to soundinfo records and start generating sound events. Symmetrically, when a map's .aas fails to load, retail keeps the previous map's mapping while we have already replaced it.

**Fix.** In `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c`: 1. Delete the `AAS_SoundSubsystem_RegisterMapAssets` call and its warning block at lines 8316-8320, so the `mapname == NULL` branch performs only `AAS_RefreshIndexTables` and returns `BLERR_NOERROR` — matching `1000ecf7 j_sub_1000dcc0(...)` / `1000ed02 return 0`. 2. Move the call at line 8343 to the very end of the successful-load path, immediately before `AAS_LoadMap` returns `BLERR_NOERROR`, so it runs after the BSP and AAS lumps are in and after the link-heap / linked-entity / reachability init that retail performs at `1001ac00`, `1001ade0`, `10018c70` — i.e. it must never run on any path that returns an error. Since retail's `sub_1001d140` has no failure return (its `GetMemory` cannot return NULL), the relocated call should not abort an otherwise-successful load: downgrade the current `AAS_ReturnMapLoadFailure(BLERR_INVALIDIMPORT)` on that call to a `PRT_WARNING` print, or drop the check, rather than turning a completed load into an error retail never emits. Keep passing `soundindexes`/`soundindex`; retail reads `aasworld.soundindex_table` (already replaced by `sub_1000dc20` earlier in the same call), which holds the same data.

### MEDIUM BotLibLoadMap is missing retail's autolaunchbspc / WinBSPC spawn branch

- status: **open**
- retail: `BotLibLoadMap (+ sub_1000E140)` @ `0x1000e880 (branch at 0x1000eb6e-0x1000eba3); sub_1000E140 @ 0x1000e140`
- HLIL: 12991-13006 (BotLibLoadMap give-up path), 12495-12641 (sub_1000E140)
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:8534`

**Divergence.** BotLibLoadMap (sub_1000e880 @0x1000e880) is missing retail's autolaunchbspc branch, and sub_1000E140 (@0x1000e140, the WinBSPC spawner) is not implemented anywhere in our tree. Retail, HLIL 12991-13006: once both loose ".aas" probes fail, BotLibLoadMap calls the aasN.zip helper `j_sub_1000e430(arg1)` (12991) and returns 0 only on success (12993-12994). On ANY non-zero return — the "not found" `return 5` at 12849 and the inner AAS-load error `return *sub_10044f86()` at 12829 alike — it falls through to 12996 `st0 = j_sub_10038a90("autolaunchbspc", &data_1005b570)`. The packed x87 status at 13000-13001 is masked `:1.b & 0x40`, which is the C3/equal bit; `== 0` therefore means "libvar != 0", so the branch is taken when autolaunchbspc is non-zero. It then calls `j_sub_1000e140(arg1)` (13002) and prints `data_10063fe8(5, "\ncreating AAS for %s...\n\nThis may take several minutes\n...", arg1, arg1, arg1)` at level 5 = PRT_EXIT with the map name three times (13003). Only afterwards does 13005 print `no AAS file available\n` at level 4 = PRT_FATAL and 13006 `return 5`. sub_1000E140 (HLIL 12495-12641): builds `<map>.bsp`; builds `<basedir>/<gamedir>/` via LibVarGetString + AppendPathSeperator(sub_10041900); saves that prefix; appends "maps" and probes `_access(path, 4)` (sub_10052e5c, 12565) — on success appends a separator, on failure writes 0 at `strlen-4` to strip the "maps" it just added (12589); appends `<map>` and ".aas"; appends "winbspc.exe" to the saved prefix (12626); `Log_Write("spawning \"%s\"", exePath)` (12627-12628); `sprintf(buf, "bsp2aas(%s,%s);", bspName, aasPath)` (12632); `_spawnl(_P_NOWAIT /*1*/, exePath, exePath, buf, NULL)` (12636); on a negative return prints `can't execute WinBSPC\n` at level 3 = PRT_ERROR (12641). Ours: src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:8528-8536 goes straight from `!aasDiscovered` to `BotLib_Print(PRT_FATAL, "no AAS file available\n")` and BLERR_NOAASFILE. The same branch is missing from AAS_ReturnAASLoadFailure (aas_map.c:5884-5893), which retail's control flow also funnels through 12996. `grep -rn "autolaunchbspc|winbspc|WinBSPC|creating AAS|bsp2aas|SpawnProcess|spawning" src/` returns nothing, so it is not implemented in a caller, wrapper or bridge either. CORRECTION to the original claim: AAS_ReturnAASLoadFailure is NOT wrong about the error code. It already reproduces retail's behaviour of discarding the zip helper's specific error, printing "no AAS file available" and returning BLERR_NOAASFILE. The only thing missing from it is the autolaunchbspc branch that must run before that print. Corroboration: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_main.c:366-395 (sub_1000E140) and :536-558 (the branch). The branch is reachable in practice — ref/gladiator-bot-restored/game/bl_main.c:920-922 maps the Q2 cvar `autolaunchbspc` onto `BotLibVarSet("autolaunchbspc", "1")`.

**Failure scenario.** Server has `autolaunchbspc 1` (retail default string is "0", so the operator sets it) and loads a map with a .bsp but no .aas and no aasN.zip copy. Retail: logs `spawning "...winbspc.exe"`, launches WinBSPC to build maps/<map>.aas in the background, prints the 'creating AAS for <map>...' block at PRT_EXIT, then 'no AAS file available'. Ours: prints only 'no AAS file available' and never launches the compiler, so the AAS file is never generated and the operator gets no explanation.

**Fix.** Add a static AAS_SpawnBSPC(const char *mapname) to src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c reproducing sub_1000E140, and call it from the two give-up sites. 1. AAS_SpawnBSPC (retail sub_1000E140 @0x1000e140, HLIL 12495-12641), with the repo's commented header block and tabs: - char bspName[144]; strncpy from mapname (0x90), strncat ".bsp". - char aasPath[144]; strncpy LibVarGetString("basedir") (0x90); AppendPathSeperator; strncat LibVarGetString("gamedir"); AppendPathSeperator. - char exePath[144]; strncpy aasPath (0x90) -- snapshot BEFORE "maps" is appended. - strncat aasPath "maps"; if (_access(aasPath, 4) != 0) aasPath[strlen(aasPath) - 4] = '\0'; else AppendPathSeperator(aasPath, ...). (HLIL 12565: sub_10052e5c(&var_240, 4) == 0 takes the append-separator branch; the else at 12589 does the -4 truncation.) - strncat aasPath mapname; strncat aasPath ".aas". - strncat exePath "winbspc.exe". - Log_Write("spawning \"%s\"", exePath); /* Log_Write appends "\r\n" itself */ - sprintf(args, "bsp2aas(%s,%s);", bspName, aasPath); - int result = _spawnl(_P_NOWAIT, exePath, exePath, args, NULL); - if (result < 0) return BotLib_Print(PRT_ERROR, "can't execute WinBSPC\n"); return result; Every strncat uses retail's `144 - strlen(dst)` bound, not sizeof. 2. Factor the branch into a helper (retail runs it at exactly one site, 0x1000eb6e, so one helper called from both of ours keeps the ordering identical) and invoke it immediately BEFORE each existing `BotLib_Print(PRT_FATAL, "no AAS file available\n")`: - aas_map.c:8534 (the !aasDiscovered give-up path) - aas_map.c:5892 (inside AAS_ReturnAASLoadFailure's zipped branch) Body: if (LibVarValue("autolaunchbspc", "0") != 0.0f) { AAS_SpawnBSPC(mapname); BotLib_Print(PRT_EXIT, "\ncreating AAS for %s...\n\nThis may take several minutes\n\n" "You cannot play the map %s with\nbots before AAS (%s.aas) has been\n" "created.\n\nYou probably want to close Quake2 now\n" "to free up processing power for the\ntool which creates the AAS file.\n\n", mapname, mapname, mapname); } PRT_EXIT is 5 (src/botlib/common/l_log.h:11) — do not use PRT_FATAL here; the PRT_FATAL "no AAS file available" print and the BLERR_NOAASFILE return stay exactly where they are, after the branch. AAS_ReturnAASLoadFailure currently has no mapname in scope; thread it through (it is available in AAS_LoadMap) rather than reaching for aasworld state. 3. Gate the _spawnl/_access call on _WIN32 (they are MSVC CRT calls; the Linux gladi386.so has no sub_1000E140 at all). Do NOT leave the non-Windows path silently empty if you also gate the print — the reference's Linux path prints "the BSPC tool is a Win32 program\n" at PRT_MESSAGE under the same libvar test. Since the DLL is our target, the simplest faithful arrangement is: keep the libvar test and the PRT_EXIT notice unconditional (matching the DLL), and gate only the AAS_SpawnBSPC body's spawn on _WIN32. Leave AAS_ReturnAASLoadFailure's existing "swallow the error code, return BLERR_NOAASFILE" logic alone — that part already matches retail (HLIL 12993 falls through to 13005-13006 regardless of which non-zero code the zip helper returned).

### LOW SOLID_BSP hit overwrites trace->ent with the entity number; retail copies the model trace verbatim

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_EntityCollision` @ `0x10003680`
- HLIL: 5192-5217 (10003a42..10003aad)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_bspq2.c:513-521
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2024`

**Divergence.** AAS_EntityCollision @ 0x10003680: the SOLID_BSP (solid==3) arm at HLIL 10003a42-10003aad copies the AAS_TraceBSPModel result into the caller's trace with a bare `__builtin_memcpy(dest: arg10, src: &var_b4, n: 0x54)` and performs no `arg10[0x14] = arg4` (trace->ent = entnum) fixup, unlike the two SOLID_BBOX arms at 10003805 and 1000397c which do. Because AAS_TraceBSPModel zeroes its entire 0x54-byte result at 10004518 and never writes +0x50, retail's trace->ent is 0 after a SOLID_BSP hit (and any nonzero ent left by an earlier SOLID_BBOX entity in the same area loop is wiped). Our src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2024 writes `modeltrace.ent = entnum;` first, so we emit the mover's entity number where retail emits 0. Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_bspq2.c:513-521. The claim's failure scenario is incorrect and must not be repeated: bot_move.c:242 (BotOnMover) and bot_interface.c:343 use Q2_Trace, the engine trace, matching retail's AAS_Trace @ 0x10003010 which is a pure forward to botimport.Trace (`1000304e memcpy(arg1, data_10063fec(...), 0x54)`) and ref be_ai_move.c:230/237. Neither call site can ever see an AAS_EntityCollision-produced ent, so no mover-riding or visibility branch changes. The actual reach of the divergent value is AAS_EntityCollision -> AAS_AreaEntityCollision (retail 0x1001b130, `1001b1e9 arg6[5] = var_4`; ref be_aas_sample.c:296; ours aas_map.c:2450) -> aas_trace_t.ent returned by AAS_TraceClientBBox -> move->trace.ent (ours aas_move.c:324). No code in retail botlib or in our tree reads that field, so this is an incorrect value in a returned output struct rather than an observable control-flow change.

**Failure scenario.** A bot's client-bbox trace hits a func_door. Retail: AAS_TraceClientBBox reports `trace.ent == 0`, so bot_move.c:242 `trace.ent != 0 && AAS_EntityModelNum(trace.ent) == reach->facenum` is false and bot_interface.c:343 `trace.ent == target` is false. Ours: `trace.ent` is the door's entity number, so both tests can now succeed and the mover-riding / visibility logic takes a branch retail never takes.

**Fix.** Delete the `modeltrace.ent = entnum;` line at src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2024 so the SOLID_BSP arm copies the model trace unchanged (`*trace = modeltrace;`), matching retail's raw 0x54-byte memcpy at 10003aad and leaving trace->ent as the zero AAS_TraceBSPModel memset it to. No other change is needed — nothing else in the tree compensates for or depends on the assignment. Note in the commit message that this only corrects the value of aas_trace_t.ent / move->trace.ent, which currently has no reader, so no test should be expected to change behaviour.

### LOW AAS_TraceClientBBox startsolid path clears the trace direction, suppressing retail's plane-flip

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_TraceClientBBox` @ `0x1001b260`
- HLIL: 23206-23265 (and the solid-leaf twin at 22876-22936)
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2110`

**Divergence.** AAS_TraceClientBBox @ 0x1001b260: retail's two startsolid arms (blocked area, HLIL 23235-23236 at 1001b5d2/1001b5da; solid leaf, HLIL 22905-22906 at 1001b765/1001b76d) write only trace.startsolid = 1 and trace.fraction = 0, never the trace-direction vector v1. Both fall into a shared tail (1001b667 / 1001b7fa) that computes DotProduct(v1, aasworld.planes[trace.planenum].normal) and, when it is > 0, sets trace.planenum ^= 1 (1001b84c, HLIL 23320; the guard `(ah & 0x41) != 0` is dot <= 0 -> keep). Since v1 is a function-scoped local written only on the non-startsolid arms, and neither the traversal branch nor the tracestack ever touches its slots, retail dots the plane normal against uninitialized stack residue on the startsolid path, so trace.planenum may come back flipped. Confirmed by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_sample.c:377-399 and :429-451 (with the authors' note at :324-327) and by Q3 having added VectorClear(v1) at these exact sites (dev_tools/Quake-III-Arena-master/code/botlib/be_aas_sample.c:520, :573). Our AAS_SetTraceBlocked does VectorClear(direction) at src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2110, so AAS_TraceFacingPlanenum's DotProduct(direction, plane->normal) <= 0.0f test at :2074 always holds and the XOR can never fire. Two corrections to the original statement: (a) trace.planenum on a startsolid return is always 0, because the tracestack entry whose start is still the original start inherits planenum unbroken from the root (init 0; near part keeps tmpplanenum, mirrored at aas_map.c:2656) - so the observable difference is aasworld.planes[0] vs planes[1], not the plane actually hit; (b) the only consumer that reads planenum off a startsolid trace is AAS_ClientMovementPrediction (our aas_move.c:801, ref be_aas_move.c:502) - AAS_OnGround (aas_move.c:415) bails on startsolid at :406 and the steptrace read at :823 is behind if (!steptrace.startsolid) at :821. Within that one consumer the slide VectorMA(v, -DotProduct(v,n), n, v) and the plane->normal[2] == 0.0f test are both invariant under normal negation; only the landed/onground test DotProduct(plane->normal, up) > phys_maxsteepness and the VectorMA(org, -0.25f, plane->normal, start) at aas_move.c:816 actually diverge.

**Failure scenario.** A bot's move prediction starts inside an area whose presencetype rejects PRESENCE_NORMAL (crouch-only duct) or inside a solid leaf. Retail returns trace.startsolid = 1, fraction = 0 and trace.planenum = tstack_p->planenum ^ 1 whenever the leftover v1 from the previous trace happens to face the hit plane; our build always returns the unflipped planenum. Consumers that resolve the result plane - aas_move.c:415 and :801/:823 do `AAS_PlaneFromNum(trace.planenum)` - then get the opposite normal (the AAS plane pair differs only in the low bit), so slide/step-off decisions off a startsolid trace differ from retail.

**Fix.** Do not adopt the proposed fix. Hoisting `direction` to AAS_TraceClientBBox scope and leaving it unwritten on the startsolid arm is undefined behaviour in C and still cannot reproduce retail's stack residue - and because our code already computes VectorSubtract/AAS_NormalizeVector unconditionally at aas_map.c:2102-2103, merely deleting the VectorClear would produce a third behaviour (always flip when normalize(end-start) faces planes[0].normal), matching neither retail nor the current build. Retail is nondeterministic here, so no faithful deterministic reconstruction exists. The correct action is to keep the VectorClear and document it: add a comment at src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2110 recording that retail 0x1001b260 leaves v1 stale on both startsolid arms (HLIL 23235-23236 and 22905-22906, shared tail XOR at 1001b84c), that Q3 fixed this with VectorClear(v1) (be_aas_sample.c:520, :573), that trace.planenum on this path is always 0 so retail's flip only ever selects aasworld.planes[1] over planes[0], and that we deliberately pick the unflipped (dot == 0) branch as the deterministic representative. Optionally note the single affected consumer, the landed/onground test in AAS_ClientMovementPrediction reached via aas_move.c:801.

### LOW Vis-decompression "0 repeat" diagnostic uses the wrong print level and adds a newline retail does not have

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_DecompressVis` @ `0x10005a60`
- HLIL: 6756-6757 (10005ab2..10005af0); AAS_Error at 12072-12076 (1000d7e0..1000d810)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_bspq2.c:1574-1578
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:3472`

**Divergence.** Retail routes the 0-repeat vis-decompression diagnostic through AAS_Error (`j_sub_1000d7e0("AAS_DecompressVis: 0 repeat")`, HLIL 6757), which vsprintf's and calls `botimport.Print(4, ...)` — PRT_FATAL — with a .rdata literal that carries no trailing newline (`char data_1005aac0[0x1c] = "AAS_DecompressVis: 0 repeat", 0`, HLIL 72927). Our inlined decompressor in AAS_InPVS at src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:3472 emits `BotLib_Print(PRT_ERROR, "AAS_DecompressVis: 0 repeat\n")` — level 3 instead of 4, plus an extra '\n'. Observable consequence with the shipped Q2 game.dll: its `BotLibImport_Print` prefixes the message "Error: " instead of "Fatal: " and our extra newline breaks the line where retail leaves the cursor mid-line. It does NOT change fatality — `gi.error` is commented out under `case PRT_FATAL` in dev_tools/game_source/bl_main.c:774, so PRT_FATAL and PRT_ERROR both just `gi.dprintf`; only PRT_EXIT aborts. The site is also inconsistent with our own AAS_Error convention already applied in src/botlib/aas/aas_cluster.c (lines 60, 91, 128, 327, 375, 563), which uses PRT_FATAL with no trailing newline and documents exactly this mapping.

**Failure scenario.** Loading a map whose vis lump contains a zero run-length byte (corrupt or hand-edited .bsp). Retail's host sees a PRT_FATAL message with no newline (the Q2 game.dll's BotImport_Print switches on level, so PRT_FATAL vs PRT_ERROR changes whether the host treats it as fatal and how it is coloured/logged). We report it at PRT_ERROR, so a host that only escalates on PRT_FATAL will keep running where retail would not.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:3472 change the call to `BotLib_Print(PRT_FATAL, "AAS_DecompressVis: 0 repeat");` — level 4 and no trailing newline — and add the same short comment used at src/botlib/aas/aas_cluster.c:58-59 citing AAS_Error (sub_1000d7e0 @0x1000d810) and the newline-free literal at 0x1005aac0. The existing test locks in the wrong values and must be updated with it: tests/aas/test_aas_map.c:821 `assert_int_equal(g_test_print_priority, PRT_ERROR)` -> `PRT_FATAL`, and lines 822-823 `assert_string_equal(g_test_print_message, "AAS_DecompressVis: 0 repeat\n")` -> `"AAS_DecompressVis: 0 repeat"`.

### LOW Surface-extent accumulator seeds ±FLT_MAX; retail seeds ±99999.0f

- status: **open**
- retail: `CalcSurfaceExtents` @ `0x100071e0`
- HLIL: 8061-8065 (10007248..10007265)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_bspq2.c:2344-2346, 2386-2391
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:6966`

**Divergence.** CalcSurfaceExtents @ 0x100071e0 (HLIL 8034-8159): retail seeds mins[0..1] = 99999.0f (0x47c34f80 at 10007248/10007250) and maxs[0..1] = -99999.0f (0xc7c34f80 at 10007258/10007265), where src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:6966-6967 seeds ±FLT_MAX — but that constant is unobservable in isolation, because the seed only survives when the whole face lies beyond ±99999 on an axis (or numedges == 0), and in every one of those cases our own extent-range guard at aas_map.c:7018-7025 already aborts. The real divergence is that AAS_BuildBSPPointLightExtents has three invented failure paths retail does not have: `face->numedges <= 0` (line 6960), the SHRT_MIN/SHRT_MAX extent-range abort (lines 7018-7025), and the model/node/edge/face validation prologue (lines 6882-6963).
