Retail validates nothing: 10007287 `if (eax_7 s> 0)` simply skips the vertex loop for a zero-edge face, leaving the seeds, and 1000737f/10007394 truncate `bmins*16` and `(bmaxs - bmins)*16` to int16 — so a zero-edge face emits texturemins = (short)(6249*16) = -31088 and extents = (short)((-6249 - 6249)*16) = -3360 and the load continues. Ours returns BLERR_CANNOTREADBSPLUMP, which AAS_LoadMap (aas_map.c:9089-9113) turns into a full map-load failure. Reachable only via a malformed BSP, since any map the Q2 engine can run has well-formed faces with lightmap extents well inside int16.

**Failure scenario.** A face with a large texture scale on a far-out brush, so that `DotProduct(vertex, texinfo->vecs[0]) + vecs[0][3] > 99999`: retail records texturemins from the 99999 seed and a correspondingly wrong extent, so RecursiveLightPoint's `ds/dt` window test at 10006ef7..10006f15 rejects (or mis-samples) that face and AAS_BSPTracePointLight returns a different lightspot/colour than ours, which computes the true extent. Our version additionally aborts the whole load via AAS_BSPPointLightDataFailure on a zero-edge face where retail silently produces garbage extents.

**Fix.** Make AAS_BuildBSPPointLightExtents as failure-free on face geometry as retail is; the seed alone is not enough. (a) Seed `float mins[2] = {99999.0f, 99999.0f}` / `float maxs[2] = {-99999.0f, -99999.0f}`. (b) Drop the `face->numedges <= 0` rejection at aas_map.c:6960 and let a zero-edge face fall through to the floor/ceil step with the seeds intact. (c) Replace the extent-range abort at aas_map.c:7018-7025 with retail's truncation, doing the subtraction in 16-bit as 10007384-10007394 does: `short bmin = (short)(int)floor(mins[axis] * 0.0625f); short bmax = (short)(int)ceil(maxs[axis] * 0.0625f); texturemins[axis] = (short)(bmin * 16); extents[axis] = (short)((short)(bmax - bmin) * 16);`. Keep only the guards that prevent genuine out-of-bounds reads (surfedge/edge/vertex indices); every guard that can abort the load on values retail merely truncates should go, and the downstream lightmap-dimension checks at aas_map.c:7041-7057 need the same treatment since negative extents from a zero-edge face will now reach them.

### LOW AAS data teardown frees aasworld.nodes, which retail's AAS_DumpAASData never touches

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_DumpAASData` @ `0x1000c490`
- HLIL: 11382-11471 (note 11446-11454: after zeroing reachability it jumps straight to portals -- data_10066960 (numnodes) and data_10066964 (nodes) never appear)
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:8219`

**Divergence.** Retail's AAS_DumpAASData (0x1000c490, HLIL 11382-11471) frees and nulls 12 of the 14 AAS lump pointers, omitting TWO - not one. Neither the bbox lump (data_10066914 pointer / data_10066910 count, lump 0, elem size 0x20; loaded at HLIL 11554-11557, swapped at 11102-11129) nor the node lump (data_10066964 / data_10066960, lump 10, elem size 0xc; loaded at 11674-11677, swapped at 11318-11323) is touched: the chain runs `eax_7 = data_1006695c (reachability)` / `data_10066954 = 0` / `data_10066958 = 0` / free, then straight to `eax_8 = data_1006696c (portals)` (HLIL 11439-11450), and starts at data_1006691c (vertexes) rather than data_10066914. A whole-file grep confirms neither pointer is freed anywhere in the DLL; AAS_Shutdown (sub_1000ee30) memsets them to zero at 13071 without freeing, so retail leaks numbboxes*32 + numnodes*12 bytes and 2 tracked blocks on every AAS load. The reference reconstruction (ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_file.c:174-227) reproduces both omissions. Our AAS_ClearAASData - called where retail calls AAS_DumpAASData (aas_map.c:8537 vs retail 0x1000c737, plus failure paths 9214/9221) - frees bboxes at aas_map.c:8171-8175 and nodes at 8219-8223, and zeroes numBBoxes at 8254 and numNodes at 8262. The observable consequence is confined to the botlib memory manager: FreeMemory (sub_100390b0) decrements data_10063a1c/data_10063a2c, which sub_10039150 prints via the showmemoryusage and memorydump LibVars checked every AAS frame (HLIL 12465-12481, mirrored at src/botlib/aas/aas_main.c:239-259). Across repeated map loads retail's "total memory blocks" grows by 2 per load and ours stays flat. The claim's secondary "stale nodes describe map A" scenario does NOT hold: AAS_DumpAASData clears the loaded flag at 11468 (data_100667e0 = 0), and every node consumer gates on it - e.g. AAS_PointAreaNum (sub_1001ae60) bails with "AAS_PointAreaNum: aas not loaded" at HLIL 22574-22576 before reading data_10066964 - so no gameplay, routing, or query result differs between the two builds.

**Failure scenario.** Load map A (nodes lump = N bytes), then load map B. Retail leaks the N-byte node block on every map change: `showmemoryusage` reports total botlib memory growing by N KB and total memory blocks growing by 1 per map load, and between AAS_DumpAASData and the point where lump 10 is re-read aasworld.nodes/numnodes still describe map A. Our build reports a flat block count and a NULL nodes pointer over the same sequence, so any memory-parity check against retail's counters disagrees.

**Fix.** Treat this as a bookkeeping decision, not a bug fix - our behaviour is strictly safer than retail's. If bit-for-bit teardown parity is the goal, the AAS-lump portion of AAS_ClearAASData must skip BOTH omitted lumps, not just nodes: drop the bboxes free/null at src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:8171-8175 and the nodes free/null at 8219-8223, and drop the `aasworld.numBBoxes = 0` at 8254 and `aasworld.numNodes = 0` at 8262, leaving those four fields to be cleared by AAS_ClearWorld's memset at 8286 (retail's equivalent memset is sub_1000ee30 @ 0x1000ee6f). Note that AAS_ClearAASData is already a superset of retail's AAS_DumpAASData (it also runs the reachability-heap shutdown, routing-cache free, areacontentstravelflags and areaEntityLists frees, none of which appear in sub_1000c490), so a true split would isolate just the 12-lump chain in retail's order: vertexes, planes, edges, edgeindex, faces, faceindex, areas, areasettings, reachability, portals, portalindex, clusters. The better option, given the only divergence is a leak we do not reproduce: keep the frees and record the deliberate deviation in a comment next to aas_map.c:8171 and 8219 - e.g. "retail AAS_DumpAASData (0x1000c490) omits the lump-0 bbox and lump-10 node frees, leaking numbboxes*32 + numnodes*12 bytes and 2 blocks per AAS load; we release them here, so showmemoryusage/memorydump block counts will not match retail across map changes." That documents why any memory-counter parity check against retail will legitimately disagree.

### LOW AAS_WriteAASLump emits an invented diagnostic instead of retail's text

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_WriteAASLump` @ `0x1000ce40`
- HLIL: 11741-11744
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:9308`

**Divergence.** src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:9308-9310 emits "AAS_WriteAASLump: error writing lump %d\n" with (int)lumpnum. Retail AAS_WriteAASLump @0x1000ce40 (HLIL:11741) emits `botimport.Print(PRT_ERROR, "error writing lump %s\n", lumpnum)` — no function-name prefix, and the lump index passed to a %s conversion, an upstream Mr. Elusive bug preserved verbatim from bspc/aas_file.c:409 and corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_file.c:522. The path is reachable: AAS_WriteAASFile is invoked from AAS_ContinueInit (HLIL:12430; our aas_main.c:182) whenever savefile or the "forcewrite" libvar is set, so any fwrite failure (disk full, read-only target) hits it. Retail therefore prints "error writing lump " and then dereferences address 0x0..0xd inside the host's vsprintf — a null-page access violation on Win32, i.e. retail dies where we log cleanly and unwind. Two sub-claims in the original are wrong and should be dropped: (a) the missing fclose is not a divergence — our AAS_WriteAASFile fcloses unconditionally at aas_map.c:9431 on every exit path, matching retail's one-fclose total, retail just does it a frame lower; (b) the invented "invalid lump bounds" guard at aas_map.c:9299 (and the invented `data == NULL` disjunct at 9306) are unreachable given library invariants — ftell cannot fail on a valid "wb" stream and no lump length can approach INT32_MAX — so they change no observable behaviour.

**Failure scenario.** A disk-full or read-only-target condition while writing an .aas file. Retail logs `error writing lump ` followed by whatever bytes live at address 0..13 (typically garbage or a fault in the host's Print); our build logs a clean `AAS_WriteAASLump: error writing lump 9`. Anything scraping botlib console output for the retail string, or any test asserting retail log parity, sees a different line.

**Fix.** Only the message text needs changing. At src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:9308-9310, drop the function-name prefix so the emitted literal prefix matches retail: BotLib_Print(PRT_ERROR, "error writing lump %d\n", (int)lumpnum); Do NOT reproduce the retail `%s`-fed-an-int literally by casting the index to `const char *`: that is UB, it faults on the null page, and reproducing a crash buys no parity a test can assert on. Add a comment recording that retail's format is "error writing lump %s\n" (bspc/aas_file.c:409; HLIL 0x1000ce93) and that %d is a deliberate deviation from a source bug whose retail effect is an access violation, not a log line. Leave the fclose structure alone — aas_map.c:9431 already closes on every exit path, so total behaviour matches retail. The "invalid lump bounds" branch at 9297-9301 may be deleted as dead invented code, but it is a cleanup, not a parity fix; if kept, it is harmless.

## `src/botlib/aas/aas_move.c`

### LOW Movement libvars are silently replaced by Q3 defaults when their value is <= 0

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_ClientMovementPrediction / AAS_WeaponJumpZVelocity / AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump` @ `0x1000f840, 0x1000f4d0, 0x10010780`
- HLIL: 13440-13455 (raw libvar loads), 13376 (jumpvel add), 14029-14031 (gravity/maxvelocity)
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_move.c:39`

**Divergence.** src/botlib/aas/aas_move.c:39 `AAS_MovePositiveLibVarValue` substitutes a hard-coded default whenever a movement libvar's `value` field (libvar_t offset +0x10, l_libvar.h:20) is <= 0. Retail never clamps: sub_10037a00 registers the physics libvars into the globals at 0x10064030..0x1006406c (HLIL 43410-43426) and every consumer dereferences `handle->value` raw. - AAS_PredictClientMovement (0x1000f840): HLIL 13440-13452 hoists sv_friction, sv_stopspeed, sv_gravity, sv_waterfriction, sv_watergravity, and frametime-scaled sv_maxwalkvelocity/sv_maxcrouchvelocity/sv_maxswimvelocity/sv_maxacceleration/sv_jumpvel, plus sv_step (13446/13448) and sv_maxsteepness (13450). sv_maxbarrier is not hoisted; it is read inline at HLIL 13901 (`org[2] - (*(data_10064060 + 0x10) + 48f)`). Reference be_aas_move.c:406-417 corroborates. Our aas_move.c:682-694 clamps eleven of these (only phys_maxacceleration at 690 uses the plain reader). - AAS_WeaponJumpZVelocity (0x1000f4d0): HLIL 13376 `return fconvert.t(var_58) + fconvert.t(*(data_10064068 + 0x10))`; reference be_aas_move.c:262 `return kvel[2] + libvar_sv_jumpvel->value;`. Our aas_move.c:114 and 157 clamp sv_jumpvel. Observable divergence: with `BotLibVarSet("sv_gravity","0")` retail predicts with zero gravity while we substitute 800; with `sv_friction 0` retail applies no friction while we apply 6; with `sv_jumpvel 0` retail's weapon-jump z velocity is just kvel[2] while ours adds 224. Same for any negative value. Corrections to the original claim: (a) The "+0x34/+0x38/+0x3c/+0x40/+0x48/+0x4c/+0x50/+0x54/+0x5c/+0x60/+0x64/+0x68" figures are the addresses of the global libvar handles, not struct offsets; the field offset is uniformly +0x10. (b) The sv_gravity == 0 scenario for AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump (0x10010780) is wrong. gravity 0 gives zvel/0 = +/-inf, maxjump = inf*0 = NaN, height2fall = NaN; the `height2fall < 0` test at HLIL 14037-14040 is `fnstsw ax; test ah,1` (C0), which the x87 also sets for unordered, so retail takes the branch and returns 0 with *velocity = phys_maxvelocity — identical to our line 615-619 early-out. That early-out is only observable for NEGATIVE sv_gravity with height2fall >= 0, where retail evaluates sqrt(h/(0.5*negative)) = NaN and the final `test ah,0x41` (C0|C3, both set for unordered, HLIL 14047-14053) returns 1/qtrue with *velocity = NaN, whereas we return qfalse with maxvelocity. (c) The `fabsf(total_time) <= 1e-6f` early-out at line 633 is not a divergence in any reachable case: for total_time == +0 with nonzero horizontal distance retail divides to +inf, fails `velocity <= maxvelocity`, and returns qfalse with *velocity = maxvelocity, which is exactly what we return. Reachability/severity: the stock game.dll never pushes these physics libvars through BotLibVarSet (no such call anywhere in ref/gladiator-bot-restored/game; bl_main.c:509 passes gravity per-client through the update struct instead), so under the shipped host all of them keep their positive registration defaults and the clamp is inert. The DLL's exported BotLibVarSet nonetheless accepts the values, so the guard is not provably unreachable — it is an exactness defect, not a live bug.

**Failure scenario.** A mod/server sets `sv_gravity 0` (zero-gravity mod) or a tester sets `sv_friction 0` / `sv_maxwalkvelocity 0` through BotLibVarSet. Retail's prediction then uses 0: no gravity is subtracted from frame_test_vel[2], no friction is applied, and the per-axis velocity clamp is 0 so the bot never accelerates — the predicted path (and therefore every jump/gap/ground stop-event decision) matches the server's actual physics. Ours silently substitutes 800 / 6 / 300, predicting a completely different trajectory from the one the client will actually take. Same for AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump: retail returns qtrue with a NaN velocity under sv_gravity 0, ours returns qfalse with sv_maxvelocity.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/aas/aas_move.c, delete `AAS_MovePositiveLibVarValue` (lines 32-48) and call `AAS_MoveLibVarValue` (default only for a NULL handle) at lines 114, 157 and 682-694, so every physics libvar is read raw as retail does. In `AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump`, delete only the `gravity <= 0.0f` early-out at lines 615-619 so the `zvel / gravity` and `height2fall / (0.5f * gravity)` divisions run exactly as retail performs them. The `fabsf(total_time) <= 1e-6f` early-out at 633-637 is behaviourally identical to retail's `+inf > maxvelocity` path; removing it is preferable for exactness but changes nothing observable, so do not cite it as a fix on its own. Apply the same change to the identical invented clamp in src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:117-125 (`AAS_ReachPositiveLibVarValue`, used at 1229, 1244, 1259, 1273-1274, 1293-1294, 2681, 3001): retail's sub_10011520 / sub_10011560 / sub_10011590 / sub_100116d0 read `*(data_10064038 + 0x10)` and divide by it unguarded (HLIL 14525, 14552, 14560, 14602-14603). Do not change the final `*velocity > maxvelocity` comparison in AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump — our `>` matches retail's `test ah,0x41` (return qtrue on less-or-equal-or-unordered) including the NaN case.

## `src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c`

### HIGH AAS_ClosestEdgePoints uses Quake III's two-endpoint range instead of retail's running VectorMiddle

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_Reachability_Jump (inline closest-edge-point selection)` @ `0x10013cc0`
- HLIL: 16830-16879: `if (bestdist-0.5 >= dist || bestdist+0.5 <= dist) { if (dist < bestdist) { beststart = v1; bestdist = dist; bestend = p1 } } else { j_sub_10013c70(&var_1ac, edi_5, &var_1ac); j_sub_10013c70(&var_1a0, &var_168, &var_1a0) }` — eight such VectorMiddle (sub_10013c70) calls at 100141d0/100141e4/10014291/100142a5/1001435f/1001436f/10014429/10014439
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:1365-1381
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:2462`

**Divergence.** Retail AAS_Reachability_Jump (sub_10013cc0 @ 0x10013cc0, with AAS_ClosestEdgePoints inlined) keeps a SINGLE beststart/bestend pair and merges equal-distance candidates with an in-place running VectorMiddle; our reconstruction instead implements Quake III's newer two-range variant. Retail evidence (HLIL, dev_tools/gladiator.dll.bndb_hlil.txt): - Frame at HLIL 16377-16384: `var_1b0_1 = 999999f` (bestdist), `var_1ac/var_1a8/var_1a4` (beststart), `var_1a0/var_19c/var_198` (bestend). Six contiguous floats; no second best pair exists in the frame. - sub_10013c70 is VectorMiddle (HLIL 16318-16323: componentwise `arg3 = arg1 + arg2`, then `j_sub_10043570(arg3, 0.5f, arg3)`). - Tie predicate (HLIL 16840-16858 for the v1/p1 block): the non-tie path is taken when `NOT(bestdist-0.5 < dist) || (bestdist+0.5 <= dist)`, so the tie branch is `dist > bestdist-0.5 && dist < bestdist+0.5` — same strictness as ours. - Tie branch body, all four candidate blocks: `VectorMiddle(beststart, cand_start, beststart)` + `VectorMiddle(bestend, cand_end, bestend)` at 100141d0/100141e4 (v1,p1), 10014291/100142a5 (v2,p2), 1001435f/1001436f (p3,v3), 10014429/10014439 (p4,v4). - Strictly-closer path (HLIL 16863-16874 etc.): `if (dist < bestdist) { beststart = cand_start; bestdist = dist; bestend = cand_end; }` — matches our AAS_ConsiderClosestEdgePoint's else-if. - No post-loop midpoint: HLIL 17145-17155 shows loop exit at 10014608 falling straight into `bestdist` vs `4f` at 1001460e, then `label_10014623` (HLIL 16388-16395) comparing `bestdist` vs `var_114_1` (maxjumpdistance). Corroboration: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:1365-1381. Provenance: retail matches Quake III's OLD, commented-out AAS_ClosestEdgePoints (dev_tools/Quake-III-Arena-master/code/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:1656-1829, VectorMiddle at 1736-1737); our code is a verbatim port of Q3's NEWER two-range version (same file, 1831+). Our divergence (src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c): - :2426-2444 AAS_ExtendClosestRange (Q3's newer greedy range-extension heuristic) - :2464-2465 tie branch calls it instead of VectorMiddle - :2470-2473, :2500-2503 duplicate writes into beststart2/bestend2 - :2703/:2705 beststart2/bestend2 locals, :2782-2783 post-loop AAS_VectorMiddle - prototype src/botlib/aas/aas_local.h:835-845 carries the extra pair Behavioural difference: the two schemes agree while at most two candidates tie (the first tie makes ExtendClosestRange copy the candidate into beststart1, so the post-loop midpoint equals retail's (a+b)/2). They diverge from the THIRD tied candidate onward: retail's running halving gives a/4 + b/4 + c/2 for ties a,b,c, while we return the midpoint of the range AAS_ExtendClosestRange greedily grew (it replaces an endpoint only when the new candidate is farther from the opposite endpoint than the current range length — a heuristic approximation of the diameter, not an exact one). Three-plus ties are routine: two parallel ground edges yield up to four equal-distance candidates within one AAS_ClosestEdgePoints call, and bestdist carries across every edge pair in the four nested loops. The resulting beststart/bestend feed the `bestdistance <= 48 && fabsf(beststart[2]-bestend[2]) < 8` walk-off-ledge gate, AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump's speed, the prediction trace, and the stored reach.start/reach.end.

**Failure scenario.** Three or more candidate point pairs whose distances all fall within 0.5 of bestdist — routine on maps where two areas face each other across several coplanar parallel edges. With candidates b0, p1, p2 retail ends at b0/4 + p1/4 + p2/2; we end at the midpoint of the two most separated of {b0,p1,p2}. The jump start/end points then differ, which changes AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump's speed, the prediction trajectory and the stored reach.start/reach.end.

**Fix.** Replace the two-range scheme with retail's single running-average pair. 1. src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c: move AAS_VectorMiddle (currently at :2644-2650) above AAS_ConsiderClosestEdgePoint, or add a forward declaration. 2. AAS_ConsiderClosestEdgePoint (:2453-2475): drop the beststart2/bestend2 parameters; make the tie branch AAS_VectorMiddle(beststart, start, beststart); AAS_VectorMiddle(bestend, end, bestend); and the else-if branch keep only `*bestdist = distance; VectorCopy(start, beststart); VectorCopy(end, bestend);`. Leave the tie predicate at :2462 as-is — it already matches retail. 3. Delete AAS_ExtendClosestRange (:2418-2444) entirely; it has no other caller. 4. AAS_ReplaceClosestEdgePoint (:2485-2504): drop the beststart2/bestend2 parameters and the two duplicate VectorCopy calls at :2501/:2503. The `distance >= *bestdist` early-out is correct (retail: `if (dist < bestdist)`). 5. AAS_ClosestEdgePoints (:2514-2635): drop the beststart2/bestend2 parameters and update the ten call sites at :2601-2632 accordingly. 6. AAS_Reachability_Jump: delete the beststart2 (:2703) and bestend2 (:2705) locals, drop them from the AAS_ClosestEdgePoints call at :2765-2776, and delete the post-loop AAS_VectorMiddle calls at :2782-2783 so `bestdistance <= 4.0f || bestdistance >= maxjumpdistance` is evaluated immediately after the loops (retail 1001460e / label_10014623). 7. src/botlib/aas/aas_local.h:835-845: remove the `vec3_t beststart2, vec3_t bestend2` parameters and rename beststart1/bestend1 to beststart/bestend. Note VectorMiddle argument order is immaterial (float addition is commutative in IEEE-754), so `AAS_VectorMiddle(beststart, start, beststart)` reproduces retail's `sub_10013c70(&var_1ac, cand, &var_1ac)` bit-exactly.

### HIGH AAS_Reachability_Jump doubles maxjumpdistance (Quake III behaviour, not Gladiator's)

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_Reachability_Jump` @ `0x10013cc0`
- HLIL: 16338 `float var_114_1 = fconvert.s(j_sub_10011590(ecx_1))` (no multiply), used at 16345-16362 for the x-y reject and again at 16388-16395 `x87_r7_53 = bestdist; temp3_1 = var_114_1`
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:1245 `maxjumpdistance = (float)AAS_MaxJumpDistance(phys_jumpvel);`
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:2682`

**Divergence.** AAS_Reachability_Jump (retail 0x10013cc0) stores AAS_MaxJumpDistance(sv_jumpvel) verbatim; we multiply it by 2 (the Quake III form), making both the x-y proximity reject and the final bestdist gate twice as permissive. Proof from the retail binary (dev_tools/assets/gladiator.dll), not only the HLIL: at 0x10013d50 the call to the AAS_MaxJumpDistance thunk (0x10001A69 -> sub_10011590) is immediately followed at 0x10013d55 by `fstp dword [esp+0B0h]` (bytes e8 14 dd fe ff / d9 9c 24 b0 00 00 00) -- no `fadd st,st` and no multiply by any 2.0 constant. That slot (S+0ACh after the push/add-esp bookkeeping) is reloaded at 0x10013d75 `fld dword [esp+0ACh]` for the per-axis reject at 0x10013d7c/0x10013d9d, and again at label_10014623 (HLIL 16388-16395) for the `bestdist < maxjumpdistance` gate. sub_10011590 itself contains no factor 2: it computes maxvelocity * (sqrt(450/(0.5*gravity)) + jumpvel/gravity), with the .rdata doubles at 0x10058018 = 0.5 and 0x10058150 = 450.0 -- identical to our AAS_MaxJumpDistance at src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:1271. Corrected magnitude (the original claim's numbers were wrong): with retail defaults sv_gravity 800, sv_maxvelocity 300, sv_jumpvel 224, AAS_MaxJumpDistance = 300 * (sqrt(450/400) + 0.28) = 402.2 units, NOT ~309. Retail's threshold is therefore 402.2 and ours is 804.4. Corrected failure scenario: two grounded, non-crouch areas whose closest ground-edge points are 600 units apart with a substantial height drop. Retail rejects the pair -- first in the x-y bbox test (`maxjumpdistance + area2->maxs[i] < area1->mins[i]`) once the areas are far enough apart, and unconditionally at the final gate since 600 >= 402.2. We accept: 600 < 804.4, so we run the full edge search and prediction, and the TRAVEL_WALKOFFLEDGE branch (AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump with zvelocity 0) succeeds on a long drop, emitting a reachability retail never links. Between 402.2 and 804.4 units we both emit extra reachabilities into the generated .aas and burn roughly 4x the area-pair search time in the widened x-y prefilter. Confirmed independently by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:1245 (`maxjumpdistance = (float)AAS_MaxJumpDistance(phys_jumpvel);`, no factor 2) versus dev_tools/Quake-III-Arena-master/code/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:2134 (`maxjumpdistance = 2 * AAS_MaxJumpDistance(phys_jumpvel);`), which is the form we copied.

**Failure scenario.** With the retail defaults (sv_gravity 800, sv_maxvelocity 300, sv_jumpvel 224) AAS_MaxJumpDistance ~= 309 units. Two areas 400 units apart: retail rejects them in the x-y bbox test (`maxjumpdistance + area2->maxs[i] < area1->mins[i]`) and, even if the bbox passed, rejects at `bestdist < maxjumpdistance`. We accept and run the full prediction, emitting TRAVEL_JUMP/TRAVEL_WALKOFFLEDGE reachabilities across gaps retail never links, and burning generation time on ~4x as many area pairs.

**Fix.** src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:2682 -- drop the Quake III doubling: float maxjumpdistance = AAS_MaxJumpDistance(jumpvelocity); No other edit is needed: AAS_MaxJumpDistance (src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:1271-1282) already matches retail sub_10011590 bit-for-bit in form, and the two consumers at lines 2686-2687 and 2784 already match retail's comparison senses. Expect reachability-generation regression tests that were tuned against the doubled 804.4 threshold to need their expected gap distances re-baselined to retail's 402.2.

### HIGH AAS_Reachability_Jump has an invented short-hop branch (speed 400, WALKOFFLEDGE)

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_Reachability_Jump` @ `0x10013cc0`
- HLIL: 16410-16422: `if (j_sub_10010780(0f, &var_1ac, &var_1a0, &var_16c) == 0) { if (j_sub_10010780(ecx_1, ...) != 0) { ebx_7 = 5; goto label_100146db } } else { ebx_7 = 7; var_16c = var_16c * 1.2 }` — only two branches exist
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:1508-1518
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:2791`

**Divergence.** `AAS_Reachability_Jump` (0x10013cc0) contains an invented short-hop branch at `src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:2791`. Retail's first action after the `bestdist > 4` / `bestdist < maxjumpdistance` / fall-damage gates is `AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump(0.0f, beststart, bestend, &speed)` at 0x1001468f (HLIL 16413) — and that helper is called exactly twice in the whole routine, with no `400`, `48`, or `8` literal present anywhere in 0x10013cc0..0x10014ac6. Our `bestdistance <= 48 && |beststart[2]-bestend[2]| < 8 -> speed = 400, traveltype = TRAVEL_WALKOFFLEDGE` branch therefore fires for gaps retail resolves differently: at dz == 0 retail's zvel-0 call divides by t == 0, overflows sv_maxvelocity and returns 0, so retail retries with sv_jumpvel and emits TRAVEL_JUMP with `cmdmove[2] = jumpvel`; at small negative dz retail's zvel-0 call succeeds and emits TRAVEL_WALKOFFLEDGE with `speed * 1.2` (e.g. 360, not 400). Two further divergences sit in the same block: `speed *= 1.05f` (line 2807) has no retail counterpart (no `1.05` constant in the routine, ref be_aas_reach.c:1517 just sets traveltype = 5), and the `VectorLength(hordir) < 10` rejection (line 2812) is JUMP-only in our code but is common to both travel types in retail at `label_100146db` (0x100146db-0x10014707, HLIL 16422-16435).

**Failure scenario.** Two ground areas 30 units apart at nearly the same height: retail calls AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump with zvel 0, which for a near-flat short hop fails (no ballistic solution), then retries with sv_jumpvel and produces a TRAVEL_JUMP with the computed speed and a jumpvel Z impulse in cmdmove. We instead force speed = 400 with a purely horizontal cmdmove and traveltype 7, so the movement prediction runs a completely different trajectory and the resulting link (if any) has the wrong travel type for the runtime mover.

**Fix.** In `src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c`, delete the `bestdistance <= 48.0f && fabsf(beststart[2]-bestend[2]) < 8.0f` branch (lines 2791-2795) so the first test is `AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump(0.0f, beststart, bestend, &speed)`. While in the same block, also drop `speed *= 1.05f` (line 2807, no retail counterpart), and hoist the `VectorSubtract(bestend, beststart, hordir); hordir[2] = 0; if (AAS_VectorLength(hordir) < 10.0f) return qfalse;` check (lines 2809-2815) out of the JUMP branch so it runs for both travel types, matching retail's shared `label_100146db` path. Resulting shape: float speed; int traveltype; if (AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump(0.0f, beststart, bestend, &speed)) { traveltype = TRAVEL_WALKOFFLEDGE; speed *= 1.2f; } else { if (!AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump(jumpvelocity, beststart, bestend, &speed)) return qfalse; traveltype = TRAVEL_JUMP; } vec3_t hordir; VectorSubtract(bestend, beststart, hordir); hordir[2] = 0.0f; if (AAS_VectorLength(hordir) < 10.0f) return qfalse;

### HIGH AAS_Reachability_Jump applies the 10-unit horizontal-length gate only to the JUMP branch

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_Reachability_Jump` @ `0x10013cc0`
- HLIL: 16422-16435: `label_100146db:` is the *common* target of both `ebx_7 = 5` and `ebx_7 = 7`; it then computes `var_190/var_18c_1` from bestend-beststart, `var_188_1 = 0`, `st0_11 = j_sub_10043500(&var_190)` and requires `!(st0_11 < 10f)`
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:1519-1521
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:2812`

**Divergence.** AAS_Reachability_Jump (retail 0x10013cc0, HLIL 16413-16435) merges both travel-type arms at label_100146db: traveltype 5 (TRAVEL_JUMP) reaches it via an explicit `goto` and traveltype 7 (TRAVEL_WALKOFFLEDGE) falls into it. Only at that merge point does retail test the horizontal component of (bestend - beststart): `var_190/var_18c_1 = bestend.xy - beststart.xy`, `var_188_1 = 0`, `st0_11 = VectorLength(&var_190)`, and the whole remainder of the routine (both -100 barrier traces, the prediction, the probe loop, the AAS_AllocReachability) executes only when `st0_11 >= 10f`; otherwise the routine returns 0. Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:1519-1521. In src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c the block lives at 2809-2815, inside the `else` (TRAVEL_JUMP) arm only, so BOTH of our walk-off-ledge paths bypass it: the zvel=0 path at 2796-2800 and the additional `bestdistance <= 48 && fabsf(dz) < 8 -> speed = 400` shortcut at 2791-2795 (which retail does not have at all). Because AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump(0, ...) succeeds for every candidate whose destination is at or below the source, traveltype 7 is the common branch and the gate is skipped for the majority of jump candidates. Concretely, for two grounded areas whose closest ground-edge points are 3 units apart horizontally and 60 units apart vertically, retail computes VectorLength((3,0,0)) = 3 < 10 and returns 0, while we run the traces and prediction and can emit a spurious TRAVEL_WALKOFFLEDGE reachability, altering the generated .aas data.

**Failure scenario.** Two areas stacked almost vertically (horizontal separation 3 units, 60-unit drop) where AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump(0,...) succeeds: retail computes VectorLength((dx,dy,0)) = 3 < 10 and returns 0, producing no link. We take the walk-off-ledge branch, skip the gate, normalize a 3-unit direction and run the prediction, emitting a spurious TRAVEL_WALKOFFLEDGE reachability.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c, remove the gate from the TRAVEL_JUMP arm (delete lines 2809-2815) and place it once after the whole traveltype/speed selection chain ends at 2816, i.e. immediately before the `vec3_t direction; VectorSubtract(bestend, beststart, direction);` at 2818-2819 that feeds the two -100 barrier traces: vec3_t horizontaldirection; VectorSubtract(bestend, beststart, horizontaldirection); horizontaldirection[2] = 0.0f; if (AAS_VectorLength(horizontaldirection) < 10.0f) { return qfalse; } That single placement covers all three of our paths, matching retail's label_100146db merge. Note this does not by itself fix the separate divergences on the same lines: retail has no `bestdistance <= 48 && fabsf(dz) < 8 -> speed = 400` shortcut (2791-2795) and no `speed *= 1.05f` on the TRAVEL_JUMP arm (2807) — retail scales only the walk-off-ledge speed, by 1.2 (HLIL 100146a0). Those should be audited separately.

### MEDIUM AAS_AreaVolume flips the face plane by backarea; retail uses the raw planenum

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_AreaVolume` @ `0x10011220`
- HLIL: 14431-14473 (deciding: 14455-14462 `int32_t* ecx_7 = data_1006693c + ((eax_19 ^ edx_7) - edx_7) * 0x18` / `int32_t edx_8 = *ecx_7 * 5` / `float* eax_25 = eax_24 + (edx_8 << 2)`)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:211 `plane = &aasworld.planes[face->planenum];`
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:981`

**Divergence.** AAS_AreaVolume (retail 0x10011220, HLIL 14431-14468) indexes the plane array with the face's raw planenum: `100112c3 int32_t edx_8 = *ecx_7 * 5` / `100112d7 float* eax_25 = eax_24 + (edx_8 << 2)`, where `*ecx_7` is offset 0 (planenum) of `face = &faces[abs(faceindex[area->firstface + i])]` and data_10066924 is aasworld.planes with a 20-byte stride. Retail never reads face+0x14 (backarea) and cannot: `1001123e arg1 = 0f` reuses the areanum argument slot as the volume accumulator right after `1001123a esi_2 = arg1 i* 0x30 + data_1006694c` computes the area pointer, so areanum is gone before the loop starts. That the decompiler would have shown a side flip is proven by AAS_Reachability_Swim at `100119f2 data_10066924 + (*ebp_5 ^ edx_4) * 0x14`, which renders the identical idiom explicitly. Our src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:981-982 instead computes `int side = face->backarea != areanum; int planenum = face->planenum ^ side;` and uses `planes[planenum]` — the Quake III Arena form of the routine, which Gladiator predates. Since AAS planes are stored in mirrored pairs (confirmed by `trace.planenum ^= 1` at ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_sample.c:399 and 451), `planes[planenum ^ 1]` carries the negated normal and dist, so `distance = -(DotProduct(corner, normal) - dist)` comes out with the opposite sign for every face where side==1 — that is, every face whose faceindex entry for this area is positive (the area is the face's frontarea), roughly half the faces of a typical area. Consequence: AAS_AreaVolume returns a value that is neither retail's nor a correct volume. Its only consumer is AAS_Reachability_Swim (our aas_reach.c:1557, retail HLIL 10011a05-10011a1a: `if (AAS_AreaVolume(area2num) < 800.0f) traveltime += 200`, encoded as `eax_21[0xa].w += 0xc8`), so generated TRAVEL_SWIM reachabilities get traveltime 201 instead of 1, or 1 instead of 201, for most water areas — a routing-cost divergence in the generated reachability data. Nothing in the caller or any wrapper compensates. ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:211 corroborates with `plane = &aasworld.planes[face->planenum];`, and that same reference emits `^ side` at be_aas_reach.c:447, 737-739 and 1821-1822 where retail genuinely does it, so line 211 is a deliberate distinction.

**Failure scenario.** Any area whose faces are not all stored with `backarea == areanum` (i.e. essentially every AAS area, since faceindex sign encodes front/back membership). Retail sums d*a with a consistent plane orientation and returns a positive volume; we flip the sign of the contribution of roughly half the faces, so AAS_AreaVolume returns a wrong (often near-zero or negative) value. The only consumer is AAS_Reachability_Swim's `if (AAS_AreaVolume(area2num) < 800.0f) traveltime += 200` (HLIL 10011a05-10011a1a), so swim reachabilities get the +200 tight-space penalty applied/omitted incorrectly for most water areas.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c, inside AAS_AreaVolume's per-face loop, delete the `side` computation and the XOR, and bound-check the raw planenum instead (keeping the existing defensive guard shape used elsewhere in the file): const aas_face_t *face = &aasworld.faces[facenum]; if (face->planenum < 0 || face->planenum >= aasworld.numPlanes) { continue; } const aas_plane_t *plane = &aasworld.planes[face->planenum]; float distance = -(DotProduct(corner, plane->normal) - plane->dist); volume += distance * AAS_FaceArea(face); That is, replace lines 981-988 (`int side = face->backarea != areanum;` / `int planenum = face->planenum ^ side;` / the `planenum` guard / `&aasworld.planes[planenum]`) with the raw-planenum form. Leave the `-(DotProduct(...) - plane->dist)` negation and the `volume += distance * AAS_FaceArea(face)` accumulation exactly as they are — both already match HLIL 100112e4/10011303. Do not touch the other `^ side` sites in this file (lines 1536, 2111, 3106-3107); those correctly mirror retail (`100119f2` and ref be_aas_reach.c:447/737/1821). Leave the `/ 3.0f` return alone unless the audit separately decides to mirror retail's `* 0.333333343f` multiply from 10011318.

### MEDIUM AAS_ReachabilityExists also searches the stored reachability lump; retail only walks the temporary per-area chain

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_ReachabilityExists` @ `0x10011700`
- HLIL: 14607-14616: the whole body is `for (int32_t* i = *(data_1006675c + (arg1 << 2)); i != 0; i = i[0xb]) if (*i == arg2) return 1; return 0`
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:352-364
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:1321`

**Divergence.** src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:1321-1345 adds a fallback search of the stored reachability lump that retail's AAS_ReachabilityExists (0x10011700) does not have. HLIL 14607-14613 shows the complete retail body is only the temporary per-area chain walk over areareachability (data_1006675c): `for (int32_t* i = *(data_1006675c + (arg1 << 2)); i != 0; i = i[0xb]) if (*i == arg2) return 1; return 0`; the next symbol starts at 0x10011740. ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:352-364 corroborates. Because neither AAS_InitReachability (aas_reach.c:254-289) nor AAS_ResetReachabilityGenerator (134-156) clears aasworld.reachability/numReachability or resets areasettings[].firstreachablearea/numreachableareas — retail does not clear them either (ref be_aas_reach.c:3020-3037) — the old file lump stays live for the entire duration of generation, not merely the first frame. With forcereachability set on a map whose .aas already carries a reachability lump, our predicate therefore returns true for every pair present in the old lump on every frame, and never self-corrects, since a vetoed pair is never generated into the chain the first loop consults. Result: AAS_ContinueInitReachability (4514-4559) skips Swim/EqualFloorHeight/Step-Barrier-WaterJump/Ladder/Jump/Grapple/WeaponJump for those pairs, and AAS_Reachability_WalkOffLedge (gated at 4421) is suppressed too, so areareachability ends nearly empty. AAS_StoreReachability then overwrites aasworld.reachability with that near-empty set, and because AAS_InitReachability set aasworld.saveFile, aas_main.c:173-175 writes the gutted reachability back over the .aas file on disk. Retail regenerates the full set. The fallback has no legitimate use: when a valid lump exists and forcereachability is off, AAS_InitReachability short-circuits and AAS_ReachabilityExists is never called at all.

**Failure scenario.** Regeneration with `forcereachability` set on a map whose .aas already contains a reachability lump. AAS_InitReachability (aas_reach.c:264-271) leaves aasworld.reachability/numReachability intact, so on the very first generation frame AAS_ContinueInitReachability's `AAS_ReachabilityExists(i, j)` returns true for every pair that existed in the old lump and `continue`s — retail would return false and regenerate. Result: forced regeneration produces almost no reachabilities, and AAS_Reachability_Ladder/Grapple/WalkOffLedge (which also gate on this predicate) are suppressed as well.

**Fix.** Delete the stored-lump fallback at src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:1321-1345 so the function ends with `return qfalse;` immediately after the areareachability chain loop, matching HLIL 10011722. Do not instead clear aasworld.reachability/numReachability in AAS_InitReachability: retail deliberately leaves the old lump allocated during generation (ref be_aas_reach.c:3020-3037), and AAS_StoreReachability (aas_reach.c:1454) relies on the old pointer still being valid to FreeMemory it, so clearing it early would both diverge from retail and leak. Keep the existing `areareachability != NULL && area1num > 0 && area1num < reachabilityareacount` bound at 1307-1308 as a portability guard; callers only ever pass area1num >= 1, so it is unreachable and does not diverge from retail's unchecked index.

### MEDIUM AAS_Reachability_Jump is missing retail's fall-damage rejection

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_Reachability_Jump` @ `0x10013cc0`
- HLIL: 16396-16410: `float var_110_2 = fconvert.s(fconvert.t(var_1a4) - fconvert.t(var_198))` (beststart[2]-bestend[2]); `eax_73 = j_sub_10011520()` (AAS_FallDamageDistance); `x87_r7_56 = float.t(var_10c)` vs `temp4_1 = var_110_2`; `if ((eax_73:1.b & 1) == 0)` continues only when falldamagedistance >= drop
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:1504-1507
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:2789`

**Divergence.** AAS_Reachability_Jump (retail 0x10013cc0) is missing retail's fall-damage rejection. HLIL 16396-16410 (0x10014648-0x1001466e): immediately after the `bestdist < AAS_MaxJumpDistance(phys_jumpvel)` gate and before the first AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump call, retail computes drop = beststart[2] - bestend[2] (`float var_110_2 = fconvert.s(fconvert.t(var_1a4) - fconvert.t(var_198))`), calls AAS_FallDamageDistance (`eax_73 = j_sub_10011520()`, sub_10011520 @ HLIL 14541), and continues only when `(eax_73:1.b & 1) == 0`, i.e. when (float)(int)AAS_FallDamageDistance() >= drop; otherwise the routine returns 0 with no reachability. Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:1504-1506. Our src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:2660-2973 has no such test anywhere in the routine (nor in the caller at aas_reach.c:4545): line 2784's distance gate falls straight through to the speed/traveltype selection at 2789-2796. Correct magnitudes and scenario: AAS_FallDamageDistance at sv_gravity 800 returns 187 (0.5*800*(sqrt(30*10000)/800)^2 = 187.5, ftol'd), not ~468. A plain dry lethal drop is NOT the failure case, because our later prediction (aas_reach.c:2901-2921) passes SE_HITGROUNDDAMAGE and rejects on (stopevent & 0x38), and our predictor flags damage at impact |vz| > ~55 (aas_move.c:873-893, (delta*10)^2*0.0001 > 30). The observable divergence is where the prediction terminates before any damage is registered: two grounded areas whose ground edges are, say, 300 units apart vertically and ~60 apart horizontally with water in the gap. Retail rejects the pair at 0x1001466e (187 < 300). We fall through, pick TRAVEL_WALKOFFLEDGE via AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump(0, ...), and the prediction stops on SE_ENTERWATER - bit 4 is in retail's 0x3d stop mask but not in the 0x38 reject mask, and aas_move.c:905-926 takes the feet-contents exit without ever evaluating the damage branch (which `swimming` would zero anyway at aas_move.c:877-880). The probe loop then matches area2num at the water surface and we emit a reachability with traveltime dist*240/sv_maxwalkvelocity+600 that retail never creates. A narrower window also exists just above 187 units, where the 0.1s frame granularity undersamples the landing velocity, and when the clip loop takes the `step` path and skips the damage test entirely.

**Failure scenario.** Two ground areas 60 units apart horizontally with the destination 500 units lower. AAS_FallDamageDistance() with sv_gravity 800 is ~468, so retail rejects the pair and produces no jump link (the walk-off-ledge generators handle it with their own 3000-tic penalty). We fall through, AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump(0,...) succeeds, and we emit a cheap TRAVEL_WALKOFFLEDGE/TRAVEL_JUMP reachability with traveltime dist*240/sv_maxwalkvelocity+600 — bots will route over lethal drops.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c, immediately after the existing distance gate at lines 2784-2787 and before any speed/traveltype selection (i.e. before the `bestdistance <= 48.0f && fabsf(beststart[2] - bestend[2]) < 8.0f` branch at 2791, which retail does not have at all), insert the retail test: /* 0x10014648-0x1001466e: reject pairs whose drop exceeds the fall damage distance before choosing a jump speed. */ if ((float)AAS_FallDamageDistance() < beststart[2] - bestend[2]) { return qfalse; } AAS_FallDamageDistance already returns int (aas_reach.c:1227), matching retail's ftol at 0x10011545, so the single (float) cast reproduces retail's `float.t(var_10c)` conversion; keep the comparison strict-less-than for rejection so that the equal case still produces a link, matching retail's `>=` continue condition. Note separately (out of scope for this claim, but visible in the same HLIL window) that the HLIL compares bestdist against AAS_MaxJumpDistance undoubled at 0x10014623/0x10013d7c while our line 2682 uses `2.0f * AAS_MaxJumpDistance(...)`, and that our 48/8 speed=400 special case at 2791 and the 1.05f multiplier at 2807 have no counterpart between 0x1001468f and 0x100146db - those deserve their own audit.

### MEDIUM AAS_Reachability_Jump scales the TRAVEL_JUMP speed by 1.05; retail applies no multiplier

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_Reachability_Jump` @ `0x10013cc0`
- HLIL: 16413-16421: the traveltype-5 path is `if (j_sub_10010780(ecx_1, &var_1ac, &var_1a0, &var_16c) != 0) { ebx_7 = 5; goto label_100146db }` with no write to var_16c; only the traveltype-7 path does `var_16c = fconvert.s(fconvert.t(var_16c) * fconvert.t(1.2))` at 100146a0
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:1510-1517
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:2807`

**Divergence.** AAS_Reachability_Jump (retail sub_10013cc0 @ 0x10013cc0) applies no speed multiplier on the traveltype 5 (TRAVEL_JUMP, sv_jumpvel) branch: HLIL 100146ca sets `ebx_7 = 5` and jumps directly to label_100146db, skipping the `var_16c *= 1.2` at 100146a0 that belongs solely to the traveltype 7 (zvel 0, TRAVEL_WALKOFFLEDGE) branch. The unmultiplied `var_16c` is then used verbatim at 10014927 as the VectorScale magnitude for the prediction cmdmove. Our src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:2807 does `speed *= 1.05f;` on that branch, a constant imported from Quake III Arena's version of the routine, which retail does not contain (the literal 1.05 appears nowhere in the HLIL).

**Failure scenario.** Any area pair that needs a real jump (zvel 0 has no solution): retail feeds AAS_ClientMovementPrediction cmdmove = dir*speed with cmdmove[2] = sv_jumpvel; we feed dir*(speed*1.05). The predicted landing point is ~5% further along dir, so the -0/-8/-16/-24/-32 probe loop lands in a different area and the jump link is created or rejected differently from retail on marginal gaps.

**Fix.** Delete `speed *= 1.05f;` at src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:2807, leaving the TRAVEL_JUMP branch as `if (!AAS_HorizontalVelocityForJump(jumpvelocity, beststart, bestend, &speed)) return qfalse; traveltype = TRAVEL_JUMP;`. Keep `speed *= 1.2f;` at line 2798 on the zvel-0 / TRAVEL_WALKOFFLEDGE branch, which does match 100146a0. (Separately, and not part of this fix: the `AAS_VectorLength(horizontaldirection) < 10.0f` guard at lines 2809-2815 should be hoisted out of the TRAVEL_JUMP branch so it runs for both travel types, matching the common join at label_100146db / HLIL 16422-16435.)

### MEDIUM AAS_Reachability_Jump adds a lava/slime contents term to the two barrier-trace rejections

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_Reachability_Jump` @ `0x10013cc0`
- HLIL: 16604-16627 (first trace) and 16574-16603 (second trace): the only two conditions are `planes[trace.planenum].normal[2] < 0.7` (100147e7/100148be) and `teststart[2] - trace.endpos[2] > sv_maxbarrier` (1001480d/100148de); there is no AAS_PointContents call
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:1529-1541
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:2839`

**Divergence.** AAS_Reachability_Jump (sub_10013cc0, 0x10013cc0) performs no contents query of any kind - AAS_PointContents (0x1001afa0) and the import thunk (0x10003080) appear nowhere in HLIL lines 16327-17190. Each of the two barrier probes accepts the candidate iff `!trace.startsolid && (trace.fraction >= 1 || aasworld.planes[trace.planenum].normal[2] < 0.7 || teststart[2] - trace.endpos[2] > sv_maxbarrier)`, proved by 100147cf/100147f2/1001480d for probe 1 and 100148a5/100148be/100148de for probe 2, corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:1528-1541. Our src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:2838-2841 and 2859-2862 add a third conjunct `(AAS_PointContents(trace.endpos) & (CONTENTS_LAVA|CONTENTS_SLIME)) == 0` to each rejection, so a probe that lands on walkable ground within sv_maxbarrier fails to reject the candidate whenever the trace endpoint sits in lava or slime. Because trace.endpos is an AAS client-origin position (~24 units above the blocking floor), the realizable case is a liquid pool deep enough that floor+24 is still submerged: e.g. ledge edge at z=0, lava surface z=-10, lava floor z=-50, endpos z=-26, drop 26 <= sv_maxbarrier 50, plane normal (0,0,1). Retail returns 0 and emits no jump link; we skip the rejection, run the prediction, and can emit a TRAVEL_JUMP/TRAVEL_WALKOFFLEDGE reachability retail never produces. The extra term is live (AAS_PointContents forwards to gi.pointcontents via src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:217) and no existing test depends on it.

**Failure scenario.** A ledge whose 1-unit-inset probe traces down onto a lava surface 10 units below with a floor normal of (0,0,1): retail sees fraction<1, normal[2]=1 >= 0.7, drop 10 <= sv_maxbarrier(50) and returns 0 — no jump link. We skip the rejection because the endpoint is in lava, continue to the prediction, and can emit a jump reachability that starts on a lava lip.

**Fix.** Delete the `(AAS_PointContents(trace.endpos) & (CONTENTS_LAVA | CONTENTS_SLIME)) == 0 &&` conjunct from both rejection conditions in src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c, at lines 2839 and 2860, leaving `if (plane->normal[2] >= 0.7f && teststart[2] - trace.endpos[2] <= AAS_ReachPositiveLibVarValue(Bridge_MaxBarrier(), 50.0f)) return qfalse;` in each block. Do not otherwise disturb the surrounding startsolid / fraction / planenum-bounds structure, which already matches retail. tests/aas/test_aas_reach.c:640 does not exercise this path and needs no change.

### MEDIUM AAS_Reachability_Grapple adds an invented cluster-portal / 20-area trace rejection

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_Reachability_Grapple` @ `0x10016ba0`
- HLIL: 19096-19110: `if ((*(data_10066954 + eax_36 * 0x1c) & 6) == 0 && eax_36 != arg1 && j_sub_10011700(arg1, eax_36) == 0 && j_sub_10011670(eax_36) != 0) { int32_t* eax_41 = j_sub_10010ff0(); ... }` — the allocation follows the contents/area checks immediately, with no trace-areas call in between
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:2482-2507
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:3899`

**Divergence.** AAS_Reachability_Grapple (retail 0x10016ba0, HLIL 18659-19180) allocates the reachability immediately after the destination-area checks at 100170b6 (`contents & 6 == 0`, `areanum != area1num`, `!AAS_ReachabilityExists`, `AAS_AreaGrounded`) - see 100170df `j_sub_10010ff0()`. Retail's AAS_TraceAreas is sub_1001ba00 and is not called anywhere inside sub_10016ba0. Our src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:3899-3920 inserts a Quake III-derived guard (verbatim from dev_tools/Quake-III-Arena-master/code/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:3900-3907) that sweeps `AAS_TraceAreas(areastart, bsptrace.endpos, tracedareas, NULL, 20)` and `continue`s when the sweep saturates (numareas >= 20) or when any swept area passes AAS_AreaClusterPortal. Both rejects are live: our AAS_TraceAreas (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:2747) is a real BSP sweep and AAS_AREACONTENTS_CLUSTERPORTAL (bit 8) is populated in aasworld.areasettings by the time reachability generation runs. The caller (src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:4557) matches retail 10018a86 and does not compensate; retail also has no calcgrapplereach gate, so grapple generation always runs. Effect: for any grapple candidate whose hook path crosses a cluster-portal area or spans 20+ areas, retail creates a TRAVEL_GRAPPLEHOOK (14) reachability with traveltime = 500.0 + VectorLength(end-start)*0.25 and increments reach_grapple, while we silently drop it - so our grapple reachability counts and the resulting routing graph are systematically a subset of retail's.

**Failure scenario.** A grapple shot whose hook path crosses a cluster portal — the common case for a hook fired between two rooms — is created by retail as a TRAVEL_GRAPPLEHOOK reachability with traveltime 500 + len*0.25, but silently dropped by us. Likewise any hook path crossing 20+ areas. Grapple reachability counts come out systematically lower than retail's.

**Fix.** Delete the invented block at src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:3899-3920 (the `int tracedareas[20]` declaration, the AAS_TraceAreas call, the `numareas >= 20` reject, and the AAS_AreaClusterPortal scan loop with its `areaindex < numareas` reject) so that the `VectorSubtract(bsptrace.endpos, areastart, direction)` / AAS_LinkAdjacentReachability sequence at 3922-3930 follows the grounded/contents/self/exists check at 3888-3897 directly, matching HLIL 100170b6 -> 100170df. Leave the rest of the routine untouched; the traveltype (TRAVEL_GRAPPLEHOOK), traveltime formula (500.0f + len*0.25f), facenum assignment, reach_grapple increment, and the NULL-allocation early return already match retail.

### LOW Reachability progress message prints one percent sign; retail's format string emits two

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_ContinueInitReachability` @ `0x10018920`
- HLIL: 21024 `data_10063fe8(1, "\r%6d%%%%", 0x64)` and 21029 `var_18_9 = "\r%6d%%%%"` (string data at 1005bd74: `char data_1005bd74[0x9] = "\r%6d%%%%", 0`)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_reach.c:3008 and :3013
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:4588`

**Divergence.** AAS_ContinueInitReachability @ 0x10018920 uses the format literal `"\r%6d%%%%"` (four literal `%` bytes; proven by HLIL 21024 and 21029, and by the string data at 1005bd74 declared `char data_1005bd74[0x9] = "\r%6d%%%%", 0` — 8 content bytes + NUL). Because the shipped game.dll's `BotLibImport_Print` (ref/gladiator-bot-restored/game/bl_main.c:740-791) applies exactly one printf pass — `vsprintf(str, fmt, ap)` followed by `gi.dprintf("%s", str)`, where the `"%s"` prevents any second interpretation — retail's `%%%%` collapses once to `%%`, so the console receives two literal percent signs: `\r 100%%`. Our reconstruction uses `"\r%6d%%"` at src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c:4588 and :4595, and our print chain has the same single-pass topology (BotLib_Print vsnprintfs at src/botlib/common/l_log.c:281 then calls `imports->Print(priority, "%s", message)` at :67; src/q2bridge/update_translator.c:263 likewise), so we emit only one percent sign: `\r 100%`. Every reachability progress tick therefore differs from retail by one trailing `%` character. This is a cosmetic diagnostic-text divergence only — no state, control-flow, or timing effect. The adjacent percentage arithmetic already matches retail (HLIL 21028 `divs.dp.d(sx.q(eax_23 * 0x64), ecx_4)` == our `numReachabilityAreas * 100 / numAreas`).

**Failure scenario.** During reachability generation on any map the retail console/log shows lines like `\r 37%%`; ours shows `\r 37%`. Any log-diffing or console-scraping against retail output mismatches on every progress tick.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/aas/aas_reach.c, change the format literal from `"\r%6d%%"` to `"\r%6d%%%%"` in both places: - line 4588: `BotLib_Print(PRT_MESSAGE, "\r%6d%%%%", 100);` - line 4595 (the literal argument of the call spanning 4594-4596): `"\r%6d%%%%"`, leaving the `aasworld.numReachabilityAreas * 100 / aasworld.numAreas` argument on 4596 unchanged. No other call site needs touching: `\r%6d%%%%` is the only `%%%%` literal in the retail DLL, and the three cluster-progress strings in src/botlib/aas/aas_cluster.c:804, :937 and :938 already match retail exactly. Do not "fix" the doubled percent as if it were a typo — it is retail's own authoring slip and reproducing it is the point of bug-for-bug parity.

## `src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c`

### MEDIUM AAS_InitRoutingUpdate's two persistent numareas*40 allocations are never made

- status: **open**
- retail: `AAS_InitRoutingUpdate` @ `0x100194a0`
- HLIL: 21436-21451
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:578`

**Divergence.** Retail `AAS_InitRoutingUpdate` (sub_100194a0, 0x100194a0, HLIL 21436-21451) frees any existing `aasworld.areaupdate` / `aasworld.portalupdate` and allocates each as `GetClearedMemory(numareas * 0x28)`. It runs once per routing init: `AAS_InitRouting` (sub_10019520, HLIL 21456-21463) calls it second, and AAS_ContinueInit (sub_1000df30) calls AAS_InitRouting at 0x1000dfc9 (HLIL 12439). Both consumers only clear the shared buffer — `AAS_UpdateAreaRoutingCache` (sub_10019700) memsets `data_10066a68` for `numareas*0x28` bytes (HLIL 21588-21593) and `AAS_UpdatePortalRoutingCache` (sub_10019c00) memsets `data_10066a6c` (HLIL 21784-21788). Retail never frees the pair at map teardown: AAS_FreeRoutingCaches (sub_10019550, HLIL 21468-21471) does not touch them; they live until the next AAS_InitRoutingUpdate. Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_route.c:307-317, be_aas_def.h:279-280 (areaupdate at +0x288 = VA 0x10066A68, portalupdate at +0x28C), and the memsets at be_aas_route.c:422 and :610. We have no equivalent state. `AAS_InitRetailRoutingCaches` (src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:388) allocates only the two cache-head tables, and both update passes allocate a fresh buffer per call and free it: GetClearedMemory at aas_route.c:578-580 and :836-838, FreeMemory at :714 and :969. Observable divergence: retail's botlib counters (data_10063a1c bytes / data_10063a2c blocks, maintained in sub_10038f90 HLIL 44269-44270 and sub_100390b0 HLIL 44312-44313) carry two extra permanent blocks and 2*numareas*40 bytes from routing init until the next map load. Those counters are printed by sub_10039150 ("total botlib memory: %d KB", "total memory blocks: %d", HLIL 44333-44334) via the `showmemoryusage` and `memorydump` libvars in AAS_StartFrame (HLIL 12465-12481) — a diagnostic our build reproduces at src/botlib/aas/aas_main.c:239-259. Under our build the steady-state block count is 2 lower and the byte total is short by the two arrays, while transiently spiking by one block during each cache population. The exact byte figure (~468 KB on a 6000-area map) is retail's; our 64-bit aas_retailroutingupdate_t is 32 bytes rather than 40, so byte-exactness is out of reach by design and only the block count and the allocation lifetime are meaningfully comparable.

**Failure scenario.** Retail's botlib memory counters carry 2 extra blocks and 2*numareas*40 bytes for the whole map lifetime. On a 6000-area map, `showmemoryusage` prints "total botlib memory" ~468 KB higher and "total memory blocks" 2 higher under retail than under our build (our aas_main.c:231-245 prints the same diagnostic). It also puts a GetClearedMemory/FreeMemory pair in the routing hot path that retail does not have.

**Fix.** Add persistent `areaupdate` and `portalupdate` arrays to aasworld (aas_local.h) alongside the existing retail cache-head pointers, typed as the existing `aas_retailroutingupdate_t` / `aas_retailportalupdate_t` (retail uses a single aas_routingupdate_t for both; keeping our two typed structs is fine since only observable behaviour matters). Add an `AAS_InitRoutingUpdate(void)` in src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c that mirrors sub_100194a0 exactly: if `aasworld.areaupdate` is non-NULL FreeMemory it, then `aasworld.areaupdate = GetClearedMemory((size_t)aasworld.numAreas * sizeof(aas_retailroutingupdate_t))`; then the same free-then-allocate for `aasworld.portalupdate`. Retail performs the free before each allocate, in that order, so re-init on a second map load nets zero extra blocks. Call it from `AAS_InitRetailRoutingCaches` (aas_route.c:388) before the two cache-head tables are built, matching AAS_InitRouting's order (InitTravelFlagFromType, InitRoutingUpdate, CreateReversedReachability, InitClusterAreaCache, InitPortalCache, CalculateAreaTravelTimes). In `AAS_UpdateRetailAreaRoutingCache`, delete lines 578-584 and the `FreeMemory(updates)` at :714; instead use `aas_retailroutingupdate_t *updates = aasworld.areaupdate;` and `memset(updates, 0, (size_t)aasworld.numAreas * sizeof(*updates));` after the existing entry guards. Same for `AAS_UpdateRetailPortalRoutingCache`: drop 836-842 and :969, use `aasworld.portalupdate` with the matching memset. The `updates == NULL` early-returns become a plain guard on the aasworld pointer (retail has no allocation-failure check at all; keep only what the numAreas<=0 case needs). Do NOT free the pair in AAS_FreeRetailRoutingCaches or at AAS shutdown — retail's AAS_FreeRoutingCaches (0x10019550) leaves them allocated, so freeing them there would swap the +2 block divergence for a -2 one after map teardown.

### MEDIUM AAS_AreaTravelTime returns 0 for sub-unit distances; retail clamps to 1

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_AreaTravelTime` @ `0x10018f50`
- HLIL: 21158-21181 (deciding: 21176-21181)
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:1888`

**Divergence.** AAS_AreaTravelTime @ 0x10018f50 truncates the scaled distance to int BEFORE clamping: HLIL 21176-21181 is `int32_t result = __ftol(x87control, x87_r7_8)` / `if (result s> 0) return result` / `return 1`. Retail therefore returns 1 for every scaled value in the open interval (0,1). Our port at src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:1888 clamps on the float first (`if (distance <= 0.0f) return 1U;`) and then truncates (`return (unsigned short)distance;`), so it returns 0 across that whole band. The affected band is the scaled value, not the raw distance: raw distance in (0, 3.0303) for a normal area (x0.33), (0, 0.7692) for a crouch area (x1.3), and (0, 1.0) for a swim area (x1.0). The common non-crouch case therefore diverges for any separation under about three units, not merely sub-unit ones. Retail's only direct caller is HLIL line 21259 inside AAS_CalculateAreaTravelTimes @ 0x10019010, which fills the precomputed aasworld.areatraveltimes[area][outreach][inreach] matrix from an outgoing reach `start` and an incoming reach `end` in the same area — a pair that is routinely within a few units for two-way walk reachabilities. Retail's routing-cache update then reads the clamped >=1 value out of that matrix. Our reconstruction computes that same term live at aas_route.c:2019 through the unclamped AAS_AreaTravelTime, so our fallback route cache accumulates 0 where retail accumulates 1. The public wrappers at aas_route.c:2446 (AAS_AreaTravelTimeToGoalArea origin term), 2704 (route-trace local cost) and 3368 (AAS_NearestHideArea) inherit the same loss, though note those origin-based call sites have no direct counterpart in retail's AAS_AreaTravelTimeToGoalArea @ 0x10019FA0, which takes no origin argument. Our AAS_RetailLocalTravelTime at aas_route.c:512 already implements the correct truncate-then-clamp tail (lines 539-545) and already scales in double, so only the public AAS_AreaTravelTime is wrong. Secondary and much smaller: retail multiplies in x87 against the double constants 1.3 and 0.33, while we use 1.3f/0.33f on a float, which can flip the truncation exactly at integer boundaries.

**Failure scenario.** Two-way walk reachabilities between adjacent areas routinely have the incoming reach's `end` and the outgoing reach's `start` within a few units of each other. With |start-end| = 2.0 in a non-crouch, non-swim area, retail computes 2.0*0.33 = 0.66 -> (int)0.66 = 0 -> returns 1; we return (unsigned short)0.66 = 0. Live callers: AAS_AreaTravelTimeToGoalArea line 2446 (origin -> first-reach start term) and AAS_NearestHideArea line 3368 both silently drop that unit of cost, and a 0 here can turn a real local cost into "free".

**Fix.** In src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c, replace the tail of AAS_AreaTravelTime (lines 1874-1897) so the scaling is done in double and the clamp happens AFTER truncation, mirroring AAS_RetailLocalTravelTime: double scaled = (double)VectorDistance(start, end); if (AAS_AreaCrouch(areanum)) { scaled *= 1.3; } else if (!AAS_AreaSwim(areanum)) { scaled *= 0.33; } int traveltime = (int)scaled; if (traveltime <= 0) { traveltime = 1; } return (unsigned short)traveltime; Notes: - Keep the existing `start == NULL || end == NULL` early-out returning 0; retail would fault there, so it is a host-portability guard, not behaviour. - The swim branch becomes a no-op multiply exactly as retail does (`* 1.0`), so writing it as `else if (!AAS_AreaSwim(areanum)) scaled *= 0.33;` preserves the call order of AAS_AreaCrouch then AAS_AreaSwim that HLIL 21167-21171 shows. Do not collapse it in a way that skips the AAS_AreaSwim call, since retail evaluates it whenever crouch is false. - Drop the `distance >= ROUTE_INVALID_TIME` clamp at line 1892: retail has no upper clamp and returns the raw int. If it is kept for host safety, note in a comment that it is ours, not retail's; it only differs above 65535 travel units, which the retail caller cannot produce from in-area distances.

### MEDIUM AAS_RandomGoalArea passes an origin, turning retail's single route query per candidate into a full per-reachability scan

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_RandomGoalArea` @ `0x1001a410`
- HLIL: 22136-22209 (deciding: 22163)
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:3125`

**Divergence.** src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:3125 passes `aasworld.areas[areanum].center` as the origin to our 4-arg AAS_AreaTravelTimeToGoalArea. Retail's candidate gate at 0x1001a473 (HLIL 22163) is the bare three-argument query `j_sub_10019fa0(arg1, ebx, arg2) u> 0`, and retail's AAS_AreaTravelTimeToGoalArea (0x10019fa0, HLIL 21945; ref be_aas_route.h:25) has no origin parameter at all - the argument is a Q3 leftover (Q3 be_aas_route.c:2017). The non-NULL origin sends our wrapper into the branch at aas_route.c:2441, which runs AAS_RetailFirstReachabilityToGoalArea: one extra AAS_RetailAreaTravelTimeToGoalArea per outgoing reachability of areanum, for every candidate that passes the travel-time gate. Retail performs none of those queries here. The extra queries do not change the gate itself (line 2432 already returns 0 when the base travel time is 0, and AAS_AreaTravelTime is non-negative, so zero-ness matches retail). They diverge through side effects on shared routing state: most are cache hits, but a query whose reach->areanum is a portal area bordering the candidate's cluster reaches AAS_GetRetailAreaRoutingCache(goalcluster, candidate, flags) and creates a cluster cache retail never builds at this site. Each such creation increments g_retail_area_cache_updates (printed by AAS_RunFrameDiagnostics under `showcacheupdates`, aas_main.c:230-235, retail AAS_RoutingInfo @ 0x1001a610) and g_retail_frame_routing_updates, which gates every remaining routing query in the frame via the `> 10` test at aas_route.c:2202 (retail 0x10019fec, HLIL 21967). Every extra query also refreshes cache->time, perturbing the 15-second cache aging order. Magnitude is roughly +1 routing update per routable candidate in boundary-adjacent topologies - not the ~9-per-candidate collapse originally claimed.

**Failure scenario.** Bot roams (bot_goal.c:2076 -> AAS_RandomGoalArea) in an area with 8 outgoing reachabilities. Retail burns at most 1 frameroutingupdate per candidate, so it scans many candidates before the >10 cutoff trips and normally returns a roam goal. We burn up to 9 per candidate, so after ~2 candidates every subsequent AAS_AreaTravelTimeToGoalArea in that frame returns 0 and AAS_RandomGoalArea returns qfalse - the bot gets no roam goal that frame - plus ~9x the routing work.

**Fix.** Pass NULL as the origin at src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:3126 so the candidate gate is retail's bare three-argument routing query, matching what AAS_AlternativeRouteGoals already does at lines 2950, 2980 and 2995: int traveltime = AAS_AreaTravelTimeToGoalArea(areanum, NULL, candidate, travelflags); No other change is needed - the accept/reject behaviour of the loop is unchanged, only the extra AAS_RetailFirstReachabilityToGoalArea scan and its cache side effects disappear. Separately worth auditing (out of scope for this claim): retail has no origin argument on this routine anywhere, so the other call sites that pass a non-NULL origin - src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:1510, src/botlib/ai_goal/bot_goal.c:2271 - should each be checked against their retail call site to confirm the local travel-time addition belongs there rather than being another Q3 carry-over.

### LOW sub_1001AB80 allocates the alt-routing scratch with GetMemory, we use GetClearedMemory

- status: **applied**
- retail: `sub_1001AB80` @ `0x1001ab80`
- HLIL: 22428-22443 (deciding: 22435, 22441)
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:1201`

**Divergence.** Confirmed but overstated in impact: sub_1001ab80 (HLIL 22435, 22441) allocates both alt-routing scratch arrays with j_sub_10038f90 = GetMemory (uncleared), while our AAS_InitAlternativeRoutingScratch uses GetClearedMemory at src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:1201 and :1209. The scenario's "wrong baseline for any future code" framing overstates the risk: the sole consumer of both arrays, AAS_AlternativeRouteGoals (sub_1001a720), has no caller anywhere in the retail image — only the ILT thunk j_sub_1001a720 at HLIL 1824 exists, with zero call sites and no data-table reference — so retail never reads these buffers at all. Even if it did, sub_1001a720 memsets the whole midrange table at 1001a7a2 and resets the cluster counter at 1001a8cc before any read. The only real delta is an extra numareas*12-byte memset at map load; allocation totals are unchanged because GetClearedMemory routes through GetMemory. This is allocation-parity only, with no reachable behavioural effect.

**Failure scenario.** Latent rather than currently observable: AAS_AlternativeRouteGoals memsets the midrange table before use and clusterareas is always written before read, so no uninitialised byte is consumed today. The divergence is an extra numareas*12-byte memset at map load and a wrong baseline for any future code that reads either array before writing it.

**Fix.** Change both calls in AAS_InitAlternativeRoutingScratch from GetClearedMemory to GetMemory — src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:1201-1203 (g_alternative_route_midrange) and :1209-1211 (g_alternative_route_cluster_areas) — matching j_sub_10038f90 at HLIL 22435/22441 and ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_routealt.c:234,237. Keep the explicit memset of the midrange table at aas_route.c:2955, which is what makes the uncleared allocation safe and mirrors retail's memset at 1001a7a2, and keep the NULL checks after each allocation. Do not extend this change to any other allocation in the TU: the routing/cluster-area/portal caches, the routing update array, and portals/portalindex/clusters all use j_sub_10039000 = GetClearedMemory in retail and already match.

### LOW AAS_ReachabilityFromNum range check is one tighter than retail's

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_ReachabilityFromNum` @ `0x1001a2e0`
- HLIL: 22096-22105 (deciding: 22098)
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:2568`

**Divergence.** AAS_ReachabilityFromNum (retail 0x1001a2e0, HLIL line 22098) rejects with `arg2 s> data_10066958`, i.e. `num > aasworld.reachabilitysize`, so `num == reachabilitysize` is ACCEPTED and memcpy'd from `reachability + num * 0x2c` — 44 bytes past the end of the lump allocated at 0x1000cb33 as exactly `count * 0x2c` bytes. The independent reconstruction (ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_route.c:864) agrees. Our src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:2568 uses `num >= aasworld.numReachability` and returns a zeroed record instead. Contrary to the original claim, this is NOT limited to externally supplied numbers: sub_1001a2e0 is not exported, and AAS_NextAreaReachability (HLIL 22116-22117) returns `firstreachablearea` for reachnum 0 with no numreachableareas cap. Retail's sub_100310e0 (0x100310fa, HLIL 38729/38759) and our own equivalents (src/botlib/ai_move/bot_move.c:354-373, src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:2362-2371) feed that straight into AAS_ReachabilityFromNum. For an area with numreachableareas == 0 whose trailing areas also carry none, firstreachablearea == reachabilitysize, so a well-formed AAS file reaches the boundary. Retail then reads 44 garbage bytes; we return zeros.

**Failure scenario.** A caller that hands back num == aasworld.numReachability (e.g. a stale stored reachnum) gets whatever 44 bytes follow the reachability lump under retail, versus an all-zero reachability from us; the caller then sees traveltype 0 / areanum 0 rather than garbage. Normal callers stay below the bound (AAS_NextAreaReachability caps at firstreachablearea+numreachableareas), so this is only reachable through externally supplied reachability numbers.

**Fix.** Do not blindly relax the bound to `num > aasworld.numReachability` — retail's load path allocates exactly `numReachability * sizeof(aas_reachability_t)`, so matching the comparison alone converts a clamp into a genuine heap over-read, and the bytes retail returns there are nondeterministic (they cannot be reproduced for parity anyway). Either: (a) keep `num >= aasworld.numReachability` at src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:2568 and add a comment citing retail 0x1001a2fc / HLIL 22098 recording that retail used `>` and read one record past the lump — this is a deliberate, documented hardening; or (b) if bit-for-bit shape matters more, change to `num > aasworld.numReachability` AND over-allocate the reachability lump by one zero-filled record in src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c (near :9189) so the extra index is defined and yields the same all-zero record our clamp produces today. Either way, keep the guard consistent with the sibling pre-filter at src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:2366 so the two paths do not disagree about the boundary index.

### LOW AAS_AlternativeRouteGoals is missing retail's per-candidate Log_Write("%d midrange area %d")

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_AlternativeRouteGoals (sub_1001A720)` @ `0x1001a720`
- HLIL: 22262-22417 (deciding: 22313-22319)
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:3010`

**Divergence.** AAS_AlternativeRouteGoals (sub_1001A720 @ 0x1001a720) is missing retail's per-candidate Log_Write("%d midrange area %d", n, areanum). HLIL 22313-22319: inside the phase-1 acceptance branch retail pushes esi (areanum) at 1001a861 and ebx (a 0-based accepted-candidate counter, initialised to 0 at 1001a731) at 1001a862; stores midrangeareas[areanum].valid=1 (1001a868), .starttime=eax_9 (1001a874), .goaltime=eax_12 (1001a87f); calls the Log_Write thunk j_sub_10038d80("%d midrange area %d") at 1001a884; then increments ebx at 1001a88c. Push order makes the vararg order (counter, areanum), so the first accepted area logs "0 midrange area <n>". The counter is dead after the loop — phase 2 counts emitted goals separately in arg2, and the tail print at 1001aa85 uses arg2 — so its only consumer is this message. Our src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:3008-3010 writes the three fields and emits nothing, and keeps no such counter. Confirmed not handled in a caller: "midrange area" appears nowhere in src/, and AAS_AlternativeRouteGoals has a single definition at aas_route.c:2908 with no logging wrapper. Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_routealt.c:166-171. Effect is log-only: with the botlib logfile enabled, retail appends one line per accepted midrange area and our build appends none. The claim's parenthetical about the count "lagging by one" is inaccurate — it is simply a 0-based index printed before its increment, not a retail quirk.

**Failure scenario.** With botlib logging enabled, calling AAS_AlternativeRouteGoals on a map with N route-portal areas inside the 1.5x detour budget makes retail append N lines "0 midrange area 123", "1 midrange area 145", ... to the log; our build appends none, so the log diff is N lines short.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c, add `int nummidrangeareas = 0;` before the phase-1 loop (before line 2960), and immediately after the goaltime store at line 3010 insert: BotLib_LogWrite("%d midrange area %d", nummidrangeareas, areanum); nummidrangeareas += 1; Order matters and must match retail exactly: the three field stores first, then the log write with the pre-increment counter value (so the first accepted area prints 0), then the increment. Use BotLib_LogWrite (the repo's Log_Write equivalent, declared in src/botlib/common/l_log.h:45 and already used at aas_route.c:3142), not BotLib_Print — sub_10038d80 is the logfile writer, not botimport.Print. Keep the format string byte-identical, including the absence of a trailing newline: retail passes "%d midrange area %d" with no "\n" or "\r\n". The counter is otherwise unused; do not conflate it with numaltroutegoals in the phase-2 loop.

### LOW Cumulative area/portal cache-update counters are zeroed on map load; retail never resets them

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AAS_RoutingInfo / numareacacheupdates / numportalcacheupdates` @ `0x1001a610`
- HLIL: 22217-22220 (plus the only writes: 21591, 21783; .data initialisers 78840, 78844)
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:3480`

**Divergence.** Retail's cumulative routing-cache counters `numareacacheupdates` (data_10066758) and `numportalcacheupdates` (data_10066748) are never reset for the entire time gladiator.dll is mapped; our reconstruction zeroes them on every AAS load and on AAS shutdown. Proof: the two globals appear exactly three times each in the whole HLIL — one increment, one read, one BSS initialiser. Area: `1001972a data_10066758 += 1` in AAS_UpdateAreaRoutingCache (sub_10019700), read at `1001a61d` in AAS_RoutingInfo (sub_1001a610, HLIL 22217-22220), initialiser at HLIL 78844. Portal: `10019c09 data_10066748 += 1` in AAS_UpdatePortalRoutingCache (sub_10019c00), read at `1001a63a`, initialiser at HLIL 78840. There are no aliased writes (no `0x10066748`/`0x10066758` literals anywhere in the listing). Crucially they sit *outside* aasworld: aasworld's base is 0x100667e0 (frameroutingupdates at +0x290 == data_10066a70, zeroed at `1000e03a`) and its size is 0x2A4, so AAS_Shutdown's `memset(&aasworld, 0, 0x2A4)` at 0x1000EE30 leaves both counters intact. AAS_DumpAASData (0x1000C490, ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_file.c:174-226), which retail runs at the head of every AAS_LoadAASFile (0x1000C730, ref be_aas_file.c:335), does not touch them either. They are therefore per-DLL-load totals that survive both map loads and full BotShutdownLibrary/BotSetupLibrary cycles. Our AAS_RouteFrameResetDiagnostics (src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:3477-3483) zeroes g_retail_area_cache_updates and g_retail_portal_cache_updates, and is invoked from AAS_ClearAASData (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:8151) at all four sites that mirror retail's AAS_DumpAASData: the head of the AAS load (aas_map.c:8537), the two load-failure paths (9214, 9221), and AAS_ClearWorld/full shutdown (8281). Observable scenario: run a map rotation with bots. g_spawn.c:853 calls BotLib_BotLoadMap for every already-loaded botlib on each level load, and BotUseLibrary only refcounts (bl_main.c:1195-1210), so gladiator.dll stays mapped while bot clients persist across the level change (guaranteed if `freebotlib 0`, bl_main.c:1226-1227). After bots have routed on map A and map B has loaded, setting `showcacheupdates 1` makes retail print the running cross-map total via AAS_StartFrame 0x1000e05a (HLIL 12456-12463), while we print only the counts accrued since map B loaded. The same divergence appears across a BotShutdownLibrary -> BotSetupLibrary cycle without a FreeLibrary.
