Impact is diagnostic only: both counters are read solely by AAS_RoutingInfo behind the one-shot `showcacheupdates` libvar and feed no bot decision. The behaviour-carrying per-frame counter is the separate aasworld.frameroutingupdates (data_10066a70, our g_retail_frame_routing_updates), which gates the >10 budget test (src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:2202; ref be_aas_route.c:763); that one is correctly zeroed each frame and is unaffected.

**Failure scenario.** Load map A, let bots route, then load map B and set `showcacheupdates 1`. Retail prints the running total across both maps; we print only the updates since map B was loaded.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:3477-3483, drop the two cumulative resets from AAS_RouteFrameResetDiagnostics so the routine only clears the frame-scoped state: void AAS_RouteFrameResetDiagnostics(void) { memset(&g_route_frame_state, 0, sizeof(g_route_frame_state)); g_retail_frame_routing_updates = 0; } Leave g_retail_area_cache_updates and g_retail_portal_cache_updates untouched for the whole lifetime of the library — they must not be cleared by AAS_ClearAASData (src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:8151) on AAS load or on the load-failure paths, nor by AAS_ClearWorld/AAS_Shutdown, since retail's `memset(&aasworld, 0, 0x2A4)` at 0x1000EE30 cannot reach them (they live below aasworld's 0x100667e0 base). If any other reset path is added later, it must not touch them either. Keeping the `g_retail_frame_routing_updates = 0` line here is safe and matches retail: AAS_StartFrame zeroes data_10066a70 at 0x1000e03a, i.e. *after* AAS_ContinueInit at 0x1000e030, so any zeroing that happens inside an AAS load is immediately re-established by the frame reset before the counter is next read. Update the parity tests accordingly: tests/parity/test_aas_debug.c currently relies on the per-load reset (e.g. the AAS_RetailAreaCacheUpdateCount()==1 expectations around lines 2754-2768 and 3064). Those tests should either explicitly re-zero the statics through a test-only hook or express their assertions as deltas, and one should be extended to cover the new invariant: load an AAS, drive some routing, load a second AAS, and assert the counters have grown rather than restarted from 0. tests/parity/test_bot_interface.c:11647-11648 ("0 area/portal cache updates") stays valid only as the first observation in a fresh process.

## `src/botlib/aas/aas_sound.c`

### LOW Sound subsystem setup reads its libvars in the wrong order (max_soundinfo before max_aassounds)

- status: **applied**
- retail: `sub_1001D260 (AAS_SetupSound)` @ `0x1001d260`
- HLIL: 24487-24494
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_sound.c:497-504
- ours: `src/botlib/aas/aas_sound.c:487`

**Divergence.** `AAS_SoundSubsystem_Init` (src/botlib/aas/aas_sound.c:480) performs retail's sound-setup steps in the wrong order. Retail `AAS_SetupSound` / sub_1001D260 (0x1001d260, HLIL L24487-24490) is `j_sub_1001cab0(); return j_sub_1001c760(j_sub_10038a60("soundconfig", "sounds.c"))`, giving the order: 1. `max_aassounds` read + clamp to [0, 0x10000] + `GetMemory(n * 0x34)` sound heap + free-list threading (sub_1001CAB0, HLIL L24089-24131, deciding line `1001cac9 int32_t esi = __ftol(x87control, j_sub_10038a90("max_aassounds", &data_1005c268))`), 2. `LibVarString("soundconfig", "sounds.c")`, 3. `max_soundinfo` read + clamp to [0, 0xffff] + `GetClearedMemory(n * 0xb0)` info array, then parse (sub_1001C760, HLIL L23970-23985, deciding line `1001c781 int32_t ebx = __ftol(x87control, j_sub_10038a90("max_soundinfo", &data_1005c268))`). We do it as max_soundinfo (line 487) -> max_aassounds (line 497) -> soundconfig (line 519). Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_aas_sound.c:497-504. Additionally, retail frees each old buffer immediately before its own reallocation (`1001cafb`/`1001cb05` inside sub_1001CAB0; `1001c7b3`/`1001c7bd` inside sub_1001C760), whereas we free all three buffers up front at lines 482-484. Observable delta: when both libvars are set out of range, retail prints `max_aassounds out of range [0, 65536]` and calls `LibVarSet("max_aassounds", "256")` *before* the corresponding `max_soundinfo` pair; we emit them reversed. Allocation order of the two heaps is likewise swapped. Post-init state is otherwise identical, and the claim's "libvar list ordering" argument does not hold in our build because src/q2bridge/bridge_config.c:116-118 already creates all three libvars during `BridgeConfig_Init` before AAS setup runs.

**Failure scenario.** Set both `max_soundinfo 99999` and `max_aassounds 99999`. Retail prints `max_aassounds out of range [0, 65536]` first and then `max_soundinfo out of range [0, 65535]`, and calls LibVarSet in that order; we print and reset them the other way round. LibVarValue also creates the libvar on first touch, so the botlib libvar list ends up in a different order than retail's.

**Fix.** Reorder `AAS_SoundSubsystem_Init` (src/botlib/aas/aas_sound.c:480) to retail's sequence, and pair each free with its own reallocation instead of freeing everything up front: 1. Drop the blanket `AAS_Sound_FreeInfos()` / `AAS_Sound_FreeSoundEvents()` from the top (keep `AAS_Sound_FreeSoundSummaries()` and the `initialised = false` reset, which are our own derived-view bookkeeping). 2. `max_aassounds = AAS_Sound_ReadCapacityLibVar("max_aassounds", "256", 65536, 256)`; free the old sound-event heap; `GetMemory(count * sizeof(aas_sound_event_t))`; `AAS_Sound_InitSoundHeap()`. 3. `const char *requested = LibVarString("soundconfig", "sounds.c");` 4. `max_soundinfo = AAS_Sound_ReadCapacityLibVar("max_soundinfo", "256", 65535, 256)`; free the old info array; allocate it (retail uses GetClearedMemory at 0x1001c7db). 5. Then resolve/parse the config exactly as today. Leave the bounds, the 256 fallback, and the "256" default string unchanged — those already match retail.

## `src/botlib/ai/ai_weapon.c`

### MEDIUM LoadWeaponConfig emits the wrong file-error diagnostics (no "couldn't find", no "counldn't load" typo)

- status: **applied**
- retail: `LoadWeaponConfig` @ `0x10034bb0`
- HLIL: 41085-41095
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai_weap.c:137-147
- ours: `src/botlib/ai/ai_weapon.c:428`

**Divergence.** LoadWeaponConfig (0x10034bb0) file-error diagnostics diverge three ways, not one. Retail (HLIL 41085-41095, ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai_weap.c:137-147) has two distinct PRT_ERROR emissions, both taking the strncpy'd requested filename `&var_558` (HLIL 41083): - resolver miss -> `data_10063fe8(3, "couldn't find %s\n", &var_558)`; return 0 - load failure -> `data_10063fe8(3, "counldn't load %s\n", &var_558)`; return 0 (retail's own misspelling) and emits nothing else on either path. The caller BotSetupWeaponAI then prints PRT_FATAL "couldn't load the weapon config\n" (HLIL 41546). Our reconstruction diverges in three ways: 1. src/botlib/ai/ai_weapon.c:109-142 — AI_Weapon_OpenSource returns NULL silently for both the BotLib_ResolveAssetPath failure and the PC_LoadSourceFile failure, so the two retail cases are merged before the caller can tell them apart. 2. src/botlib/ai/ai_weapon.c:428 — a single invented literal "couldn't load %s\n" replaces both retail strings; neither "couldn't find" nor the "counldn't load" typo is ever printed. 3. src/botlib/ai/ai_weapon.c:206-215 (AI_Weapon_LoadFailed, reached from lines 429, 439, 447, 454) — an extra PRT_ERROR "couldn't load weapon config %s\n" is printed on every failure path. Retail's LoadWeaponConfig never emits that string; its only retail home is BotLoadWeaponWeights @ 0x10035368 at PRT_FATAL (HLIL 41394), which we already reproduce correctly at src/botlib/ai_weapon/bot_weapon.c:549. Additionally the format argument is wrong: line 427 prefers `resolved_path`, which BotLib_ResolveAssetPath fills with the last search-path candidate on failure (src/botlib/common/l_assets.c:1141-1143), whereas retail always prints the bare requested name. Failure scenario — weapons.c absent from the pak/search path: retail console: `couldn't find weapons.c` (PRT_ERROR), then `couldn't load the weapon config` (PRT_FATAL) ours: `couldn't load botfiles/weapons.c` (PRT_ERROR), `couldn't load weapon config weapons.c` (PRT_ERROR), then `couldn't load the weapon config` (PRT_FATAL) Every sibling loader in our tree (aas_sound.c:522/530, ai_character.c:1278/1286, ai_chat.c:7197/7204, bot_goal.c:3145/3152, ai_weight.c:817/824) already reproduces the retail pair, so ai_weapon.c is the sole outlier. Confidence: high. Diagnostic-only — no state, return value, or allocation behaviour changes.

**Failure scenario.** weapons.c is missing from the pak/search path. Retail prints `couldn't find weapons.c` (PRT_ERROR) then `couldn't load the weapon config` (PRT_FATAL from BotSetupWeaponAI). We print `couldn't load weapons.c` plus `couldn't load weapon config weapons.c` plus the fatal — a different, longer console transcript for the single most common bot-install failure.

**Fix.** Split AI_Weapon_OpenSource (src/botlib/ai/ai_weapon.c:109) into a resolve step and a load step so the two retail branches stay distinguishable, and print from the branch that failed: - On BotLib_ResolveAssetPath failure: `BotLib_Print(PRT_ERROR, "couldn't find %s\n", requested);` and return NULL. - On PC_LoadSourceFile failure: `BotLib_Print(PRT_ERROR, "counldn't load %s\n", requested);` (preserve retail's misspelling verbatim) and return NULL. Both must take the strncpy'd requested name — retail passes `&var_558` (HLIL 41083/41086/41094), never the resolved candidate. Keep the existing AI_WEAPON_MAX_PATH copy of the request for this purpose rather than reusing `resolved_path`, whose contents on failure are a search-path candidate (src/botlib/common/l_assets.c:1141-1143). Then delete the caller-side print at src/botlib/ai/ai_weapon.c:428 (the source==NULL branch becomes a bare cleanup + return), and remove the `BotLib_Print(PRT_ERROR, "couldn't load weapon config %s\n", ...)` from AI_Weapon_LoadFailed (src/botlib/ai/ai_weapon.c:211-213) so it only frees artifacts and returns NULL — retail's LoadWeaponConfig prints nothing extra on any failure path. Leave the PRT_FATAL copy of that string in BotLoadWeaponWeights (src/botlib/ai_weapon/bot_weapon.c:549) untouched; it is the correct retail home (0x10035368). The invented alloc-failure diagnostic at src/botlib/ai/ai_weapon.c:415-417 has no retail counterpart either (retail's GetClearedMemory at HLIL 41097 is unchecked); if it is kept as a host-portability guard it should not reuse a retail string — reword it to the bracketed `[ai_weapon]` form already used at line 453/161 so it cannot be mistaken for retail output. While in this routine, note but do not necessarily change: our parse/fixup diagnostics pass `log_path` (resolved) where retail passes the requested `&var_558` (HLIL 41173, 41238, 41249, 41310), and retail's success print has a pak variant `"loaded %s\%s\n"` (HLIL 41329) alongside `"loaded %s\n"` (HLIL 41332). Those are separate findings.

### LOW LoadWeaponConfig prints an invented "couldn't load weapon config %s" on every parse/fixup failure

- status: **applied**
- retail: `LoadWeaponConfig` @ `0x10034bb0`
- HLIL: 41172-41211, 41235-41251, 41307-41312
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai_weap.c:163-233
- ours: `src/botlib/ai/ai_weapon.c:211`

**Divergence.** LoadWeaponConfig (retail sub_10034bb0, HLIL 41056-41333) prints exactly one diagnostic per failure exit and never prints "couldn't load weapon config %s". Its exits are: 10034c99 "couldn't find %s\n" and 10034ce1 "counldn't load %s\n" (both before the config is allocated, so no frees); 10034efa "more than %d weapons defined in %s"; 10034f45 "more than %d projectiles defined in %s"; 10034f7b "unknown definition %s in %s\n"; 10034f1d, the ReadStructure failure, which prints nothing at all -- each of these five followed only by j_sub_100390b0(config) + j_sub_1003e000(source) + return 0; and the shared tail 100350d3, reached by "weapon %d has no name in %s\n" (10035086), "weapon %s has no projectile in %s" (100350a9) and "weapon %s uses undefined projectile in %s" (100350cc), which calls j_sub_100390b0(config) only because the source was already freed at 10034e97. That is seven printed messages across six printing exits plus one deliberately silent exit -- not six. Our AI_Weapon_LoadFailed (src/botlib/ai/ai_weapon.c:206-215) appends a retail-never-emitted PRT_ERROR line "couldn't load weapon config %s\n" after the per-path diagnostic, and it is reached from all four call sites in AI_LoadWeaponLibrary: line 429 (source open failed), 439 (parse failed), 447 (fixup failed) and 454 (library-wrapper allocation failed). The same invented string is hardcoded a second time at ai_weapon.c:415-418 on the config-allocation guard, so the claim's single-site framing is incomplete. In retail that string exists exactly once, at HLIL 41394 / 10035368 inside sub_10035340 (BotLoadWeaponWeights), at severity 4 (PRT_FATAL), formatted against arg2 -- the weight filename. Our reproduction of that one, at src/botlib/ai_weapon/bot_weapon.c:549-551, is correct and must be left alone. The separate non-format string "couldn't load the weapon config\n" belongs to the setup routine at 100356a8 and is correctly reproduced at src/botlib/ai_weapon/bot_weapon.c:311. Failure scenario holds as stated: with a stray top-level token in weapons.c, AI_Weapon_ReadConfigDefinitions (ai_weapon.c:303-307) prints "unknown definition weaponinf in weapons.c" and returns false, then line 439 emits a second line retail never produces. Everything else on these paths -- return value, freed memory, state writes -- already matches retail; the divergence is confined to console/log text on error paths, which is why this is low severity rather than a behavioural break.

**Failure scenario.** weapons.c contains a stray token, e.g. `weaponinf { ... }`. Retail prints `unknown definition weaponinf in weapons.c` and nothing more. We print that plus `couldn't load weapon config weapons.c`, so log-scraping tools and the console see a message retail attributes to a different subsystem and severity.

**Fix.** Two edits, both in src/botlib/ai/ai_weapon.c: 1. Delete the BotLib_Print call at lines 211-213 so AI_Weapon_LoadFailed only frees the source and the config and returns NULL. The `requested` parameter then becomes unused -- drop it from the signature and from the four call sites at lines 429, 439, 447 and 454, or simply have those sites call AI_Weapon_FreeLoadArtifacts directly and return NULL, which removes the helper entirely. Keep every per-path diagnostic in AI_Weapon_ReadConfigDefinitions (lines 254, 265, 303) and AI_Weapon_FixupDefinitions (lines 355, 364, 375) exactly as they are -- those already match retail, including the silent ReadStructure failure at lines 274-277 and 295-298 that mirrors 10034f1d. 2. Delete the duplicate invented print at lines 415-418 on the AI_Weapon_AllocateConfig failure guard. Retail's GetClearedMemory never returns NULL (it hard-errors), so that path is synthetic; if the guard is kept for host safety it must stay silent rather than emit a string retail reserves for BotLoadWeaponWeights. Do not touch src/botlib/ai_weapon/bot_weapon.c:549-551 (correct PRT_FATAL reproduction of 10035368) or :311 (correct reproduction of 100356a8). No test updates are needed: the "couldn't load weapon config \n" assertions at tests/ai/test_ai_character.c:2266, 2271, 2296 and 2300 all specify PRT_FATAL and originate in the weights loader, not this routine, so they are unaffected. Re-run tests/ai/test_ai_weapon.c after the change to confirm none of its cases scraped the removed PRT_ERROR line. Optionally, in the same edit, fix the adjacent path at line 428: retail distinguishes "couldn't find %s\n" (10034c99, asset not found) from the misspelled "counldn't load %s\n" (10034ce1, LoadSourceFile rejected the resolved file), while we print "couldn't load %s\n" for both. Every sibling loader already reproduces the pair (src/botlib/ai_weight/ai_weight.c:817/824, src/botlib/ai_goal/bot_goal.c:3145/3152, src/botlib/aas/aas_sound.c:522/530, src/botlib/ai/ai_character.c:1278/1286); mirror that structure here by having AI_Weapon_OpenSource report which of the two stages failed.

### LOW LoadWeaponConfig never emits the pak-relative "loaded %s\\%s" success message

- status: **applied**
- retail: `LoadWeaponConfig` @ `0x10034bb0`
- HLIL: 41328-41333
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai_weap.c:238-241
- ours: `src/botlib/ai/ai_weapon.c:462`

**Divergence.** src/botlib/ai/ai_weapon.c:462 diverges from retail LoadWeaponConfig (0x10034bb0, HLIL 41328-41333) in BOTH of retail's success-print branches, not just the pak one. Retail ends with a two-way branch on file_ref.filelen (var_4c4; struct base -0x4c8, layout {int fileofs; int filelen; char path[144];} per ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_utils.h:18-23, confirmed by 10034cc7 LoadSourceFile(&var_4c0, fileofs, filelen)): 1003504d if (var_4c4 != 0) 1003506a Print(PRT_MESSAGE, "loaded %s\%s\n", &var_4c0 /* pak path */, arg1 /* caller's filename */) 100350fb Print(PRT_MESSAGE, "loaded %s\n", &var_558 /* strncpy'd request name */) Ref be_ai_weap.c:238-241 matches verbatim. We print unconditionally `BotLib_Print(PRT_MESSAGE, "loaded %s\n", library->source_path)`, where source_path is the *resolved on-disk path* from the path-only BotLib_ResolveAssetPath (AI_Weapon_OpenSource, ai_weapon.c:109-142), which discards the source_path/pak_entry_length that BotLib_ResolveAssetPathDetailed already computes. Consequences: 1. Pak case (retail filelen != 0): retail prints `loaded pak0.pak\weapons.c`; we print the extracted cache-file path produced by BotLib_ExtractPakEntry (l_assets.c:790-804), with no container name — the operator cannot tell the config came from a pak. 2. Loose-file case (retail filelen == 0): retail prints the requested name, e.g. `loaded weapons.c`; we print the fully-resolved path, e.g. `loaded <root>/baseq2/weapons.c`. So we diverge on EVERY load, not only pak loads. Not masked by a caller: the only caller, BotSetupWeaponAI (src/botlib/ai_weapon/bot_weapon.c:299-316), emits nothing on the success path. ai_weapon.c is the last site in the tree still on the single-branch form; aas_sound.c:550, ai_chat.c:7227, bot_goal.c:3219, ai_weight.c:847 and aas_map.c:5916 all already implement the retail two-branch form. Diagnostics-only: no state, parse, or return-value effect.

**Failure scenario.** weapons.c is loaded out of pak0.pak. Retail prints `loaded pak0.pak\weapons.c`; we print `loaded <resolved absolute-or-relative path>` with no container name, so the operator cannot tell the config came from a pak.

**Fix.** In AI_LoadWeaponLibrary (src/botlib/ai/ai_weapon.c), switch the resolution to the detailed resolver and reproduce both retail branches, printing the REQUESTED name (not the resolved path) in each. 1. Change AI_Weapon_OpenSource to take a `botlib_asset_resolution_t *` out-param and use BotLib_ResolveAssetPathDetailed(requested, NULL, resolution) instead of BotLib_ResolveAssetPath, then PC_LoadSourceFile on resolution->resolved_path. Keep the existing failure path (which prints "couldn't load %s\n" with resolution->resolved_path falling back to `requested`) unchanged — that already matches retail 10034ce1. 2. Replace line 462 with the retail two-branch form, mirroring aas_sound.c:550-559: /* * Retail LoadWeaponConfig 0x10034bb0 branches on file_ref.filelen: * 1003504d if (var_4c4 != 0) * 1003506a Print(1, "loaded %s\%s\n", &var_4c0, arg1) * 100350fb Print(1, "loaded %s\n", &var_558) * Both arms report the REQUESTED name, never the resolved path. */ if (resolution.pak_entry_length != 0) { BotLib_Print(PRT_MESSAGE, "loaded %s\\%s\n", resolution.source_path, requested); } else { BotLib_Print(PRT_MESSAGE, "loaded %s\n", requested); } Note the second arm must use `requested` (retail's var_558 = strncpy of arg1), NOT library->source_path / log_path — otherwise the loose-file case stays divergent. library->source_path may keep holding the resolved path for internal bookkeeping; only the printed text changes. Leave the internal `log_path` used by the "weapon %d has no name in %s\n" / "unknown definition %s in %s\n" errors alone unless separately audited — retail passes its `path` local (var_558) there too, which is a distinct question from this finding. House rules: tabs for indentation; edit ai_weapon.c in place, no new source file.

## `src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c`

### MEDIUM Retail's x87 random fraction never reaches 1.0; our float helper collapses it to exactly 1.0f, shifting every count-based pick at the RNG maximum

- status: **applied**
- retail: `RandomString / BotChooseInitialChatMessage / BotReplyChat` @ `0x1002bdd0, 0x1002e3b0, 0x1002e7d0`
- HLIL: 34477-34479 (1002be32), 36538-36541 (1002e46e), 36796-36800 (1002e93a)
- ours: `src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:277`

**Divergence.** Retail's three count-based chat selectors (RandomString 0x1002bdd0 @ HLIL 34477-34479, BotChooseInitialChatMessage 0x1002e3b0 @ 36538-36541, BotReplyChat 0x1002e7d0 @ 36796-36800) evaluate `rand()&0x7fff` * 3.05185094e-05f * count entirely in x87 (`float.t`/`fconvert.t`, never `fconvert.s`), so with the constant being exactly 2^-15+2^-30 the maximum fraction is 1 - 2^-30 and the truncated index is always <= count-1. Our `BotChat_RetailRandomFloat()` (src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:275-278) returns `float`, which rounds 1 - 2^-30 to exactly 1.0f (verified by compiling the expression), and every consumer then multiplies in single precision, yielding index == count. Affected call sites: ai_chat.c:1069 (BotChat_SelectRandomFromTable), :1093 (BotChat_SelectRandomFromStateTable), :2167 (BotChat_ChooseInitialTemplate), :4817 (BotChat_SelectWeightedSynonymFromGroup), :5402 (BotChat_SelectIndex, reached from :684 and :5433/:5446), :8895 (BotChat_SelectRetailReplyResponse). Corrections to the original wording: (a) at n = count-1 retail's walk returns the LAST node of the linked list, not the head (the original text said both); in our reversed-array storage that is entries[0]. (b) r == 32767 is not the only diverging sample — a full sweep of r in [0,0x7fff] against counts 1..64 shows 36 additional low-15-bit values (e.g. 4681, 9362, 14043, 1057, 2114, ...) where our single-precision product truncates differently from the x87 chain for at least one count, so the fix must restore precision for the whole chain rather than special-case the maximum. (c) the claim omitted BotChat_SelectRandomFromTable at :1069 and the BotChat_SelectRecentIndex path at :5446. Observable effects at the max sample: a \001r<name>\001 expansion drops the whole chat message and prints PRT_ERROR "BotConstructChat: unknown random string %s" that retail never emits; an all-available reply rule is silently skipped and its priority never recorded; an initial-chat type speaks nothing; a synonym group returns NULL instead of its last phrase.

**Failure scenario.** Any frame where `rand() & 0x7fff == 32767` (1 in 32768 per expansion). Expanding a template containing a \001r<name>\001 random reference: retail RandomString returns the head entry of the list (n = numstrings-1 walks to the last node) and the chat is emitted; ours computes index == entry_count, hits `index >= table->entry_count` and returns NULL, so BotChat_ExpandChatMessageOnce prints "BotConstructChat: unknown random string %s" and drops the whole message. Same roll in BotReplyChat: retail selects the last reachable chatmessage, ours runs `--num < 0` one iteration short of the list end, BotChat_SelectRetailReplyResponse returns 0 and the reply rule is skipped entirely (no reply sent, and the rule's priority is never recorded). Same roll in BotChooseInitialChatMessage: retail returns a line, ours returns NULL and no initial chat is spoken.

**Fix.** Keep the whole rand*const*count chain at >= double precision up to the truncation, matching retail's x87 evaluation. Change the helper to `static double BotChat_RetailRandomFloat(void) { return (double)(rand() & 0x7fff) * (double)3.05185094e-05f; }` (keep the constant as the float literal so it stays exactly 2^-15+2^-30, then widen), and change each consumer to multiply in double before the integer cast: ai_chat.c:1069 and :1093 `(size_t)(BotChat_RetailRandomFloat() * (double)table->entry_count)`, :2167 `(int)(BotChat_RetailRandomFloat() * (double)available_count)`, :5402 `(size_t)(BotChat_RetailRandomFloat() * (double)count)`, :8895 `(int)(BotChat_RetailRandomFloat() * (double)available_count)`, and :4817 make both `roll` and `cumulative` double so the endpoint comparison `roll < cumulative` still selects the last phrase. Do not add a clamp — the correct precision makes index <= count-1 by construction, and the existing `index >= entry_count` guards then become genuinely unreachable rather than load-bearing. Also update the now-false comments that encode the wrong model: ai_chat.c:270-272 ("A maximum rand sample is deliberately 1.0f, so count-based retail selections can miss their list endpoint"), :8872-8873 ("the RNG endpoint can miss"), and :4833 ("HOST SAFETY: retail dereferences NULL on the inclusive endpoint") — retail's endpoint is always reachable. Update the test helper tests/chat/test_bot_chat.c:163 (`seed_retail_chat_ordinal`) to compute its predicted ordinal with the same double-precision chain, otherwise its seed search can disagree with production for the ~37 diverging low-15-bit samples. The same single-precision collapse exists verbatim at src/botlib/ai/ai_dm.c:273, src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:3376, tests/ai/test_ai_dm.c:228, and — as a real count-based index — at src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:3081 (AAS_RandomGoalStartArea, whose comment repeats the same "numareas-inclusive" misconception); those are separate sites but should be audited with the same reasoning.

### MEDIUM Random-string block accepts an empty { } silently and aborts the whole file on a bad entry, where retail diagnoses and continues

- status: **applied**
- retail: `BotLoadRandomStrings` @ `0x1002b990`
- HLIL: 34337-34340 (1002bb1d: if (PC_ExpectTokenType(esi_1, 1, 0, &var_430) != 0) do {...}) and 34399-34403 (1002bbdf/1002bbe9 loop exit, then 1002bbf8 PC_ReadTokenHandle continues the outer loop)
- ours: `src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:6468`

**Divergence.** BotChat_ParseRandomStringBlock (src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:6461-6499) has the wrong loop shape relative to BotLoadRandomStrings @ 0x1002b990. Retail (HLIL 34337 `if (j_sub_1003d740(esi_1, 1, 0, &var_430) != 0)` = PC_ExpectTokenType(source, TT_STRING, 0, token); HLIL 34393 the only PC_CheckTokenString("}") AFTER an entry is stored; HLIL 34399-34403 the loop-exit falling into 1002bbf8 PC_ReadTokenHandle; ref be_ai_chat.c:654) has no `}` check before the first element and treats a failed PC_ExpectTokenType as "stop this table, keep reading the file". Two observable consequences: (1) DIAGNOSTIC LOSS, always reproducible: for `misc0 = { }` and for a trailing comma `misc0 = { "a", }`, retail's PC_ExpectTokenType consumes the `}` and prints `expected a string, found }` (SourceError, l_precomp.c:2382) then resumes the outer table loop; ours takes the loop-top PS_CheckTokenString(script,"}") at line 6468/6490 and returns 1 with no message. The loaded data is identical in both cases (retail also registers the zero-entry table, since the randomlist node is created before the block is parsed), so this is a missing-diagnostic divergence only. (2) DATA LOSS on resync, narrower: when the element position holds a token that is neither `}` nor a string, ours returns 0 at line 6474-6477, which BotChat_ParseSetupRandomScript (7121) turns into BotChat_FreeRandomTables, discarding every table parsed so far; retail breaks the inner loop and continues at 1002bbf8. For most such inputs retail also aborts a token later with `unknown random %s`, so the difference only materialises when the stream resyncs -- most realistically a truncated rnd.c such as `misc0 = { "a",` at EOF, where retail prints `couldn't read expected token`, completes both passes and RETURNS the list containing misc0, while ours frees every random table. The claim's statement that a trailing comma makes ours print the expect error and discard all tables is incorrect: our loop-top `}` check accepts that input silently.

**Failure scenario.** A rnd.c containing `misc0 = { }` followed by valid tables. Retail prints `rnd.c(N): expected string, found }` and still loads every later table. Ours registers a zero-entry `misc0` with no warning; and for the sibling case `misc0 = { "a", }` (trailing comma) ours prints the expect error and then throws away ALL random tables, so every \001r reference in ichat/rchat fails with "BotConstructChat: unknown random string" and the bots stop talking entirely.

**Fix.** Rewrite BotChat_ParseRandomStringBlock to retail's shape and delete the loop-top `}` check entirely: while (PS_ExpectTokenType(script, TT_STRING, 0, &token)) { /* store the entry (StripDoubleQuotes semantics unchanged) */ if (!add_entry(table, &token)) return 0; if (PS_CheckTokenString(script, "}")) break; /* 1002bbba */ if (!PS_ExpectTokenString(script, ",")) return 0; /* 1002bbcc -> label_1002bca7 */ } return 1; /* loop exit via failed PS_ExpectTokenType is NOT a file-level error */ The single behavioural change is that exiting the loop through a failed PS_ExpectTokenType must return 1 (success) so BotChat_ParseRandomStringTables keeps its `while (PS_ReadToken(...))` running -- no second return code is needed, because PS_ExpectTokenType has already consumed the offending token and emitted its own ScriptError, exactly as retail does. Only PS_ExpectTokenString(",") failure keeps the whole-file abort (return 0 -> BotChat_FreeRandomTables), which matches retail's FreeSource/return NULL at 1002bca7. No change is needed in BotChat_ParseRandomStringTables or BotChat_ParseSetupRandomScript.

### MEDIUM "loaded <file>" is printed even when the asset parse failed; all four retail loaders return before that print on any parse error

- status: **applied**
- retail: `BotLoadSynonyms / BotLoadRandomStrings / BotLoadMatchTemplates / BotLoadReplyChat` @ `0x1002b110, 0x1002b990, 0x1002c410, 0x1002d270`
- HLIL: 34783-34787 vs 34891-34896 (1002c6dc returns 0; the print is at 1002c687/1002c70c); same shape at 33884-33886 vs 34130-34135, 34265-34268 vs 34415-34420, 35598-35602 vs 35770-35773
- ours: `src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:7224`

**Divergence.** src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:7224-7234 (BotChat_LoadSetupScript) emits the retail `"loaded %s\n"` / `"loaded %s\%s\n"` PRT_MESSAGE unconditionally, including when the parser callback reported failure. In retail all four setup loaders -- BotLoadSynonyms 0x1002b110, BotLoadRandomStrings 0x1002b990, BotLoadMatchTemplates 0x1002c410, BotLoadReplyChat 0x1002d270 -- reach that print only on the fall-through path after the parse loop terminates normally; every SourceError branch does SourceError -> free partial list -> FreeSource -> `return 0` and never prints (HLIL 1002c6d5-1002c6fc and 1002c6a5-1002c6bf vs 1002c66d/1002c687/1002c70c; 1002b546/1002b562/1002b581/1002b5f8/1002b619 vs 1002b4ec/1002b63d/1002b506; 1002bc84/1002bca7 vs 1002bc29/1002bccb/1002bc43; 1002d63a/1002d663 vs 1002d61a/1002d685/1002d628; corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai_chat.c:963-966 vs :1012-1017). Correction to the original wording: retail's fall-through is NOT the same as "the loader returned a non-NULL list". A file that yields no tokens at all (empty match.c) falls through with `result == nullptr` and still prints "loaded" (HLIL 1002c499 / 1002c4ad / 1002c65f). Our parsers already return 1 in exactly that case (BotChat_ParseMatchScript:6918), so `parsed != 0` is a precise match for retail's print set -- the guard must be on `parsed`, not on a non-NULL result. Failure scenario: ship a match.c whose first token is not an integer. Retail prints only `match.c(1): expected integer, found ...` and leaves matchtemplates NULL. Ours prints that SourceError and then `loaded match.c`, so the log claims a successful load for an asset BotChat_FreeMatchContexts just discarded. Same for a syn.c with `too many }`, an rnd.c with `unknown random ...`, and an rchat.c with `expected [, found ...`. Diagnostic-only: no bot state differs, but the console log is misleading and contains a message retail never emits for that input.

**Failure scenario.** Ship a `match.c` whose first token is not an integer (or a `syn.c` with `too many }`). Retail prints only `match.c(1): expected integer, found ...` and matchtemplates stays NULL. Ours prints the same SourceError and then `loaded match.c`, so the console log claims the file loaded while BotChat_FreeMatchContexts has just thrown the templates away — a server operator reading the log cannot tell a broken asset from a good one.

**Fix.** In BotChat_LoadSetupScript (src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c), wrap the trailing PRT_MESSAGE block in `if (parsed)`, leaving PS_FreeScript/PC_FreeSource ahead of it (retail frees the source at 1002c65f before printing at 1002c687/1002c70c), and replace the now-wrong comment at :7224: const int parsed = parser(state, script); PS_FreeScript(script); PC_FreeSource(source); /* Retail prints only on the fall-through after the parse loop ends; every SourceError branch returns 0 first (1002c6d5, 1002c6a5). */ if (parsed) { if (resolution.pak_entry_length != 0) { BotLib_Print(PRT_MESSAGE, "loaded %s\\%s\n", resolution.source_path, file_name); } else { BotLib_Print(PRT_MESSAGE, "loaded %s\n", file_name); } } return parsed; Do not change the guard to a non-NULL-result test: an asset that parses to an empty list must still print (retail 1002c499 -> 1002c65f -> 1002c70c), and our parsers already return 1 there. Out of scope but adjacent, do not let this edit obscure it: for rchat retail prints "loaded" BEFORE BotCheckReplyChatIntegrety (HLIL 1002d61a -> 1002d68f -> 1002d699 "no rchats\n"), whereas BotChat_ParseSetupReplyScript (:7154-7165) runs BotChat_CheckReplyChatIntegrity inside the parser, i.e. before the print. Guarding the print does not fix that log-ordering difference and does not make it worse; it should be filed separately.

### LOW Synonym loader aborts on a top-level token retail silently ignores

- status: **applied**
- retail: `BotLoadSynonyms` @ `0x1002b110`
- HLIL: 33872-33892 and 34111-34117 (1002d1d9: if (var_b0 == 3) ... 1002b22f: else if (var_b0 == 5) ... falls straight to the next PC_ReadTokenHandle at 1002b4b5 with no else branch)
- ours: `src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:5726`

**Divergence.** BotLoadSynonyms (0x1002b110) dispatches top-level tokens with exactly two arms and no else: `token.type == 3` (TT_NUMBER, HLIL 33875-33891 / 1002b1d9-1002b21b) pushes `token.intvalue` onto the context stack with no integer-subtype test and then expects "{", and `token.type == 5` (TT_PUNCTUATION, HLIL 33892-34109 / 1002b22f) handles "}" / "[" and raises SourceError("unexpected %s") only for other punctuation (1002b5e6). Any other token type (TT_STRING, TT_LITERAL, TT_NAME) falls through to the next PC_ReadTokenHandle at 1002b4b5 (HLIL 34113) and is silently ignored. Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai_chat.c:377-486. Our BotChat_ParseSynonymContextsFromScript diverges twice: src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:5726 turns any non-punctuation token into ScriptError("unexpected %s") + return 0, and ai_chat.c:5708 additionally requires BotChat_NumberTokenIsInteger before a TT_NUMBER is treated as a context, so a float number token (which retail accepts, using its intvalue) also falls into that error path. On failure BotChat_ParseSetupSynonymScript (ai_chat.c:7104) frees every parsed context, so the bot loses all synonym replacement. Impact is narrower than claimed. The claim's own example — an unexpanded CONTEXT_FOO before a block — fails in retail too: retail skips the TT_NAME, then hits `{`, which is punctuation that is neither `}` nor `[`, so 1002b5e6 fires with "unexpected {". Only the diagnostic text differs there. A genuine load-outcome divergence requires a non-punctuation token that is not followed by an offending punctuator (a stray quoted string or literal at file scope, a bare identifier followed by a valid context number or at EOF) or a non-integer number before `{`. The stock dev_tools/assets/syn.c, syn.h and game.h contain none of these, so shipped data parses identically; the defect only surfaces on a hand-edited or mod-supplied syn.c reached through the synfile libvar.

**Failure scenario.** A syn.c that leaves a bare identifier or quoted string at file scope — e.g. an unexpanded `CONTEXT_FOO` macro from a mod-supplied syn.h, or a stray `"note"` outside any block. Retail skips it and loads every synonym list in the file; ours prints `syn.c(N): unexpected CONTEXT_FOO`, returns 0, BotChat_ParseSetupSynonymScript frees the whole table, and the bot loses all synonym replacement for the session (BotReplaceSynonyms and BotReplaceWeightedSynonyms become no-ops).

**Fix.** In the loop at src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:5706: 1. Change the context arm at :5708 to `if (token.type == TT_NUMBER)` (drop the BotChat_NumberTokenIsInteger precondition) and use the token's intvalue as the pushed value rather than BotChat_NumberTokenValue — retail does `contextstack[level] = token.intvalue; context |= token.intvalue`, and the strtoul path in BotChat_NumberTokenValue would yield 1 for "1e3" where retail yields 1000. 2. Replace the `if (token.type != TT_PUNCTUATION) { ScriptError("unexpected %s"); return 0; }` block at :5726 with a plain `continue`, so TT_STRING / TT_LITERAL / TT_NAME are skipped exactly as retail skips them (fall-through to the next PC_ReadTokenHandle at 1002b4b5). 3. Keep the existing "unexpected %s" ScriptError at :5745 for punctuation that is neither "}" nor "[" — that is the only place retail raises it (1002b5e6). Leave the "too many }" (1002b581), "more than 32 context levels" (1002b546) and trailing "missing }" (1002b619) paths unchanged. Note the context-stack bound at :5710 is already correct (retail writes contextstack[31] then errors on level==32; ours errors on the same 32nd push), so do not touch it.

### LOW Reply-chat priority keeps the fractional token value; retail stores (float)token.intvalue

- status: **applied**
- retail: `BotLoadReplyChat` @ `0x1002d270`
- HLIL: 35698-35702 (1002d4dc: PC_ExpectTokenString "=" + PC_ExpectTokenType(3,0); 1002d52f: result_1[1] = fconvert.s(float.t(var_28.q)) where var_28 is token.intvalue)
- ours: `src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:6408`

**Divergence.** BotLoadReplyChat @ 0x1002d270 (HLIL 35698-35702) reads the reply weight with PC_ExpectTokenType(source, TT_NUMBER, 0, &token) — which accepts a float literal — and then stores the *truncated unsigned integer* value: at 1002d516-1002d52f the retail code does `mov edx,[token+0x408]` (token.intvalue) / `mov [esp+0x18],esi` (esi=0) / `fild qword` / `fstp dword [ebp+4]`, i.e. `rc->priority = (float)(unsigned long)token.intvalue`. For a TT_FLOAT token, NumberValue has already set intvalue = (unsigned)floatvalue, so the fraction is discarded at load time. Our BotChat_ParseReplyBlock (src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:6408) captures `token.floatvalue` and at :6420 assigns it to `rule->priority`, keeping the fraction. This *overwrites* the already-correct truncated value that BotChat_AddReplyRule wrote at :2747 (`rule->priority = (float)reply_context;`). The selection loop is not the problem — ours already matches retail (int-tracked best: our :8958/:8971 vs retail 1002e8e8/1002e8f4/1002e966), and so does traversal order. Only the stored value diverges, which changes which reply is spoken for any mod rchat.c using a fractional priority (e.g. a `] = 0.5` rule can never fire in retail but fires for us; a `] = 2.5` rule loses to a later-in-file `] = 2` rule in retail but wins for us). Stock rchat.c uses only integer priorities 0-8, so this is latent for shipped assets.

**Failure scenario.** An rchat.c rule written `["hello"] = 2.5` competing with a later rule at `] = 2`. Retail truncates 2.5 to 2.0, so the 2.5 rule is NOT preferred over an already-selected priority-2 rule (`2.0 < 2.0` is false) and the priority-2 rule's response is spoken. Ours keeps 2.5, the comparison `2.5 <= 2` fails, and the 2.5 rule wins — a different reply line. (The stock dev_tools/assets/rchat.c uses only integer priorities 0-8, so this is latent for shipped assets and live for any mod-authored rchat.c.)

**Fix.** In src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c BotChat_ParseReplyBlock, stop capturing the fractional value. Replace unsigned long reply_context = BotChat_NumberTokenValue(&token); float priority = token.floatvalue; ... rule->priority = priority; with the truncated unsigned integer retail stores: unsigned long reply_context = BotChat_NumberTokenValue(&token); ... rule->priority = (float)reply_context; (equivalently, simply delete the `rule->priority = priority;` line at :6420 and the `float priority = token.floatvalue;` at :6408, since BotChat_AddReplyRule at :2747 already sets `rule->priority = (float)reply_context;` — the exact `(float)(unsigned long)token.intvalue` retail computes at 1002d52f.) Leave the ranking loop at :8958/:8971 alone; it already reproduces retail's int-tracked `(float)var_fc < rc->priority`.

### LOW Retail's "no rchats" load-time diagnostic is not emitted

- status: **applied**
- retail: `BotLoadReplyChat` @ `0x1002d270`
- HLIL: 35775-35779 (1002d68f: BotCheckReplyChatIntegrety(result); 1002d699: if (result == 0); 1002d6a2: data_10063fe8(1, "no rchats\n"))
- ours: `src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:7163`

**Divergence.** Retail BotLoadReplyChat (0x1002d270) ends its success path in this order: FreeSource (1002d60b), the load message ("loaded %s\n" at 1002d685 or "loaded %s\\%s\n" at 1002d628), BotCheckReplyChatIntegrety(replyhead) (1002d68f), then `if (replyhead == 0) botimport.Print(PRT_MESSAGE, "no rchats\n")` (1002d699 -> 1002d6a2), then `return replyhead`. replyhead is NULL on that path in exactly one case: PC_ReadToken at 1002d305 found no token at all, i.e. rchat.c is empty or comments-only after preprocessing — every parse failure returns early at 1002d661/1002d67d without printing either message, so the claim's scenario is the only trigger. Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai_chat.c:1568-1576. Our reconstruction never emits "no rchats": BotChat_ParseReplyScript (src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:6944) returns 1 with state->replies.rule_count == 0 for a token-free script; BotChat_ParseSetupReplyScript (src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:7154-7165) runs BotChat_CheckReplyChatIntegrity and returns 1; the shared BotChat_LoadSetupScript (src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:7174-7236) prints only "loaded ..."; and BotSetupChatAI (src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:7513-7520) discards the loader's return value. A secondary ordering divergence is visible at the same site: retail runs BotCheckReplyChatIntegrety (and therefore its per-response "random ... not found" diagnostics) AFTER the "loaded" print, while ours runs it inside the parser callback, before the print.

**Failure scenario.** Run with `nochat 0` and an rchat.c that contains only comments/#includes (or is empty after preprocessing). Retail: the parse loop never runs, replychats stays NULL, and the console shows `loaded rchat.c` followed by `no rchats`. Ours: the console shows `loaded rchat.c` and nothing else, so the operator gets no indication that reply chat is inert.

**Fix.** The message cannot be emitted where the claim proposes. BotChat_LoadSetupScript is shared by all four setup assets and invokes the parser callback (which is where BotChat_CheckReplyChatIntegrity lives) BEFORE printing "loaded ...", so adding the print inside BotChat_ParseSetupReplyScript would both fire for the wrong ordering and require asset-specific logic in a generic parser. Instead, in BotSetupChatAI (src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:7513-7520), capture the currently-discarded return of the rchatfile BotChat_LoadSetupScript call and, when it is non-zero (file resolved, loaded, and parsed — matching retail's early returns at 1002d661/1002d67d which print nothing) and bot_chat_setup_state->replies.rule_count == 0, emit BotLib_Print(PRT_MESSAGE, "no rchats\n"). That places it after the "loaded ..." print, reproducing 1002d628/1002d685 -> 1002d68f -> 1002d6a2. Optionally, to also fix the secondary ordering divergence, move BotChat_CheckReplyChatIntegrity out of BotChat_ParseSetupReplyScript so its diagnostics are emitted after the "loaded" print as at 1002d68f.

### LOW "couldn't find chat %s in %s" prints the requested chat-file name instead of retail's resolved container path

- status: **applied**
- retail: `BotLoadInitialChat` @ `0x1002d8a0`
- HLIL: 36201-36203 (1002dce1: if (var_564 == 0); 1002ddc5: data_10063fe8(3, "couldn't find chat %s in %s\n", arg2, &var_558))
- ours: `src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:7606`

**Divergence.** src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:7606 passes `chatfile` as the second `%s` of `"couldn't find chat %s in %s\n"`, where retail BotLoadInitialChat @0x1002d8a0 passes the resolved fileref path (HLIL 1002ddc5: `data_10063fe8(3, "couldn't find chat %s in %s\n", arg2, &var_558)`; `&var_558` is the `bot_fileref_t.path` filled by sub_10041F60/sub_10041BA0 and fed to LoadSourceFile at 1002d920, also used at 1002dd3b and 1002dd21). Per sub_10041BA0 (HLIL 10041dc6, 10041e0d, 10041e74-10041e99) that path is `<basedir>/<gamedir>/<chatfile>` for a loose hit and `<basedir>/<gamedir>/pakN.pak` for a pak hit, so retail never prints the bare requested spelling here. Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai_chat.c:1718, 1756, 1900 (`chatname, file_ref.path`). The divergence affects console text on the chat-not-found failure path only; the success messages at ai_chat.c:7619-7628 already match retail. The claim's second cited site, line 7588, is not a retail-equivalent path: it fires only for `open_status == -1` (host-only `PS_CreateScriptFromSource` failure), and retail's two genuine open failures — `"couldn't find %s\n"` with the requested name (1002d8d1) and `"counldn't load %s\n"` with the resolved path (1002dd3b) — are already reproduced at ai_chat.c:7317 and 7319.

**Failure scenario.** Bots configured with a chat name that is absent from a chat file living inside a pak (e.g. `BotLoadChatFile(cs, "bots/chats.c", "snotty")` where chats.c is inside pak0.pak). Retail prints `couldn't find chat snotty in <resolved pak/container path>`, which tells the operator which physical file was actually searched; ours prints `couldn't find chat snotty in bots/chats.c`, hiding the fact that a stale pak copy shadowed the loose file.

**Fix.** Change only the second argument at src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:7606 from `chatfile` to `state->active_resolution.source_path` (which is valid there, because `open_status == 1` means BotChat_OpenActiveScript stored the resolution at line 7334), keeping `chatname` first — matching the success message at 7623. Do NOT apply the same change at line 7588: `state->active_resolution` is only assigned on a successful open and is not cleared by BotChat_CloseActiveScript, so at that site it is zeroed on a fresh state or stale from a previous load. That site is better left as-is, or removed entirely, since retail emits no `"couldn't find chat %s in %s"` for an open/load failure.

## `src/botlib/ai_goal/bot_goal.c`

### MEDIUM BotUpdateEntityItems: entity-link path recomputes the area with a stale iteminfo index in retail, the correct one in ours

- status: **open**
- retail: `BotUpdateEntityItems` @ `0x1002fa20`
- HLIL: 37700-37753 (decisive: 37702 `if (*(result_2[1] + *(esi_1 + 4) * 0x11c + 0xf0) == j)` matches on li->iteminfo, but 37747 `void* ecx_10 = result_2[1] + ebx_1 * 0x11c` then 37748-37750 `j_sub_1000b300(esi_1 + 8, ecx_10 + 0x100, ecx_10 + 0x10c, esi_1 + 0x18)`); ebx_1 is written only at 37601 `int32_t ebx_1 = var_108` (uninitialised) and inside the LABEL_24 modelindex search at 37664-37671
- ours: `src/botlib/ai_goal/bot_goal.c:1810`

**Divergence.** BotUpdateEntityItems (retail 0x1002fa20, HLIL 37560-37773) computes the AAS_BestReachableArea bbox for the entity-link path from a reused itemconfig index that is NOT the matched levelitem's iteminfo. The register ebx is loaded once at 0x1002fa90 (`mov ebx,[esp+14h]`) from a local slot that is only ever written later with the modelindex (`mov [esp+14h],eax` at 0x1002fa9d), i.e. an uninitialised read; thereafter ebx is assigned solely by the "search itemconfig by modelindex" loop at 0x1002fbef-0x2fc0d (HLIL 37664-37674), which leaves it in [0, numitems] and equal to numitems on failure. The link branch at HLIL 37747-37750 (0x1002fba5 `lea ecx,[ebx+ebx*8]` / `shl ecx,3` / `sub ecx,ebx` / 0x1002fbbc `lea ecx,[edx+ecx*4]`) therefore passes `ic->items[ebx].mins/.maxs`, whereas the match that selected the levelitem used `ic->items[li->iteminfo].modelindex` (0x1002fb18). Our reconstruction (src/botlib/ai_goal/bot_goal.c:1810-1813) passes the linked item's own `itemdef->mins/maxs`. Correction to the original claim's scenario: differing bbox sizes between item classes cannot happen with retail data — all 66 iteminfos in the shipped items.c use mins {-15,-15,-15} / maxs {15,15,15}, so a stale but in-range index is unobservable. The observable divergence comes from the index going out of range. LoadItemConfig allocates max_iteminfo (default 256) zeroed iteminfo slots, so ic->items[numitems] is valid zeroed memory. Concrete reachable case: on the first BotUpdateEntityItems tick, a door/plat (nonzero modelindex, no matching iteminfo) drives the search loop to exhaustion leaving ebx = numitems = 66; the next static levelitem with entitynum 0 that links to its spawned entity is then given an areanum/goalorigin computed with mins = maxs = {0,0,0} instead of +/-15. That only changes the result on AAS_BestReachableArea's fallback path (point-area fudge grid and crouch trace both fail), i.e. for items sitting outside the AAS mesh — for those, retail degrades the areanum that BotInitLevelItems had computed correctly with +/-15, and our build keeps the correct one. Additionally, before any search loop has run in a call, ebx holds true uninitialised stack, so retail's first link per call indexes itemconfig with garbage.

**Failure scenario.** Map with two item classes of different bbox (e.g. a 15-unit weapon box and a 32-unit powerup box). Frame N: a dropped entity with an unknown modelindex runs the LABEL_24 search and leaves ebx_1 = index of the powerup iteminfo. Frame N+1: a static shotgun levelitem (entitynum 0) is within 20 units of the spawned shotgun entity, so it is linked. Retail computes its areanum/goalorigin with the *powerup* mins/maxs and can land on a different AAS area or a different goalorigin; our build computes them with the shotgun's own mins/maxs. The bot's route to that item, and BotTouchingGoal's box for it, differ from retail.

**Fix.** Do NOT use a file-scope/static variable — retail's ebx is a function-local that is re-read from an uninitialised stack slot on every call, so it does not carry across calls. In BotUpdateEntityItems (src/botlib/ai_goal/bot_goal.c:1751), declare one function-local `int reused_iteminfo` before the entity loop and: 1. Assign it only in the modelindex-search step. Replace the pointer-returning `BotGoal_FindItemDefByModelIndex(modelindex)` use at :1832 with an explicit index loop that mirrors retail: `for (reused_iteminfo = 0; reused_iteminfo < g_itemdef_count; ++reused_iteminfo) if (g_itemdefs[reused_iteminfo].modelindex == modelindex) break;` then `if (reused_iteminfo >= g_itemdef_count) continue;`. The not-found exit value (== g_itemdef_count) is load-bearing: it is what makes the later link branch read the zeroed slot, so the loop must be allowed to leave it there rather than resetting or clamping. 2. In the link branch at :1810-1813, pass `g_itemdefs[reused_iteminfo].mins` / `.maxs` instead of `itemdef->mins` / `itemdef->maxs`. This requires the itemdef table to be allocated at the full max_iteminfo capacity and zeroed (bot_goal.c:3131 already reads the libvar; verify the allocation is GetClearedMemory-equivalent over the whole 256 slots so index g_itemdef_count reads zeros, matching retail). 3. Leave the new-item branch at :1853-1856 using the freshly found index (retail HLIL 37685, ref be_ai_goal.c:547-551) — that one is correct in retail. 4. Retail's pre-first-search value is an uninitialised stack read and is not reproducible. Initialise `reused_iteminfo = g_itemdef_count` (the zeroed slot) rather than 0, since that is the only defined value retail can also produce, and add a comment recording that the true retail value on the first link of a call is indeterminate stack garbage.

### LOW LoadItemConfig: retail uses FreeMemory on the source (not FreeSource) when the iteminfo name token is missing

- status: **open**
- retail: `LoadItemConfig` @ `0x1002ed20`
- HLIL: 37063-37066 — `if (j_sub_1003d740(eax_5, 1, 0, &var_430) == 0) { j_sub_100390b0(result); j_sub_100390b0(eax_5); return 0 }`; contrast 37071-37074 for the ReadStructure failure, which uses `j_sub_100390b0(result); j_sub_1003e000(eax_5)` (FreeMemory + FreeSource), and 37085-37088 for the unknown-definition path
- ours: `src/botlib/ai_goal/bot_goal.c:3207`

**Divergence.** LoadItemConfig (retail 0x1002ed20) has four error exits inside its parse loop. Three of them -- over-capacity (HLIL 37085-37088 via the shared tail), ReadStructure failure (37071-37074), and unknown-definition (37085-37088) -- release the parser with FreeSource (j_sub_1003e000). The fourth, the PC_ExpectTokenType(src, TT_STRING, 0, token) failure that guards the quoted classname, instead calls FreeMemory (j_sub_100390b0) on the source pointer: HLIL 37063-37066 reads `if (j_sub_1003d740(eax_5, 1, 0, &var_430) == 0) { j_sub_100390b0(result); j_sub_100390b0(eax_5); return 0 }`, with both frees going to the same target 100390b0. Because FreeSource (ref l_precomp.c:2573-2610) is what tears down the scriptstack (each script_t plus its file image), the pushed-back token list, every define, the indent stack, and the 4096-byte definehash before finally calling FreeMemory(source), retail's header-only free reclaims only the source_t block -- 1624 bytes on the DLL per ref l_precomp.h:76-88, NOT the "40-ish bytes" the original claim states -- and leaks the entire rest of the parser state. Our BotGoal_LoadItemDefs routes all four failures through one shared PC_FreeSource(source) at src/botlib/ai_goal/bot_goal.c:3207 (PC_FreeSource is a plain wrapper over the full FreeSource, src/botlib/precomp/l_precomp.c:3936), so we free everything on the classname-token path where retail frees only the header. Reachability and observability, both of which I verified rather than assumed: the branch fires for any items.c where the `iteminfo` keyword is not followed by a TT_STRING (e.g. `iteminfo { ... }`, or EOF right after the keyword). The resulting BLERR_CANNOTLOADITEMCONFIG does not disable the library, because HLIL 43444 sets botstate.setup (data_10064020 = 1) at 10037c04, before BotSetupLibrary is called at 10037c78 -- so BotLibSetup still passes and AAS_StartFrame keeps running. AAS_StartFrame's showmemoryusage/memorydump handlers print "total botlib memory: %d KB" and "total memory blocks: %d", which our build also implements at src/botlib/aas/aas_main.c:239-259 over the same tracked allocator (our source_t is GetMemory-allocated at l_precomp.c:3984). Both numbers therefore differ between retail and our build after a bad items.c. No gameplay or bot-behaviour effect; the divergence is confined to those two diagnostic counters on an error path.

**Failure scenario.** Point `itemconfig` at an items.c whose first definition is `iteminfo { ... }` (keyword present, quoted classname missing). Retail leaks the loaded script buffer (the whole file image) plus its token list; our build frees them. Any memory-usage report taken after a bad items.c differs by the size of the script file plus its tokens.

**Fix.** In BotGoal_LoadItemDefs (src/botlib/ai_goal/bot_goal.c), split the classname-token failure out of the shared cleanup at line 3207 instead of retagging the whole exit. Concretely: 1. Add a distinct flag (or a small enum) alongside `parsed` so the `!PC_ExpectTokenType(source, TT_STRING, 0, &classname_token)` failure at line 3186-3190 is distinguishable from the other three exits. 2. Add a header-only release helper in src/botlib/precomp/l_precomp.c next to PC_FreeSource -- e.g. PC_FreeSourceHeader(pc_source_t *) -- that mirrors retail's FreeMemory(source): it must NOT walk scriptstack, tokens, defines, indentstack, or definehash, so those tracked blocks stay live and BotMemory_BlockCount()/BotMemory_TotalAllocated() match retail. Give it the repo's commented header block and a comment citing HLIL 37063-37066 as the reason the leak is deliberate, otherwise a future cleanup pass will "fix" it back. 3. One thing the original proposed fix misses: our FreeSource also frees source->diagnostics_head with plain malloc/free (l_precomp.c:4096-4106), which has no retail counterpart -- it is our own host-side allocation, not a tracked botlib block. PC_FreeSourceHeader must still free that diagnostics list (and then the source_t itself via FreeMemory) so we do not introduce a host-allocator leak that retail never had while emulating the tracked-allocator one that it did. 4. Leave the over-capacity, unknown-definition, and BotGoal_ParseItemInfoBlock failures on the existing full PC_FreeSource path -- HLIL 37071-37074 and 37085-37088 confirm those are FreeSource in retail. Given the severity, an equally defensible resolution is to leave the code as-is and record the divergence as a knowingly-unreplicated retail memory bug, since replicating it means deliberately introducing a ~4 KB-plus leak into our build for an error path that only shows up in two diagnostic counters. If the project's parity target includes the memory counters, apply the fix above; if not, document it.

## `src/botlib/ai_weapon/bot_weapon.c`

### LOW BotFreeWeaponWeights nulls the state pointers retail deliberately leaves dangling

- status: **open**
- retail: `BotFreeWeaponWeights` @ `0x10035300`
- HLIL: 41371-41386
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai_weap.c:258-264
- ours: `src/botlib/ai_weapon/bot_weapon.c:124`

**Divergence.** BotFreeWeaponWeights (sub_10035300, HLIL 41371-41383) frees ws->weightconfig via FreeWeightConfig2 (j_sub_100359b0) and ws->itemweights via FreeMemory (j_sub_100390b0) but writes neither field back, so both stay dangling. Our BotWeapon_ClearWeights (src/botlib/ai_weapon/bot_weapon.c:124-128) nulls state->weights/config/weight_config and clears owns_weights/fresh_weights. The observable path is NOT the 0x1f branch of BotLoadWeaponWeights (unreachable: itemweights can only be non-zero after an earlier load that required weaponconfig != 0, and BotLoadWeaponWeights runs exactly once per client on a slab zeroed by the memset at 10029750). It is the second caller: sub_10029480 (BotSetupClient) fails chat-state load at 10029586, calls j_sub_10035300(&ebx_2[0x412]) at 10029593, and returns 0 at 100295a0 without zeroing the client slab and without setting *ebx_2 = 1, leaving both weightconfig and itemweights dangling. sub_10029690 will not clean them up either - it hits `if (*s == 0)`, prints "client %d already shutdown", and returns 0x17 before reaching its memset - and ref/gladiator-bot-restored/game/bl_spawn.c:200 shows game.dll simply G_FreeClientEdicts the slot. When the next bot lands on that client number, BotSetupClient passes the `*ebx_2 != 0` gate, reaches BotLoadWeaponWeights at 10029542, and the BotFreeWeaponWeights call at 10035347 double-frees both blocks: FreeMemory's 0x12345678 header check (sub_10039040) makes retail print PRT_FATAL "FreeMemory: invalid memory block\n" or corrupt the arena if the block was recycled, and FreeWeightConfig2 re-walks the already-freed fuzzyseperator tree. Our reconstruction runs the same caller sequence (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:2836) but reloads cleanly and emits no diagnostic. Note also that retail has no BotFreeWeaponState - the 0x1c-byte weaponstate is embedded in the client slab at client*0x11d0 + 0x1048.

**Failure scenario.** BotLoadWeaponWeights runs, the weight file loads but `weaponconfig` is NULL, so retail returns 0x1f at 1003538a with ws->itemweights still holding the pointer freed by the BotFreeWeaponWeights call at 10035347. A later BotFreeWeaponWeights (or BotFreeWeaponState) double-frees it. We silently do not, so allocator statistics, BotMemory leak reports and any regression test that measures retail heap behaviour after that sequence diverge.

**Fix.** Do not remove the null-out. Reproducing retail's double free is heap UB with no deterministic behaviour worth matching, and our modular ownership model (owns_weights / fresh_weights, cached vs fresh weight configs) does not map onto retail's unconditional two-field free anyway. Record it as an intentional deviation: add a comment above the null-out block in src/botlib/ai_weapon/bot_weapon.c:124 noting that retail sub_10035300 leaves ws->weightconfig and ws->itemweights dangling, that sub_10029480's chat-failure path at 10029593 returns without clearing the slab so a re-setup of the same client double-frees both blocks, and that this reconstruction deliberately clears the fields instead. If a heap-parity regression test ever needs the retail trace, gate the divergence behind an explicit debug flag rather than changing the default.

## `src/botlib/ai_weight/ai_weight.c`

### LOW ReadFuzzySeperators_r: we free the partial separator chain on the "invalid name" case-body error; retail leaks it

- status: **open**
- retail: `ReadFuzzySeperators_r` @ `0x10035a20`
- HLIL: 41967-41970 — `if (eax_28 != 0)` (token is neither "return" nor "switch") -> `j_sub_10039200(arg1, "invalid name %s\n"); return 0`, with no `j_sub_10035960` (FreeFuzzySeperators_r) call, unlike every other error exit in this routine (41814, 41837, 42047)
- ours: `src/botlib/ai_weight/ai_weight.c:637`

**Divergence.** ReadFuzzySeperators_r @ 0x10035a20 has exactly one non-freeing error exit: the case/default *body* whose first token is neither `return` nor `switch` (nor an opening `{`, which is consumed earlier). HLIL 41967-41970 shows `10035cc5 if (eax_28 != 0)` -> `10035e4d j_sub_10039200(arg1, "invalid name %s\n")` -> `10035e61 return 0`, jumping over the shared `10035e63 j_sub_10035960(...)` (FreeFuzzySeperators_r) to the 10035e77 epilogue. Retail therefore leaks the entire partially built separator chain rooted at `firstfs` for this switch level - every sibling separator allocated so far plus each of their already-parsed child subtrees, not just the single current 32-byte node. All other error exits in the routine do free (HLIL 41814, 41837, 42047). This is an upstream quirk, not a decompilation artifact: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai_weight.c:236-240 omits the free, and Mr. Elusive's own Q3 source (dev_tools/Quake-III-Arena-master/code/botlib/be_ai_weight.c:230-234) has the identical omission next to a freeing sibling branch at 244-249. Our src/botlib/ai_weight/ai_weight.c:637 calls BotWeight_FreeFuzzySeperators(first) on that path, so after loading a malformed weight config the botlib block count / byte total is lower than retail's by the size of that leaked subtree. Observable via the retail memory diagnostic (PrintUsedMemorySize @ 10039150, reached from AAS_StartFrame under the `showmemoryusage` / `memorydump` libvars), which we implement at src/botlib/common/l_memory.c:292 and src/botlib/aas/aas_main.c:245,256.

**Failure scenario.** Load a weight config containing `weight "x" { switch(2) { case 1: foo; default: return 1; } }`. Retail: SourceError("invalid name foo"), the one already-built `case 1` separator (32 bytes from GetClearedMemory) stays allocated forever, so botlib's allocated-block count/byte total is permanently 1 block / 32 bytes higher. Our build releases it, so any memory-parity dump or AvailableMemory reading after a malformed bot config differs from retail.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/ai_weight/ai_weight.c, delete line 637 (`BotWeight_FreeFuzzySeperators(first);`) from the trailing `else` at 635-639, so the branch becomes SourceError(source, "invalid name %s\n", token.string); return NULL; with no free - matching HLIL 10035cc5/10035e46-10035e61. Leave every other BotWeight_FreeFuzzySeperators call in the routine (lines 571, 577, 592, 599, 607, 611, 619, 626, 632, 643, 649, 659) untouched. Add a short comment on the deleted line's site noting the omission is deliberate retail behaviour (HLIL 10035e61 jumps past the shared free at 10035e63; corroborated by Q3 be_ai_weight.c:230-234), so a future cleanup pass or static analyzer does not "fix" it back. If the repo runs leak detection (ASan/valgrind) over the weight-config parser tests, suppress or annotate this one path rather than reintroducing the free. Do not change the outer invalid-name branch at lines 570-572: retail frees before calling SourceError there (10035d64 then 10035d7b) while we error first, but that ordering has no observable effect.

### LOW ReadWeightConfig: our parse-failure path frees the in-progress weight name and separator tree that retail leaks

- status: **open**
- retail: `ReadWeightConfig` @ `0x10035fa0`
- HLIL: 42152 (`result_2[*result_2 * 2 + 1] = j_sub_10038f90(not.d(i))` — name allocated at index numweights), 42170-42173 / 42212-42215 / 42259-42261 / 42316-42319 (every failure after that point does only `j_sub_100359b0(result_2); j_sub_1003e000(eax_4); return 0`), and 42322 (`*result_3 += 1` — numweights is incremented only after the whole entry succeeds)
- ours: `src/botlib/ai_weight/ai_weight.c:759`

**Divergence.** In retail ReadWeightConfig (@0x10035fa0) the weight name is allocated with GetMemory into `config->weights[numweights].name` at HLIL 42152 (`10036113`, `result_2[*result_2 * 2 + 1] = j_sub_10038f90(...)`), but `numweights` is not incremented until HLIL 42322 (`100362a6`, `*result_3 += 1`). Every failure path in between calls FreeWeightConfig2 (`sub_100359b0`, HLIL 41707-41725), which walks only `i < *arg1` (numweights) and therefore never touches index numweights. Retail consequently leaks the in-progress name block on all six failure paths: 42170-73 (PC_ExpectAnyToken after the name), 42212-15 (PC_ExpectAnyToken after `{`), 42259-61 (ReadFuzzySeperators_r returned NULL), 42306-10 (ReadFuzzyWeight failed - here retail *does* free the fuzzyseperator itself at 42307/`10036388`, it only leaks the name), 42295-98 -> 42330 (the "invalid name %s" else branch), and 42316-19 (`PC_ExpectTokenString("}")` failed). The last case leaks both the name and the entire firstseperator tree, because the tree was already stored into `weights[numweights].firstseperator` at 42256 (switch) or 42313 (return) before the brace check runs. Our BotWeight_ParseWeights has a `parse_failure:` label (src/botlib/ai_weight/ai_weight.c:758-764) reached from all six paths that frees `root` and `weight->name`, so our build releases blocks retail abandons. No caller compensates - BotWeight_ReadConfig:838-841 only calls BotWeight_FreeConfig, which stops at num_weights exactly as retail's FreeWeightConfig2 does (ai_weight.c:404-422 matches HLIL 41707-41725). This is observable, not cosmetic: retail's `sub_10039150` (@0x10039150) prints "total botlib memory: %d KB\n" and "total memory blocks: %d\n" from the GetMemory/FreeMemory counters `data_10063a1c`/`data_10063a2c`, driven by the `showmemoryusage` and `memorydump` libvars at HLIL 12471/12480 and by the per-bot report `sub_100293a0`. Our repo reproduces those counters (src/botlib/aas/aas_main.c:239-258, src/botlib/common/l_memory.c:288-294). After loading a weights file whose entry is `weight "ammo" { switch(1) { default: return 1; }` (missing the final `}`), retail's block count is higher than ours by 1 (name) plus one per separator node; our build reports the lower figure.

**Failure scenario.** Load a weight config whose second entry is `weight "ammo" { switch(1) { default: return 1; }` (missing the closing `}`). Retail: SourceError path at 42316-42319 frees the config and the first entry only; the `"ammo"` name block and its whole separator subtree stay allocated. Our build frees both, so the botlib heap after a malformed weights file holds fewer blocks and fewer bytes than retail.

**Fix.** Do NOT delete the whole `parse_failure:` block - retail frees the freshly allocated fuzzyseperator on one of the six paths. In src/botlib/ai_weight/ai_weight.c: 1. Replace `goto parse_failure;` with a plain `return false;` at lines 712, 717, 724, 731, 745, and 750. These correspond to HLIL 42170-73, 42212-15, 42259-61, 42295-98, and 42316-19, all of which do only `FreeWeightConfig2(config); FreeSource(source); return 0;` and therefore abandon `weights[numweights].name` (and, for line 750 / HLIL 42316-19, the separator tree too). 2. Keep exactly one cleanup, on the ReadFuzzyWeight-failure path at lines 740-742, which must stay as `FreeMemory(root); return false;` - retail's 42306-42310 (`10036388`) calls `j_sub_100390b0(eax_26)` on the fresh fuzzyseperator before FreeWeightConfig2. A plain `FreeMemory(root)` is exact here rather than BotWeight_FreeFuzzySeperators(root), since ReadFuzzyWeight (`sub_10035820` @0x10035820, HLIL 41628-41679) allocates nothing and leaves child/next NULL. 3. Delete the now-unreachable `parse_failure:` label at 758-764 (and, with it, the `weight->name = NULL` write-back, which retail never performs - retail leaves the stale pointer sitting at index numweights). 4. Because `root` is no longer freed on the brace-failure path, the assignment `weight->first_seperator = root;` at line 754 becomes purely cosmetic ordering; if you want to mirror retail write-for-write, move it up to immediately after each `root` is produced (HLIL 42256 `10036207` for switch, 42313 `1003627a` for return), i.e. before the `requires_closing_brace` check. Either ordering is unobservable once the frees match. 5. Optional exactness, unobservable on its own: line 710 should use GetMemory rather than GetClearedMemory to match HLIL 42152's `j_sub_10038f90` (GetMemory @0x10038f90; GetClearedMemory is @0x10039000). Byte and block totals are identical either way and strcpy overwrites all strlen+1 bytes. Leave BotWeight_FreeConfig (404-422) unchanged - it already matches sub_100359b0.

## `src/botlib/common/l_assets.c`

### MEDIUM Asset resolver omits retail's per-candidate "accessing %s" and "searching %s in %s" log records

- status: **open**
- retail: `sub_10041BA0 (FindFileInPath)` @ `0x10041ba0`
- HLIL: 51959-51963 (`sub_10044cb0(&var_240, arg3, ...); j_sub_100418d0(&var_240); j_sub_10038d80("accessing %s")`) and 52031-52034 (`if (sub_10052e5c(&var_240, 4) == 0) { ...; j_sub_10038d80("searching %s in %s") }`)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_utils.c:469, 489
- ours: `src/botlib/common/l_assets.c:904`

**Divergence.** Retail sub_10041BA0 (0x10041ba0, HLIL 51864-52067) emits two Log_Write (sub_10038d80) records our general asset resolver omits entirely: 1. HLIL 51959-51963 — after appending arg3 and running the separator fixup j_sub_100418d0, it writes `Log_Write("accessing %s", FileName)` unconditionally, BEFORE the `_access(FileName, 4)` test at 51965. One record per (root, subdir) loose candidate; sub_10041F60 (0x10041f60, HLIL 52077-52087) calls sub_10041BA0 twice (basedir then cddir) and each call walks subdirs {gamedir, "baseq2"}, so up to four `accessing` lines per resolve, stopping at the first readable candidate. 2. HLIL 52031-52034 — inside the pak0..pak9 loop, gated on `_access(pakpath, 4) == 0`, it writes `Log_Write("searching %s in %s", arg3, FileName)` immediately before j_sub_10041970 scans the archive. Note the argument order: the FIRST %s is the requested relative name (arg3), the SECOND is the pak path. Only existing paks produce a record. sub_10038d80 is Log_Write (ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_log.c:119); it no-ops when logfile.fp is NULL, so both records are observable exactly when a log file is open. sub_10041F60 is the resolver for every non-AAS asset load — HLIL callers at 0x1000e944, 0x1000ea69, 0x1001c817, 0x10029eea, 0x1002b131, 0x1002b9b1, 0x1002c431, 0x1002d291, 0x1002d8c7, 0x1002edb5, 0x10034c8b, 0x10035fe8, 0x1003a9c3 (character, chat, item, weapon and weight configs, sound config, precompiler includes). Our replacement resolver in src/botlib/common/l_assets.c emits nothing: BotLib_CheckFilesystemPaths (line 878; candidates formatted at 894 and 904) goes straight to BotLib_FileExists, BotLib_SearchPakForAsset (line 821) iterates pak_files at 861 straight into BotLib_ExtractPakEntry at 863, and BotLib_ResolveAssetPathDetailed (line 1015) adds no logging. The file does not even include l_log.h. None of the callers log either. src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:5687 and :5716 do reproduce both records, but only inside AAS_DiscoverMapFile for the AAS path. Result: with `openlog bot.log` active, retail's log shows the full per-candidate probe trace for a bot character load while ours shows nothing for that load.

**Failure scenario.** `openlog bot.log` with the `log` libvar enabled, then load a bot character. Retail's log contains one `accessing <basedir>\<gamedir>\bots\foo_i.c` line per probed root plus a `searching bots/foo_i.c in <root>\pak0.pak` line per scanned pak; our log contains nothing for that load, so the standard way of diagnosing a missing bot file against the retail DLL produces an empty log.

**Fix.** Add `#include "l_log.h"` to src/botlib/common/l_assets.c (currently absent; BotLib_LogWrite is declared at src/botlib/common/l_log.h:45), then: 1. In BotLib_CheckFilesystemPaths (src/botlib/common/l_assets.c:878), emit `BotLib_LogWrite("accessing %s", candidate);` immediately after each successful snprintf and BotLib_CopyPath(last_candidate, ...) — i.e. after line 896 for the preferred-subdir spelling and after line 906 for the direct spelling — and before the corresponding BotLib_FileExists call. This matches retail's unconditional pre-_access placement. 2. In BotLib_SearchPakForAsset (src/botlib/common/l_assets.c:821), emit `BotLib_LogWrite("searching %s in %s", relative, pak_files[i].path);` inside the loop at line 861, before the BotLib_ExtractPakEntry call at 863. Pass `relative` (the requested name), NOT `target` (the normalized pak key), as the first argument: retail logs arg3 verbatim. BotLib_CollectPakFiles (line 489) already returns only paks that exist, so this reproduces retail's `_access`-gated record without an extra probe. Caveat to keep in mind while applying: our BotLib_CheckPakPaths calls BotLib_SearchPakForAsset twice per root (preferred-subdir spelling, then the direct spelling), and BotLib_ResolveAssetPathDetailed runs an override-root pass before the normal root pass, so the resulting line count will not equal retail's exactly — that is a pre-existing structural divergence in the probe order, not something this fix introduces. Do not restructure the probe loops as part of this change; log one record per candidate our resolver actually forms.

### MEDIUM Asset resolver probes the raw requested path (CWD-relative) before basedir/gamedir, which retail never does

- status: **open**
- retail: `sub_10041BA0 / sub_10041F60` @ `0x10041ba0`
- HLIL: 51900-51965 (each iteration starts `var_240 = 0`, then `if (arg1 != 0 && strlen(arg1) != 0) StrCopyFixed(&var_240, arg1, 0x90); AppendPathSeperator` before `sub_10044cb0(&var_240, arg3, ...)`; and 52077-52087, where sub_10041f60 only ever calls sub_10041ba0 with basedir then cddir)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_utils.c:452-509, 513-528
- ours: `src/botlib/common/l_assets.c:1031`

**Divergence.** BotLib_ResolveAssetPathDetailed (src/botlib/common/l_assets.c:1030-1040) probes the requested path CWD-relative, at highest priority, for any request containing '/' or '\\' — a location retail's sub_10041ba0 never probes. Retail's probe set is exactly <root><sep><slot><sep><request> for root in {basedir (0x10041f92), cddir (0x10041fba)} and slot in {the gamedir libvar (0x10041c2b), the literal "baseq2" (0x10041c3f)}, with the pak0-9.pak scan under each combination; the bare request is built only when the root is empty (0x10041c6a/0x10041c78) AND the slot is empty (0x10041ca8), which can only occur on the gamedir pass since slot 1 is the non-empty "baseq2" literal. Observable divergence with Quake II's stock basedir="." / gamedir="<mod>" (ref/gladiator-bot-restored/game/bl_main.c:978-988) and a loose copy of, say, bots/hunter_i.c sitting in the CWD: retail resolves ./<mod>/bots/hunter_i.c (or fails and the caller prints "couldn't find %s"), while we return the CWD copy. Because the shortcut also precedes the override roots, it defeats an explicitly set gladiator_asset_dir. The concrete effects are (a) resolution succeeds where retail returns 0, suppressing the "couldn't find %s" diagnostic; (b) resolution.source_path reports the CWD path, so the provenance split at ai_weight.c:846 / aas_sound.c:550 ("loaded %s\\%s" vs "loaded %s", retail 0x100362cb / 0x1001c978) picks the wrong branch; (c) pak_entry_length stays 0 and the pak0-9.pak scan retail would perform is skipped; (d) for aas_sound.c:527, which parses resolution.resolved_path directly, the wrong file is actually loaded. Note the shortcut is NOT the reason ai_weight / ai_chat / bot_goal / ai_weapon would parse the CWD copy: those call PC_LoadSourceFile(<logical name>), which performs its own CWD probe at src/botlib/precomp/l_precomp.c:3837 (`else if (PC_FileExists(filename))`) ahead of the resolved path. That is the same divergence at a second layer. Secondary, same root cause: because the shortcut is gated on !bare_name, a bare request with an empty basedir and empty gamedir is never probed CWD-relative, which is precisely the one case retail does probe bare — though BotLib_BuildSearchRoots (l_assets.c:223-254) already drops empty roots entirely, so that pass is missing for a different reason.

**Failure scenario.** Server CWD is the quake2 root, basedir="." (or the same absolute path), gamedir="gladiator", and both `gladiator/bots/hunter_i.c` and a stale `bots/hunter_i.c` exist. Retail sub_10041f60 builds `.\gladiator\bots\hunter_i.c` and loads the mod's weight file. Ours hits the CWD shortcut, BotLib_FileExists("bots/hunter_i.c") succeeds, and it loads the base-directory copy -- the bot gets the wrong item/weapon weights and the resolution's source_path reports the wrong file.

**Fix.** Delete the pre-loop shortcut at src/botlib/common/l_assets.c:1030-1040 entirely. Do not relocate it to the end of the root loop: retail returns 0 there, so a last-resort CWD fallback would still turn a retail "couldn't find %s" into a success. Model the one bare probe retail really has inside the existing root machinery instead: in BotLib_BuildSearchRoots (l_assets.c:223-254), when a legacy dir (basedir, then cddir) is empty AND the gamedir libvar is empty, emit an empty-prefix root for that pass, and let BotLib_CheckFilesystemPaths/BotLib_CheckPakPaths compose "%s" rather than "%s/%s" for it. That probe must apply to bare and separator-bearing requests alike (retail does not distinguish), and must sit at the head of that pass, matching 0x10041c65-0x10041cfe. Removing the shortcut is safe for the precompiler: PC_LoadSourceFile already handles absolute and CWD-relative filenames on its own (l_precomp.c:3826 PC_PathIsAbsolute branch, l_precomp.c:3837 PC_FileExists branch) before it consults `have_resolved` at l_precomp.c:3851.
