If full retail probe order is the goal, that PC_FileExists(filename) branch at l_precomp.c:3837 needs the same treatment — it is a second, independent CWD-first probe that will keep selecting the CWD copy for every caller that loads by logical name (ai_weight.c:821, ai_chat.c:7200/7315, bot_goal.c:3149, ai_weapon.c:133) even after l_assets.c is fixed.

## `src/botlib/common/l_libvar.c`

### MEDIUM LibVarGet creates and links a new libvar on a miss; retail's is a pure lookup returning NULL

- status: **reverted**
- retail: `LibVarGet` @ `0x10038910`
- HLIL: 43924-43936 (`for (int32_t* i = data_10063f20; i != 0; i = i[5]) { ... if (eax_2 == 0) return i } return 0`)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_libvar.c:125-135, 159-170
- ours: `src/botlib/common/l_libvar.c:417`

**Divergence.** Retail LibVarGet (0x10038910, HLIL 43924-43936) is a side-effect-free walk of libvarlist that returns 0 when the name is absent -- it never allocates and never links (`for (int32_t* i = data_10063f20; i != 0; i = i[5]) ... return 0` at 1003893b). Our LibVarGet (src/botlib/common/l_libvar.c:411-417) performs the same scan via LibVar_Find but on a miss falls through to `return LibVar_CreateFromImport(var_name);`, which queries imports->BotLibVarGet and, on a hit, calls LibVar_Create -> LibVarAlloc/LibVar_CopyString -> two GetMemory calls, links the record into g_libvar_list, and sets modified = 1. The path is live because of a second, related divergence: our BotSetupLibrary (src/botlib/interface/botlib_interface.c:381) calls LibVar_ResetCache(), wiping the local list while leaving the bridge import cache (g_botImportCache, written by the exported BotLibVarSetWrapper at src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:2434-2439) intact. Retail BotSetupLibrary (0x10037bb0, HLIL 43434-43465) never clears libvarlist, so host-set records survive setup and every later getter is a pure find. In ours, after setup the first LibVarGet/LibVarGetString/LibVarGetValue/LibVar for each host-set name allocates two tracked blocks. Concrete observable failure: with "showmemoryusage" set by the host before setup, retail's AAS frame diagnostic (sub_1000e010 at 0x1000e076) reads it with the non-allocating sub_10038990 and prints counters reflecting only real botlib allocations. Ours (src/botlib/aas/aas_main.c:239-248) calls LibVarGetValue -> LibVarGet -> LibVar_CreateFromImport, performing two GetMemory calls (l_memory.c:181-182 bumps allocated_bytes and block_count) before printing "total botlib memory: %d KB" / "total memory blocks: %d" -- inflating exactly the numbers the diagnostic reports, which our own comment at aas_main.c:215-224 documents as forbidden. Same for "memorydump" (aas_main.c:250). Correction to the original claim: the LibVar("weaponconfig", "weapons.c") argument does not hold and should be dropped. Retail's LibVarSet (0x10038ac0) creates a local record when the host sets the name, so retail's LibVarGet also finds it and retail's LibVar also discards the caller default. The values callers receive match retail; only the allocation side effect and its timing diverge.

**Failure scenario.** The host has pushed "showmemoryusage" into the bridge import cache (BotInterface_BotLibVarSetWrapper) without a matching local record. Retail's AAS frame diagnostics read it with the non-allocating getter sub_10038990 and print `total botlib memory: %d KB` / `total memory blocks: %d` reflecting only real botlib allocations. Ours calls LibVarGetValue -> LibVarGet -> LibVar_CreateFromImport, which performs a GetMemory before the counters are read, inflating exactly the numbers the diagnostic prints -- the failure src/botlib/aas/aas_main.c:215-224 explicitly documents as forbidden. Same shape for LibVar("weaponconfig", "weapons.c"): retail always registers "weapons.c", ours can register the import-cache spelling instead.

**Fix.** Two coordinated changes, since removing the create-on-miss alone would silently drop every host-supplied libvar (basedir, gamedir, maxclients, maxentities) that LibVar_ResetCache destroys during setup: 1. src/botlib/common/l_libvar.c:404-418 -- make LibVarGet a pure LibVar_Find wrapper matching HLIL 43926-43936: `libvar_t *LibVarGet(const char *var_name) { return var_name != NULL ? LibVar_Find(var_name) : NULL; }` LibVar_CreateFromImport/LibVar_FetchFromImport then become setup-only helpers. 2. Restore the retail invariant that the libvar list survives setup. Retail BotSetupLibrary (0x10037bb0) never clears libvarlist, and our exported BotLibVarSetWrapper (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:2436-2437) already calls LibVarSet locally, so the local list is correct before setup for real hosts. Either drop the LibVar_ResetCache() call at src/botlib/interface/botlib_interface.c:381, or -- if the reset must stay for the reinitialisation contract -- replace it with an explicit one-shot seed that walks the bridge import cache and materialises each entry through LibVar_Create before the first LibVarValue("maxclients", "4") at line 386. Either way the seeding must complete before any retail getter runs, so LibVarGet, LibVarGetString, LibVarGetValue and LibVar stay allocation-free. Note when seeding: BotInterface_BotLibVarGetShim (bot_interface.c:2064) matches with case-sensitive strcmp while retail LibVarGet uses _strcmpi, so seed by iterating the cache rather than by probing it with names.

### LOW LibVarStringValue returns early on a trailing '.' where retail reads past the terminator

- status: **open**
- retail: `LibVarStringValue` @ `0x10038750`
- HLIL: 43831-43852 (dot branch at 1003876d/10038775, fall-through into label_10038798 at 1003879a: `x87_r6_2 = float.t(sx.d(*edx) - 0x30) / float.t(var_4)`)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_libvar.c:51-70
- ours: `src/botlib/common/l_libvar.c:43`

**Divergence.** LibVarStringValue (retail 0x10038750, HLIL 43831-43852) has no terminator check between accepting a '.' and dereferencing the next byte: `1003877c edx = &edx[1]` falls straight into `1003879a x87_r6_2 = float.t(sx.d(*edx) - 0x30) / float.t(var_4)`, and the loop-continue at `100387be eax.b = edx[1]` then reads one byte past the NUL. Our src/botlib/common/l_libvar.c:43-46 adds `if (*string == '\0') { return (float)value; }`, an invented guard. Observable divergence for any libvar value ending in '.' (e.g. LibVarSet("bot_thinktime", "1.")): we always return 1.0f; retail can never return 1.0f — it adds (0 - '0')/10 = -4.8 giving -3.8, then continues scanning past the terminator with dotfound == 100, so it returns -3.8 only if the byte after the NUL is zero, returns 0.0f if that byte is a nonzero non-digit (the `if (ecx_1 != 0 || eax.b != 0x2e)` reject fires because dotfound is now non-zero), and accumulates further garbage if it is a digit. Since the value string is a GetMemory(strlen+1) block, retail's result at this input is heap-dependent. The reference reconstruction (ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_libvar.c:57-59) also has no such check. Note that the repo already reproduces the sibling fall-through for non-digit characters — tests/common/test_libvar.c:179-180 pins LibVarStringValue(".x") ≈ 7.2 — so only the NUL case is special-cased.

**Failure scenario.** LibVarSet("bot_thinktime", "1.") (any libvar value whose last character is '.', e.g. a cvar typed as "0." or "1." in a cfg). Retail: value = 1.0 then adds (0-48)/10 = -4.8, giving -3.8, and then keeps scanning bytes past the NUL until it happens to hit a zero byte, potentially accumulating more garbage. Ours: returns 1.0 exactly. Every consumer of that libvar (LibVarValue/LibVarGetValue) then sees a completely different number.

**Fix.** Delete the invented guard at src/botlib/common/l_libvar.c:43-46 so the dot branch reads `divisor = 10; string++;` and falls directly into the `if (divisor != 0)` accumulation, matching HLIL 1003877c->10038798 and ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_libvar.c:57-60 line for line. Be aware this makes our loop, like retail's, read one byte past the terminator of the GetMemory(strlen+1) value block for a trailing-'.' input; that OOB read is retail's own bug, and its downstream result is heap-dependent (0.0f, -3.8, or more garbage), so it cannot be pinned by a deterministic unit test unless the test feeds a caller-owned buffer with a known byte after the NUL. If the project's memory-safety rules forbid reproducing the OOB read verbatim, the alternative is to keep the read in-bounds but still add the `(0 - '0') / divisor` term and then take retail's most common continuation (`return 0.0f`), with a comment citing 0x1003876d/0x10038775 — do NOT keep the current early `return (float)value;`, which is the one result retail can never produce.

## `src/botlib/ea/ea_main.c`

### LOW EA_VectorToAngles diverges from vectoangles: no whole-degree truncation, no 360 wrap, wrong degenerate-axis pitch

- status: **applied**
- retail: `vectoangles` @ `0x10041790`
- HLIL: 51653-51715 (degenerate branch 100417b4-100417fd stores `fneg(90f)`/`fneg(270f)`; general branch 1004180b `__ftol(... * 57.295779513082323)`, 10041867 `if ((eax_4:1.b & 1) != 0) x87_r6_8 = x87_r6_8 + 360f`, 10041876 `*arg2 = fneg(x87_r6_8)`)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_utils.c:269-295
- ours: `src/botlib/ea/ea_main.c:85`

**Divergence.** src/botlib/ea/ea_main.c:85 `EA_VectorToAngles` is a second, private copy of retail `vectoangles` (0x10041790, HLIL 51653-51715) that omits the routine's whole-degree truncation. Retail runs both atan2 results through `__ftol` (1004180b, 10041858) and converts back with `float.t`, so every angle vectoangles produces is an integral degree; EA_VectorToAngles keeps the fractional `atan2f` result (and uses float `atan2f` where retail uses double `atan2`). It is reached in production from EA_LookAtPoint (ea_main.c:656) at src/botlib/ai/ai_dm.c:1766, so the chase branch writes e.g. yaw 26.5651 into input.viewangles where retail's math yields 26.0, every frame the bot chases an unseen enemy. The repo's faithful port, Vector2Angles (src/botlib/common/l_utils.c:63), is used at 17 other sites; this is the lone unfaithful duplicate. Two sub-claims from the original report do not hold and should be dropped: (1) EA_VectorToAngles does wrap a negative yaw by +360 (ea_main.c:108-111), matching retail; the only wrap difference is pitch, where retail stores -(p+360) and we store its mod-360 equivalent, which no consumer can distinguish. (2) The `value[2] == 0` degenerate case cannot be reached through EA_LookAtPoint: ai_dm.c:1756 gates the call on `distance > 0.0f`, and a zero direction gives distance == 0, so the claimed "(-270,0,0) vs (0,0,0), bot looks level instead of straight down" failure never occurs. The reachable degenerate case (value[0]==value[1]==0, value[2]<0) gives +90 vs retail -270, which is angularly identical.

**Failure scenario.** AI_DM chase path, ai_dm.c:1766: bot is directly under the predicted enemy position so `direction` normalises to (0,0,0) after VectorSubtract of coincident points, or the enemy is exactly level and coincident in XY. Retail's vectoangles yields ideal_viewangles = (-270, 0, 0); EA_VectorToAngles yields (0, 0, 0), so the bot looks level instead of straight down. For every ordinary chase direction retail stores integral degrees (e.g. pitch -13, yaw 47) while ours stores -12.7431/47.2096, so the view-interpolation deltas fed to EA_View differ every frame.

**Fix.** Delete `EA_VectorToAngles` (src/botlib/ea/ea_main.c:85-118) and have `EA_LookAtPoint` (ea_main.c:665) call `Vector2Angles` from src/botlib/common/l_utils.c:63, adding `#include "botlib/common/l_utils.h"` to ea_main.c. Vector2Angles already reproduces 0x10041790 exactly, including the `(float)(int)` truncation on both axes, the +360 folds, and the -90/-270 degenerate pitches. Note that tests/ea/test_ea_inputs.c:295-353 (`compute_expected_angles` / `test_ea_view_helpers_override_input`) encodes the current non-truncating behaviour and must be updated to expect whole-degree yaw/pitch and the negated retail pitch sign convention.

## `src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c`

### HIGH BotRoamGoal measures the direction/length from trace.endpos instead of the randomized endpoint

- status: **applied**
- retail: `BotRoamGoal` @ `0x10022a60`
- HLIL: 28208-28232
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmq2.c:954-960 (`VectorSubtract(endpos, bs->origin, dir); len = VectorNormalize(dir); if (len > 100.0f) { v19 = trace.fraction * len - 40.0f; ... }`)
- ours: `src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:3444`

**Divergence.** BotRoamGoal (retail 0x10022a60, HLIL 28208-28232) measures the roam candidate direction from the *randomized* endpoint, not from trace.endpos. HLIL 28211 computes `var_12c - *(arg1 + 0x694)` at 0x10022bf1, before the trace result is copied into the separate buffer var_fc at 0x10022c03; dir[1]/dir[2] (28215/28216) likewise come from var_128_1/var_124_2. Consequently `len = VectorNormalize(dir)` is the full untraced ray length, the `len > 100` gate (28219-28225) uses that full length, and the rescale (28231-28232) is `VectorScale(dir, trace.fraction * len - 40, dir)` where var_f4 is trace.fraction. Our src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:3444 subtracts trace.endpos instead, so `length` is already fraction*fullLen: the gate at 3446 rejects candidates retail accepts, and the scale at 3452 becomes fraction^2*fullLen - 40. Example: bot at O, randomized endpoint 600 units out, wall at fraction 0.2 -> retail len 600 (gate passes), goal 80 units out along dir; ours length 120 (gate passes), scale 120*0.2 - 40 = -16, goal 16 units *behind* the bot. At fraction 0.15 ours rejects the candidate (length 90 <= 100) while retail accepts and places the goal 50 units out. Worst case is a startsolid trace (Q2_Trace returns fraction 0, endpos == start): ours always rejects; retail places the goal 40 units behind the bot. The two agree only when fraction == 1. Observable twice over: a different accompany-idle view target (bot_interface.c:6931/7098/7255 feed the roam goal into Vector2Angles for held view angles) and a different loop-iteration count, hence a different rand() consumption count (5 BotAI_LongTermGoalRandom calls per extra iteration), desynchronizing the RNG stream for all later decisions.

**Failure scenario.** Bot at origin O, randomized endpoint 600 units away, wall at 20% of the ray so trace.fraction = 0.2. Retail: len = 600 > 100 passes the gate, dir is scaled by 0.2*600 - 40 = 80, so the roam goal lands 80 units out. Ours: length = 120, still > 100, but the scale is 120*0.2 - 40 = -16, so the goal lands 16 units BEHIND the bot. With fraction = 0.15 (len 600 -> 90) ours rejects the candidate entirely at the `length <= 100.0f` continue on line 3446 while retail accepts it. Different roam view target for the accompany idle branch, and a different number of loop iterations, hence a different rand() consumption count.

**Fix.** One-token change at src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:3444 — `best_origin` still holds the randomized endpoint there (it is only overwritten at line 3453), so no additional vector is needed: VectorSubtract(best_origin, state->last_client_update.origin, direction); Leave lines 3445-3453 as they are: `length` is then the full untraced ray length, the `length <= 100.0f` continue at 3446 matches retail's `len > 100` gate, and `VectorScale(direction, length * trace.fraction - 40.0f, direction)` at 3452 matches retail's `trace.fraction * len - 40.0f`. (Retail also calls VectorNormalize before the gate rather than after, but the normalized vector is discarded when the gate fails, so our order is unobservable.)

### HIGH BotAddressedToBot uses a different team predicate than retail's BotSameTeam (teamplay compares only the skin suffix; MODELTEAMS is checked before SKINTEAMS)

- status: **applied**
- retail: `BotAddressedToBot / BotSameTeam` @ `0x10026be0 / 0x10023550`
- HLIL: 31070, 28579-28687
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmq2.c:1188-1217 (BotSameTeam) and :2384-2386 (BotAddressedToBot)
- ours: `src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:3721`

**Divergence.** Retail BotAddressedToBot (0x10026be0) gates on BotSameTeam(bs, client+1) directly (HLIL L31070 `j_sub_10023550(arg1, eax + 1)`; L31072-31073 returns 0 when it fails), and BotNumTeamMates (0x100238f0) does the same per client (ref be_ai2_dmq2.c:1234). Our BotAI_ConsoleAddressedToBot (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:3796) and BotAI_ConsoleTeamPlayerCount (:3744) instead call BotAI_ConsoleClientIsTeammate (:3683), a wholly separate predicate, even though a faithful BotAI_SameTeam already exists at :454. The two differ in six observable ways: 1. `teamplay` comparator. Retail (HLIL L28597-28605) does `_strcmpi(ClientSkin(bs->client), ClientSkin(number-1))` on the FULL skin string and returns immediately. Ours (:3721-3724) routes `teamplay` into BotAI_ConsoleSkinTeamMatches (:3662), which compares only the substring from the first '/'. 2. dmflags precedence. Retail tests `(dmflags & 0x40) || ctf` first (L28619) and reaches `dmflags & 0x80` only in the `else` (L28635). Ours tests 0x80 first (:3717), so with both bits set the model-prefix path wins instead of the skin-suffix path. 3. `teamplay_shell` (HLIL L28562-28579, `((renderfx_a ^ renderfx_b) & 0x1c00) == 0`) is absent from BotAI_ConsoleClientIsTeammate, though the libvar is registered at :2521. 4. `ch` (HLIL L28679-28686, returns 1 when `modelindex3` DIFFERS) is absent, though the libvar is registered at :2513. 5. Invented ctf handling. Ours classifies skins by `strstr("ctf_r")`/`strstr("ctf_b")` and returns early at :3704-3713. Retail's `ctf` libvar has no such role in BotSameTeam - it only ORs into the 0x40 skin-suffix condition (L28619). 6. Invented self short-circuit at :3690-3693 (`client == state->client_number` -> true) and a dropped `AAS_EntityInfo(entnum).number == 0 -> 0` guard (L28562). Under `ch != 0` retail returns 0 for self (self modelindex3 == self modelindex3 fails L28685), where ours returns true. The semantic-team branch at :3696-3700 does not mask any of this: `bot_client_state_t::team` (bot_state.h:123) is assigned only at bot_state.c:331 (`= -1`), so `state->team >= 0` is never true and the divergent path always runs. Failure scenarios, both confirmed: (a) teamplay=1, dmflags=0, ctf=0, bot skin "male/red", speaker skin "female/red" - retail's full-skin _strcmpi fails so every team command (help/accompany/defend/camp/patrol/lead) from that player is dropped, while ours matches on "/red" and the bot adopts the LTG; (b) dmflags=0xC0, teamplay=0, ctf=0, "male/red" vs "male/blue" - retail takes the 0x40 suffix path and reports different teams, ours takes the 0x80 prefix path and reports the same team.

**Failure scenario.** teamplay libvar = 1, bot skin "male/red", speaking client's skin "female/red". Retail: _strcmpi("male/red","female/red") != 0 -> BotSameTeam returns 0 -> BotAddressedToBot returns 0 -> every team command from that player (help/accompany/defend/camp/patrol/join-team) is ignored. Ours: the suffix compare "/red" == "/red" succeeds -> the bot obeys the order and adopts an LTG. Second case: dmflags = 0xC0 (both team bits), skins "male/red" vs "male/blue" - retail takes the 0x40 skin-suffix path and reports different teams, ours takes the 0x80 model-prefix path first and reports the same team.

**Fix.** Delete BotAI_ConsoleClientIsTeammate (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:3683-3728) and have both call sites use the already-correct BotAI_SameTeam (:454), which reproduces retail's full precedence including teamplay_shell, ch, the full-skin teamplay _strcmpi, and the 0x40||ctf-before-0x80 order: - bot_interface.c:3794-3799 -> `if (source_client < 0 || !BotAI_SameTeam(state, source_client + 1))` (the `+ 1` matches HLIL L31070 `j_sub_10023550(arg1, eax + 1)`). - bot_interface.c:3737-3750 -> call `BotAI_SameTeam(state, client + 1)` in the loop, matching ref BotNumTeamMates (be_ai2_dmq2.c:1230-1236). While there, note that retail bounds that loop by `botstate.num_clients`, not by the client capacity, and tests `strlen(clientsettings[i].netname)` - worth confirming BotState_ClientCapacity() is the right bound, but that is a separate finding. BotAI_ConsoleSkinTeamMatches (:3662) and BotAI_ConsoleModelTeamMatches (:~3640) then become unused duplicates of BotAI_SkinTeamMatches (:425) and BotAI_ModelTeamMatches (:404) and should be removed. If for some reason the console layer must keep its own predicate, the minimum correct shape mirrors HLIL L28562-28688 in this exact order: entity-number-zero guard -> teamplay_shell renderfx&0x1c00 -> ch modelindex3-differs -> teamplay full-string Q_stricmp (own branch, immediate return) -> (dmflags & 0x40 || ctf) skin suffix -> else if (dmflags & 0x80) model prefix -> false. The ctf_r/ctf_b strstr block at :3704-3713 and the self short-circuit at :3690-3693 must be dropped in either case; retail has neither.

### HIGH Battle Retreat never runs BotLongTermGoal: no LTG-type dispatch and no item-goal pop/reselect tail

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AINode_Battle_Retreat / BotLongTermGoal` @ `0x10020600 (call at 0x100207a6) -> 0x1001d760`
- HLIL: 26624 (0x100207a6); item tail 25193-25232 (0x1001e60e-0x1001e6dd)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmnet.c:1498 and 252-607
- ours: `src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:6565`

**Divergence.** Battle Retreat (AINode_Battle_Retreat, 0x10020600) resolves its goal with the full `BotLongTermGoal(bs, tfl, retreat=1)` at 0x100207a6 (HLIL 26624), where `tfl` is the Battle-Retreat mask 0x18FBE (|0x4000 with `usehook`). Inside BotLongTermGoal (0x1001d760, HLIL 24678-25494) `retreat` suppresses exactly three branches — ltgtype 1 `help` (24689), ltgtype 2 `accompany` (24780), and ltgtype 3 `defend` (25438: `if ((eax:1.b & 0x41) != 0 || arg3 != 0) goto label_1001dfd4`, which is additionally gated on `AAS_Time() > bs->defendaway_time`). ltgtype 4 (captureflag), 5 (rush base, itself gated on `AAS_Time() > bs->rushbaseaway_time`), 6 (camp) and 7 (patrol) run verbatim under retreat=1, including their chats, teamgoal_time expiry `ltgtype = 0` writes, patrol-waypoint advance and camp crouch/water/camp_stop handling. ltgtype 0/1/2/3 fall through to the ordinary item tail at 0x1001e60e (HLIL 25192-25232), which (a) sets `bs->ltg_time = 0` when the stack is empty, when BotTouchingGoal succeeds, or when BotItemGoalInVisButNotVisible succeeds, (b) when `AAS_Time() > bs->ltg_time` calls BotPopGoal then `BotChooseLTGItem(&bs->goalstate, bs->origin, bs->inventory, tfl)` — note it passes Battle Retreat's own tfl, not Seek LTG's — setting `ltg_time = AAS_Time() + 20` on success and calling BotResetAvoidGoals + BotResetAvoidReach on failure, and (c) returns the refreshed BotGetTopGoal. Our BATTLE_RETREAT case (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:6563-6578) does none of this. It either takes BotAI_CommitCarriedFlagRetreatGoal (only when BotAI_CarryingFlag is nonzero) or reads the top goal, and calls AI_GoalBotlib_ChooseLTG only when the goal stack is already empty. It never pops, never reads or writes `state->long_term_goal_time`, never resets either avoid layer, and never consults `state->ltg_type`. The behaviour exists in BotAI_ResolveTeamLongTermGoal (6753) and BotAI_GetItemLongTermGoal (6195), but both are reachable only from BotAI_RunGoalMovement (8159, 8202) on the Seek LTG path; neither the node dispatcher (9160-9179) nor BotAI_RunBattleRetreatMovement (8599-8678) supplies it. Two further precision points the original claim omitted: (i) our BotAI_CommitCarriedFlagRetreatGoal (6316) fuses BotCTFRetreatGoals (0x100263D0) with retail's ltgtype-5 branch but gates on "currently carrying a flag" instead of retail's `bs->ltgtype == 5 && AAS_Time() > bs->rushbaseaway_time`, and omits that branch's BotTouchingGoal handling (BotResetAvoidReach + `rushbaseaway_time = AAS_Time() + rand()*10 + 5` for a carrier, `ltgtype = 0` otherwise) and its `AAS_Time() > teamgoal_time -> ltgtype = 0` write — so a bot that drops or caps the flag mid-retreat loses the base goal a frame early in ours and retains it in retail; (ii) our BotAI_GetItemLongTermGoal hardcodes `BotAI_LongTermGoalTravelFlags(state)` (which adds the rocket-jump bit), whereas retail's tail uses whatever tfl the caller passed — Battle Retreat's mask has no rocket-jump bit — so simply calling the existing helper from Battle Retreat would introduce a second, smaller divergence.

**Failure scenario.** 1) Bot has an LTG item goal it already picked up, then a fight drives it into Battle Retreat. Retail: BotTouchingGoal sets ltg_time=0, next frame pops the dead goal and BotChooseLTGItem selects a fresh retreat target. Ours: the stale goal stays on top of the stack forever, so the bot retreats toward an item that no longer exists for as long as it stays in Battle Retreat. 2) Bot is under a teammate 'camp here' order (ltgtype 6) and enters Battle Retreat. Retail returns the camp teamgoal (and runs the camp chats/crouch/water checks); ours returns whatever item is on the goal stack, so the ordered position is abandoned during every retreat.

**Fix.** Route Battle Retreat through the same BotLongTermGoal reconstruction Seek LTG uses, parameterised on `retreat` and on the travel mask: 1. Add an `int retreat` parameter to BotAI_ResolveTeamLongTermGoal (bot_interface.c:6753). Gate only `case 1` and `case 2` on `!retreat`, and add `|| retreat` to the existing `case 3` early-out so it falls to the item tail exactly as HLIL 25438 does. Leave cases 4, 5, 6, 7 running unconditionally. 2. Add an `int travel_flags` parameter to BotAI_GetItemLongTermGoal (bot_interface.c:6195) and pass it to AI_GoalBotlib_ChooseLTG instead of the hardcoded BotAI_LongTermGoalTravelFlags(state); update the existing Seek LTG call site (8202) to pass BotAI_LongTermGoalTravelFlags(state) so its behaviour is unchanged. 3. In the BOT_AI_NODE_BATTLE_RETREAT case (6563-6578), keep the pre-goal BotCTFRetreatGoals-equivalent side effect (the `ltg_type != BOT_LTG_RUSH_BASE -> ltg_type = BOT_LTG_RUSH_BASE; rush_base_away_time = 0; team_goal_time = AAS_Time() + 120` block, already inside BotAI_CommitCarriedFlagRetreatGoal), but split it out so it runs before goal resolution rather than instead of it, matching HLIL 26621-26622 (`if (ctf libvar != 0) j_sub_100263d0(arg1)`). Then call BotAI_ResolveTeamLongTermGoal(state, ..., retreat=1); on BOT_TEAM_GOAL_READY use the returned team goal, on BOT_TEAM_GOAL_HANDLED take the existing BOT_AI_FRAME_WORK_BATTLE_RETREAT_IDLE path, and on BOT_TEAM_GOAL_NONE fall through to BotAI_GetItemLongTermGoal(state, &retreat_goal, BotAI_BattleRetreatTravelFlags()) — exactly the shape BotAI_RunGoalMovement already uses at 8159-8227. A false return from the item tail keeps the existing idle path (retail's `if (!goal) { BotChangeViewAngles; return 1; }`). 4. While there, fold retail's ltgtype-5 body into the case-5 arm of BotAI_ResolveTeamLongTermGoal (gate on `ltg_type == 5 && AAS_Time() > rush_base_away_time`, add the BotTouchingGoal branch and the teamgoal_time expiry clear) rather than leaving it in BotAI_CommitCarriedFlagRetreatGoal, so the flag path and the ordered-goal path share one implementation as retail does.

### HIGH BotCTFTeam is never computed - state->team stays -1, so every CTF flag-goal selection takes the "no team" abort path

- status: **applied**
- retail: `BotCTFTeam` @ `0x10023510`
- HLIL: 28547-28551
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmq2.c:1146-1154 (BotCTFTeam) and :2171-2174 (BotCTFSeekGoals defend branch)
- ours: `src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:7012`

**Divergence.** Retail BotCTFTeam (0x10023510, HLIL 28547-28551) returns 1 when strstr(ClientSkin(bs->client), "ctf_r") matches and 2 otherwise. Our reconstruction implements it for only one of retail's four consumers, and the field it substitutes at the other three is a constant. `bot_client_state_t::team` is written exactly once in all of src/ — to -1, in BotState_ResetForNewMap (src/botlib/interface/bot_state.c:331) — and never assigned again, so every `state->team` read yields -1. The three CTF goal-selection consumers therefore all misbehave: - HLIL 25151-25190 / 1001e4ef, the ltgtype==4 branch of BotLongTermGoal (ref be_ai2_dmnet.c:222, branch at :519-543 — note this is BotLongTermGoal, not AINode_Seek_LTG as originally stated): retail sets result = ctf_redflag then flips to ctf_blueflag when BotCTFTeam()==1, with no team-validity and no flag-availability guard anywhere in the branch. Ours is BotAI_ResolveTeamLongTermGoal case 4 (bot_interface.c:6753, guard at :7011-7016), where `(state->team != 1 && state->team != 2)` is always true, so it sets ltg_type = 0 and returns BOT_TEAM_GOAL_HANDLED; line 8191 then routes to BotAI_RunLongTermIdleView and the bot only turns its view. A "get the flag" order (match type 7) or a self-selected ltg_type 4 is dropped on the next frame. - HLIL 25244-25247 / 1001e567, the ltgtype==5 branch of the same routine (ref be_ai2_dmnet.c:545-572): same tautological abort at bot_interface.c:7035-7040. - HLIL 30787-30798 / 1002659a, BotCTFSeekGoals' defend branch (ref be_ai2_dmq2.c:2171-2174): retail copies ctf_redflag when BotCTFTeam()==1, else ctf_blueflag. Ours (BotAI_SelectAutomaticCTFGoal, bot_interface.c:4490) always picks blue_flag, so red-team bots defend the enemy base. A fourth site, bot_interface.c:6333-6339 (BotAI_CommitCarriedFlagRetreatGoal), is our merge of retail BotCTFRetreatGoals (0x100263D0, ref be_ai2_dmq2.c:2124-2139, which only promotes ltgtype and never resolves a goal) with BotLongTermGoal's ltgtype==5 resolution. It always returns false; its sole caller at bot_interface.c:6565-6566 has carrying_flag true, so the ChooseLTG fallback at :6570 is skipped and the frame falls through to BOT_AI_FRAME_WORK_BATTLE_RETREAT_IDLE at :6579. A flag carrier under Battle Retreat never gets a retreat goal. Retail's fourth consumer, 0x1002876c (chat synonym context, ref be_ai2_dmq2.c:3027), IS correctly implemented in our tree via the skin at bot_interface.c:3485-3499 — it needs no change. Beyond the constant team value, the `(state->team != 1 && state->team != 2)` conjuncts at :6334, :7012 and :7036 are invented: retail's ltgtype 4 and 5 branches contain no such abort at all.

**Failure scenario.** CTF map with both flags loaded, ctf libvar = 1, bot ordered "get the flag" (match type 7) or self-selecting ltg_type 4 through BotCTFSeekGoals. Retail resolves BotCTFTeam from the bot's skin, targets the enemy flag goal and paths to it for 180 s. Ours reaches bot_interface.c:7011-7015, `(state->team != 1 && state->team != 2)` is true, so it sets state->ltg_type = 0 and returns BOT_TEAM_GOAL_HANDLED - the bot silently drops the order on the very next frame. Same for ltg_type 5 (rush base, line 7036) and for BotAI_CommitCarriedFlagRetreatGoal (line 6334), which always returns false so a flag carrier never gets a retreat goal. In BotAI_SelectAutomaticCTFGoal (line 4490) the defend branch always picks blue_flag, so red-team bots defend the enemy base.

**Fix.** Add a BotCTFTeam helper next to BotAI_ConsoleSynonymContext in src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c, mirroring retail 0x10023510 exactly (note retail passes the raw ClientSkin result to strstr with no NULL check, but BotState_ClientSkin already returns a valid string for in-range clients, so a NULL guard is unobservable): static int BotAI_CTFTeam(const bot_client_state_t *state) { const char *skin = BotState_ClientSkin(state->client_number); return (skin != NULL && strstr(skin, "ctf_r") != NULL) ? 1 : 2; } Then, at each of the four sites, replace the `state->team` read with `BotAI_CTFTeam(state)` AND delete the `(state->team != 1 && state->team != 2)` conjunct outright — the helper can only return 1 or 2, so the test is dead, and retail's branches carry no such abort in the first place: - bot_interface.c:4490 -> `state->team_goal = BotAI_CTFTeam(state) == 1 ? red_flag : blue_flag;` - bot_interface.c:6334 -> drop the team conjunct from the guard; :6339 -> `*goal = BotAI_CTFTeam(state) == 1 ? red_flag : blue_flag;` - bot_interface.c:7012 -> drop the team conjunct; :7018 -> `*goal = BotAI_CTFTeam(state) == 1 ? blue_flag : red_flag;` - bot_interface.c:7036 -> drop the team conjunct; :7042 -> `*goal = BotAI_CTFTeam(state) == 1 ? red_flag : blue_flag;` Do NOT take the claim's alternative of populating `state->team` from the skin. BotAI_ConsoleClientIsTeammate (bot_interface.c:3696-3700) gates on `state->team >= 0 && other->team >= 0` before delegating to BotInterface_SameTeam (:353-362, `lhs->team == rhs->team`). Assigning 1/2 to every bot would make that branch live in non-CTF games too, and any two bots whose skins lack "ctf_r" would both be team 2 and thus register as teammates in plain deathmatch — a regression retail does not have, since retail derives the CTF team on demand inside BotCTFTeam and never caches it in bot_state_t. Leaving `state->team` at -1 preserves the existing skin/dmflags fallback at :3702-3720. Separately (adjacent, outside this finding): the surviving `!BotAI_ConsoleCTFFlagGoals(&red_flag, &blue_flag)` half of the guards at :7011 and :7035 is also absent from retail's BotLongTermGoal ltgtype 4/5 branches (HLIL 25151-25190, 25244-25267), which resolve the flag goal with no areanum test. That is worth auditing on its own rather than folding into this change.

### MEDIUM BotSameTeam's DF_MODELTEAMS comparison is case-insensitive; retail uses strncmp

- status: **applied**
- retail: `BotSameTeam` @ `0x10023550`
- HLIL: 28635-28678
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmq2.c:1214 (`if ( !strncmp((const char *)ClientSkin(bs->client), (const char *)ClientSkin(v8), MaxCount) )`)
- ours: `src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:415`

**Divergence.** In retail BotSameTeam @ 0x10023550, the DF_MODELTEAMS branch (dmflags & 0x80, HLIL 28635-28678, addresses 0x100236ec..0x100237ab) measures both client skins up to the first '/' (falling back to strlen when there is no '/'), requires the two prefix lengths to be equal, and then compares the prefixes with StrCompareN @ 0x100456b0 -- the MSVC strncmp intrinsic (repe cmpsb, raw byte equality at HLIL 55585), which is CASE-SENSITIVE. This deliberately differs from the two sibling branches: the DF_SKINTEAMS/ctf branch at 0x1002380e and the teamplay branch at 0x10023683 both call sub_10045cb0 @ 0x10045cb0, which case-folds each byte (-0x41 / sbb / &0x20 / +0x41, HLIL 56124-56137) and is therefore _strcmpi, CASE-INSENSITIVE. Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmq2.c:1214 (strncmp) against :1202 and :1190 (_strcmpi). Our BotAI_ModelTeamMatches (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:404-416) instead calls Q_strnicmp(left, right, left_length) at line 415, which src/shared/q_platform.h:26-33 resolves to _strnicmp / strncasecmp -- case-insensitive. The surrounding prefix measurement, the length-equality gate, and the caller's branch precedence in BotAI_SameTeam all match retail, so this single compare is the whole divergence; BotAI_SkinTeamMatches correctly stays on Q_stricmp. Failure scenario (unchanged and verified reachable): dmflags has 0x80 set and 0x40 clear, ctf/teamplay/ch/teamplay_shell all 0, bot skin "male/grunt", other client skin "Male/grunt". Both prefixes are 4 characters, so the length gate passes, but retail's strncmp("male/grunt", "Male/grunt", 4) != 0 and BotSameTeam returns 0 -- the bot targets that client as an enemy in BotFindEnemy and rejects his team commands in BotAddressedToBot. Ours returns 1 and treats him as a teammate. Skins are stored verbatim from userinfo (ref/gladiator-bot-restored/game/bl_main.c:440-442) with no case normalization, and ClientSkin @ 0x10028f80 merely returns the stored buffer, so mixed-case model names genuinely reach this compare. Correction to the claim as filed: BotAI_ConsoleModelTeamMatches (bot_interface.c:3636-3653) is NOT a reconstruction of a retail duplicate. Retail BotAddressedToBot @ 0x10026BE0 delegates to BotSameTeam directly (ref be_ai2_dmq2.c:2384) and contains no team-matching code of its own. Our console helper is an invented parallel implementation that also adds a semantic-team short-circuit, a ctf_r/ctf_b substring test, and tests modelteams BEFORE skinteams/teamplay -- the reverse of retail's order.

**Failure scenario.** dmflags has DF_MODELTEAMS (0x80) set and DF_SKINTEAMS clear, ctf/teamplay/ch/teamplay_shell all 0. Bot skin "male/grunt", other client skin "Male/grunt". Retail: lengths match (4 == 4) but strncmp("male/grunt","Male/grunt",4) != 0, so BotSameTeam returns 0 - the bot will target that client as an enemy in BotFindEnemy and will refuse team commands from him in BotAddressedToBot. Ours returns 1, so the bot treats him as a teammate and never attacks him.

**Fix.** Primary fix: in BotAI_ModelTeamMatches (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:415), replace Q_strnicmp(left, right, left_length) with the case-sensitive strncmp(left, right, left_length), keeping the length-equality gate on line 414 as-is. Leave BotAI_SkinTeamMatches (line 429) on Q_stricmp -- that branch really does use _strcmpi in retail. Secondary, and preferable to a like-for-like patch of line 3652: the console path should not carry its own copy of this logic at all. Retail BotAddressedToBot calls BotSameTeam, so BotAI_ConsoleClientIsTeammate (bot_interface.c:3683-3728) should call BotAI_SameTeam(state, client + 1) and drop BotAI_ConsoleModelTeamMatches / BotAI_ConsoleSkinTeamMatches together with the semantic-team short-circuit and the ctf substring branch -- that also repairs the reversed modelteams/skinteams precedence at lines 3716-3725. If that consolidation is deferred as out of scope, then as a stopgap change line 3652 to strncmp so the two copies at least agree, and file the precedence and extra-branch divergences separately.

### MEDIUM BotMatchMessage case 13 (leave subteam) skips BotEnterChat when the bot has no subteam

- status: **applied**
- retail: `BotMatchMessage` @ `0x10026f10`
- HLIL: 31523-31542
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmq2.c:2803-2811
- ours: `src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:5151`

**Divergence.** BotMatchMessage case 0xd (leave subteam) at 0x10027a40 runs BotEnterChat(&bs->chatstate, bs->client, 1) unconditionally: the inline-strlen guard at 0x10027a7c covers only BotInitialChat("leftteam") at 0x10027a8c, while BotEnterChat at 0x10027aa1 and the teamleader clear at 0x10027aaf sit at the outer indentation level (HLIL 31536-31542; corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmq2.c:2806-2810). Our BotAI_ConsoleLeaveSubteam (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:5147-5154) puts both calls inside `if (state->subteam[0] != '\0')` because BotAI_ConsoleEnterInitialTeamChat (bot_interface.c:3957-3970) fuses BotInitialChat and BotEnterChat, so the empty-subteam path performs no BotEnterChat at all. Retail's BotEnterChat (0x1002ea80) is itself strlen-guarded on chatstate->chatmessage, so this is a no-op divergence when nothing is staged - it does NOT emit an empty say_team. It becomes observable whenever a chat is staged but not yet flushed, which the deferred-chat paths produce routinely: BotChat_EnterGame (HLIL 27640-27649), exit_game (27688), start_level (27727), end_level (27767), death/kill/random (27837, 27909, 27965-27968) all call BotInitialChat and return without entering, leaving the flush to the Stand node at bs->stand_time (HLIL 25614; ref be_ai2_dmnet.c:663). Concrete failure: teamplay is on, the bot stages its "enter_game" greeting (or a death taunt, or a selected reply) and enters Stand; before stand_time expires a teammate says "<bot>, leave the team" with the bot's subteam empty. Retail immediately flushes the staged line via EA_SayTeam and clears chatmessage, so the later Stand-expiry BotEnterChat sends nothing. Ours emits nothing now and later sends the same line through EA_Say instead - different destination and different timing.

**Failure scenario.** A teammate says "leave the team" to a bot that never joined a subteam (teamleader empty - the normal case). Retail still calls BotEnterChat, which flushes whatever message is currently staged in the bot's chat state (or sends an empty say_team when nothing is staged) and clears the staged chat. Ours emits nothing and leaves any staged chat message pending, so the bot's next chat output differs.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c, stop using the fused helper in BotAI_ConsoleLeaveSubteam and split the two calls so only BotInitialChat is guarded: static void BotAI_ConsoleLeaveSubteam(bot_client_state_t *state) { if (state == NULL || state->chat_state == NULL) { return; } if (state->subteam[0] != '\0') { BotInitialChat(state->chat_state, "leftteam", state->subteam, NULL); } BotEnterChat(state->chat_state, state->client_number, BOT_CONSOLE_CHAT_TEAM); memset(state->subteam, 0, sizeof(int)); } Order matters and matches retail 0x10027a8c -> 0x10027aa1 -> 0x10027aaf: BotInitialChat consumes state->subteam as its variable, BotEnterChat runs next unconditionally, and the slot is cleared last. The existing unconditional clear is already correct and must stay outside the guard. Do not touch BotAI_ConsoleEnterInitialTeamChat itself - its other callers (cases 0xc and the LTG report cases) do match retail's fused InitialChat+EnterChat shape. Leave the 4-byte memset as is; retail stores one byte there but both leave a NUL at index 0.

### MEDIUM BotChat_Death and BotChat_Kill substitute the raw client netname instead of the EasyClientName-normalised one

- status: **applied**
- retail: `BotChat_Death / BotChat_Kill` @ `0x10022160 / 0x100222e0`
- HLIL: 27806-27813, 27879-27885
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmq2.c:705-709 and :750-754
- ours: `src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:5486`

**Divergence.** BotChat_Death (0x10022160, HLIL 27806-27813) and BotChat_Kill (0x100222e0, HLIL 27879-27885) both build the chat's variable-0 string as: int v4 = *(bs + 0x1064); /* bs->enemy */ if (v4) EasyClientName(v4 - 1, name); /* sub_10021860 via j_sub_10021860 */ else strcpy(name, ""); BotInitialChat(&bs->chatstate, type, name, (char *)0); sub_10021860 is EasyClientName (HLIL 27300-27460; ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmq2.c:454, annotated "gladiator.dll: 10021860..10021A12"). It copies ClientName(client) into a 128-byte buffer, masks every byte with 0x7f, deletes every space, then — if both '[' and ']' are present — removes the span from the earlier bracket through the later bracket inclusive, then strips a leading two-character "mr" in any case combination, then walks the string keeping [a-z0-9_], lower-casing [A-Z], and deleting every other character. Our BotAI_ConstructDeathChat (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:5482-5487) and BotAI_ConstructKillChat (:5540-5545) instead pass BotState_ClientName(client), which returns the untouched netname from the client-settings table (src/botlib/interface/bot_state.c:805-823). The normalisation is not recovered anywhere else: neither caller (bot_interface.c:9001 respawn path, :6460 BATTLE_FIGHT dead-enemy exit) nor BotInitialChat (src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:8559 -> BotChat_InitialChatV :8513) touches the variable. A faithful port of the normaliser already exists as BotAI_ConsoleEasyClientName (bot_interface.c:4991) and is used on ten other chat paths, including the lifecycle chat at :5446 — these two are the only chat sites that skip it. The divergence is observable because the retail chat templates interpolate variable 0 for exactly these types (dev_tools/assets/ichat.h: DEATH_INSULT1 "i vote ", 0," as the camper of the day"; KILL_INSULT3 "stick to playing duke nukem, ", 0; KILL_INSULT5 "who gave ", 0," the nerve to get killed here?"). For a killer/victim named "Mr [CLAN] Bob", retail substitutes "bob" and we substitute "Mr [CLAN] Bob", so the say/say_team line the server broadcasts differs. The enemy == 0 empty-string case already matches.

**Failure scenario.** Bot is killed by a player whose netname is "Mr [CLAN] Bob". Retail emits the death chat with the variable "bob"; ours emits it with "Mr [CLAN] Bob", so the say/say_team text the server broadcasts differs. Same for kill_insult/kill_praise/kill_telefrag with the victim's name.

**Fix.** In both functions, replace the raw-name lookup with the existing normaliser and drop the redundant capacity guard so the range check (and its retail warning) stays where retail put it — inside ClientName. In BotAI_ConstructDeathChat, replace src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:5482-5487: const char *killer_name = ""; int killer_client = state->combat.current_enemy - 1; if (killer_client >= 0 && killer_client < BotState_ClientCapacity()) { killer_name = BotState_ClientName(killer_client); } with: char killer_name[0x20]; killer_name[0] = '\0'; if (state->combat.current_enemy != 0) { BotAI_ConsoleEasyClientName(state->combat.current_enemy - 1, killer_name, sizeof(killer_name)); } and pass killer_name to BotInitialChat at :5501-5504 unchanged. Apply the identical edit in BotAI_ConstructKillChat at :5540-5545 for victim_name, and simplify the call at :5559-5562 to pass victim_name directly (the `victim_name != NULL ? victim_name : ""` ternary becomes dead once it is an array). Two notes. BotAI_ConsoleEasyClientName is defined at :4991, above both call sites, so no forward declaration is needed. Retail's caller buffer is a 32-byte stack slot (var_20 at [ebp-20h]) that EasyClientName strcpy's into unbounded; keeping char[0x20] preserves the retail size while the bounded helper avoids reproducing the overflow, which is a divergence only in already-undefined territory.

### MEDIUM MOVERESULT_MOVEMENTVIEWSET suppresses the ideal_viewangles update in Seek LTG/NBG/Activate and Battle Chase; retail only suppresses the view turn

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AINode_Seek_NBG / AINode_Seek_LTG / AINode_Battle_Chase` @ `0x1001f290 (0x1001f5d7), 0x1001f760 (0x1001fba4), 0x100200a0 (0x10020471)`
- HLIL: 26006-26008; 26243-26244; 26534-26536
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmnet.c:1008-1009, 1157-1158, 1419-1420
- ours: `src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:7229`

**Divergence.** In AINode_Seek_NBG (0x1001f290, HLIL 25931-26008), AINode_Seek_LTG (0x1001f760, HLIL 26190-26244 + label_1001fbac at 26148-26150) and AINode_Battle_Chase (0x100200a0, HLIL 26502-26536), retail evaluates the `moveresult.flags & 3` ideal-viewangles block unconditionally and uses MOVERESULT_MOVEMENTVIEWSET (flag 8) solely to skip that frame's BotChangeViewAngles (sub_10029150) call. Our BotAI_ApplyLongTermMoveResultView (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:7229-7235) returns before the entire ideal-view computation when the flag is set for the two Seek nodes, and BotAI_RunBattleChaseMovement (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:8487) nests the whole view block inside the same guard, so the retained bs->ideal_viewangles (ai_dm.c:1378) is never updated on those frames. The activate-entity exemption at line 7227 is correct (0x1001f169 turns unconditionally). The observable consequence is not "the frame after the jump" — any frame where the node re-runs its movement recomputes the ideal angles in both builds. The divergence shows up on a later frame that advances the view turn WITHOUT recomputing the ideal angles: AINode_Seek_LTG's no-goal branch (0x1001f975 `if (esi == 0) goto label_1001fbac`, our BotAI_RunLongTermIdleView at bot_interface.c:8022-8054) and AINode_Stand (0x1001ecb7) both call BotChangeViewAngles against the retained value. Concretely: a bot with the rocket-jump travel flag executes BotTravel_RocketJump (src/botlib/ai_move/bot_move.c:1703, ref be_ai_move.c:1539, the only setter of flag 8), so retail leaves bs->ideal_viewangles = vectoangles(movementviewtarget or moveresult.movedir) with roll halved; if its long-term goal then evaporates, retail turns toward that jump-direction target while ours turns toward the pre-jump value. Retail's BotChangeViewAngles also normalises ideal_viewangles[0] in place (HLIL 32375-32377), so the retained value is genuinely load-bearing.

**Failure scenario.** Bot performs a rocket jump: BotTravel_RocketJump (src/botlib/ai_move/bot_move.c:1703) returns flags == MOVERESULT_MOVEMENTVIEWSET alone. Retail still stores the movement view target (or vectoangles(movedir)) into bs->ideal_viewangles, with the roll halved, and only skips this frame's incremental turn. Ours leaves ideal_viewangles at whatever it held before the jump, so on the frame after the jump completes BotChangeViewAngles turns toward a stale pre-jump target instead of the next reachability.

**Fix.** Split the two decisions in both places. 1. src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:7229-7235 — delete the early `return false`. Run the ideal-view block exactly as retail does (`flags & 3` -> copy result->ideal_viewangles; else the waiting-roam arm for the Seek nodes; else BotMovementViewTarget/movedir with roll halved), and make every exit of BotAI_ApplyLongTermMoveResultView return the turn decision instead of `true`/`false`: `return activate_node || (result->flags & MOVERESULT_MOVEMENTVIEWSET) == 0;`. Note the roam arm's `if (BotAI_LongTermGoalRandom() >= thinktime * 0.8f) return true;` at line 7248-7251 must become a fall-through to that common return (retail's random gate at 0x1001f4aa / 0x1001fad2 skips only the ideal write, not the turn decision). 2. src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:8487-8501 — unnest: always execute the `flags & (MOVEMENTVIEW|SWIMVIEW)` / BotAI_SetBattleMovementGoalView selection, and wrap only `AI_DMState_ChangeViewAngles(state->dm_state, state, thinktime)` in `if ((result.flags & MOVERESULT_MOVEMENTVIEWSET) == 0)`. While there, match retail's order by moving the `move_state->areanum == state->combat.last_enemy_area` chase_time reset (lines 8480-8485) to sit between the view block and the turn (HLIL 26531 sits between 26529 and 26534); that reorder is behaviourally inert but keeps the routine aligned. No change to the activate-entity path.

### MEDIUM Seek NBG aims at the top goal when the goal stack has no second entry; retail aims with a NULL goal (i.e. falls back to movedir)

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AINode_Seek_NBG` @ `0x1001f290`
- HLIL: 25938-25948 (0x1001f504-0x1001f532)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmnet.c:980-983
- ours: `src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:7264`

**Divergence.** AINode_Seek_NBG @0x1001f290 (HLIL 25938-25948, bytes 0x1001f504-0x1001f52d): retail loads the view goal with BotGetSecondGoal into EDI, and when that is NULL it calls BotGetTopGoal but never moves the result into EDI (`8b f8` is present after the first call, absent after the second), so it pushes a NULL goal into BotMovementViewTarget. BotMovementViewTarget (sub_10031270, HLIL 38790) rejects a NULL goal and returns 0, so with a single-entry goal stack retail always takes the `vectoangles(moveresult.movedir, ideal_viewangles)` fallback and never the reachability-end aim. The discarded BotGetTopGoal call has no side effects, so it need not be reproduced. Our BotAI_ApplyLongTermMoveResultView (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:7263) instead seeds `view_goal` from the node's top goal and only overwrites it when GetSecondGoal succeeds, so on a single-entry stack we aim at the next reachability end (reach.end with z-15) instead of the move direction. Reachable whenever goalstacktop == 1 in Seek NBG — e.g. Seek NBG -> BotFindEnemy -> BotEmptyGoalStack -> Battle_Fight pushes one NBG goal -> Battle_NBG -> enemy lost -> AIEnter_Seek_NBG; also in team/CTF LTG modes where the LTG lives in bs->teamgoal and is never pushed. Only the NBG node is affected: Seek LTG (0x1001fb3d) and the activate node (0x1001f0c1) pass their own goal unchanged, matching our helper's non-NBG path.

**Failure scenario.** Bot is in Seek NBG with exactly one goal on the stack (the normal state right after BotChooseNBGItem pushed one nearby item), moveresult.flags has neither MOVEMENTVIEW/SWIMVIEW nor WAITING set. Retail: BotMovementViewTarget(ms, NULL, ...) returns qfalse (our own port guards `goal == NULL` at src/botlib/ai_move/bot_move.c:415), so ideal_viewangles = vectoangles(moveresult.movedir) — the bot looks where it is walking. Ours: BotMovementViewTarget succeeds against the NBG goal and returns reach.end with z-15, so ideal_viewangles points at the far side of the next reachability. Different aim every frame the bot seeks a nearby item.

**Fix.** In BotAI_ApplyLongTermMoveResultView (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:7263-7279), replace the by-value `bot_goal_t view_goal = *goal;` with a pointer that can be NULL, e.g. `const bot_goal_t *view_goal = goal;` plus a `bot_goal_t second_goal;` buffer, and for `state->ai_node == BOT_AI_NODE_SEEK_NBG` set `view_goal = (AI_GoalBotlib_GetSecondGoal(state->goal_handle, &second_goal) != 0) ? &second_goal : NULL;` (dropping to NULL also when `state->goal_handle <= 0`, since retail's EDI is NULL in that case too). Then pass `view_goal` straight to BotMovementViewTargetHandle — no need to skip the call, because BotMovementViewTargetHandle (src/botlib/ai_move/bot_move.c:2378) forwards NULL and BotMovementViewTarget (:415) already returns 0 for a NULL goal, which reproduces retail's guaranteed movedir fallback. Leave the Seek LTG and activate paths passing `goal`.

### MEDIUM BotAI_SetBattleMovementGoalView drops retail's movedir fallback and the ideal_viewangles[ROLL] *= 0.5

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AINode_Battle_Chase / AINode_Battle_Retreat` @ `0x100200a0 (0x100203f5-0x1002044f), 0x10020600 (0x1002093e-0x100209a0)`
- HLIL: 26502-26522; 26682-26698
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmnet.c:1406-1415 and 1544-1553
- ours: `src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:8305`

**Divergence.** BotAI_SetBattleMovementGoalView (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:8295-8320) drops retail's movedir fallback arm. In AINode_Battle_Chase (0x100203f5-0x1002043b, HLIL 26507-26521) and AINode_Battle_Retreat (0x1002093e-0x10020984, HLIL 26682-26696), when BotMovementViewTarget returns 0 retail still reaches the common vectoangles tail with moveresult.movedir as the direction, writing bs->ideal_viewangles. Our helper returns qfalse at 8311 and writes nothing, so ideal_viewangles keeps the previous frame's value and BotChangeViewAngles keeps turning toward a stale target. Additionally, in the retreat caller (8661-8666) AI_DMState_ChangeViewAngles is gated on the helper's return value, whereas retail's BotChangeViewAngles at 0x100209a6 (HLIL 26699) sits after the if/else and runs on both arms — so on view-target failure our retreat frame also skips the private view turn entirely. The claim's second half is wrong: `ideal_viewangles[ROLL] *= 0.5` at 0x1002044f/0x100209a0 is a dead multiply, because retail's vectoangles (sub_10041790) unconditionally stores 0 into arg2[2] (HLIL 51678, 51683, 51713) and our Vector2Angles does the same (l_utils.c:99); ROLL is also never read by AI_DMChangeViewAngles, which loops PITCH..YAW only (ai_dm.c:367).

**Failure scenario.** Battle Chase with a moveresult that carries no MOVEMENTVIEW/SWIMVIEW flag and a lastreachnum that yields no reachability to the chase goal (BotGetReachabilityToGoal returns 0 — routine when the last enemy area sits behind an avoided reachability). Retail sets ideal_viewangles = vectoangles(moveresult.movedir) with the roll halved; ours leaves ideal_viewangles at the previous frame's value, so BotChangeViewAngles keeps turning toward a stale target. In the succeeding case the roll component is twice retail's, visibly changing the bot's banking while chasing or retreating.

**Fix.** Give BotAI_SetBattleMovementGoalView the bot_moveresult_t (as the Seek-node helper at bot_interface.c:7275-7290 already has it): on BotMovementViewTargetHandle failure, replace the `return qfalse` at 8311 with `VectorCopy(result->movedir, direction)` and fall through to the shared Vector2Angles / AI_DMState_SetIdealViewAngles tail, then always return qtrue. Update both call sites (8496 chase, 8661 retreat) to pass &result; the retreat site's `if (...) { AI_DMState_ChangeViewAngles(...) }` then becomes an unconditional call after the helper, matching 0x100209a6. Do NOT add `viewangles[ROLL] *= 0.5f` for behavioural parity — it is provably a no-op after Vector2Angles zeroes ROLL; add it only if literal source-shape parity with retail is wanted, and note it as dead in the comment.

### MEDIUM Battle Retreat skips BotChangeViewAngles when the movement view target cannot be resolved

- status: **applied**
- retail: `AINode_Battle_Retreat` @ `0x10020600 (0x1002099e-0x100209a6)`
- HLIL: 26696-26699
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmnet.c:1544-1554
- ours: `src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:8661`

**Divergence.** In AINode_Battle_Retreat's low-aggression arm (attack_skill <= 0.3, HLIL 26676-26699), retail unconditionally computes ideal_viewangles and turns: if BotMovementViewTarget succeeds it uses vectoangles(target - origin), and if it FAILS it falls back to vectoangles(moveresult.movedir) (HLIL 26682-26685, var_140 = moveresult base + 0x18 = movedir), then calls BotChangeViewAngles at 26699 in both cases. Our src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:8659-8667 has two defects: BotAI_SetBattleMovementGoalView (8295-8320) returns qfalse at 8311 without the movedir fallback, so ideal_viewangles is never updated on failure; and the caller gates AI_DMState_ChangeViewAngles on that return value, so no view turn happens at all that frame. The same missing movedir fallback also affects the Battle Chase call site at line 8496 (retail equivalent: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmnet.c:1405-1415), though Chase already calls ChangeViewAngles unconditionally. The `ideal_viewangles[2] *= 0.5` at HLIL 26698 is a no-op in practice because Vector2Angles always sets ROLL to 0 (src/botlib/common/l_utils.c:99), so it is a parity nicety, not an observable divergence.

**Failure scenario.** Bot with attack_skill <= 0.3 in Battle Retreat, moveresult without MOVEMENTVIEW/MOVEMENTVIEWSET, and BotMovementViewTarget failing (no reachability to the retreat goal from the last reachability). Retail still advances the view turn toward the movedir-derived angles that frame; ours performs no view turn at all, so the bot's aim freezes for the whole time that condition holds.

**Fix.** Fix the shared helper and the caller's gate together. (1) Give BotAI_SetBattleMovementGoalView the moveresult so it can reproduce retail's fallback: on BotMovementViewTargetHandle failure, use VectorCopy(result->movedir, direction) instead of returning qfalse, then Vector2Angles + (for byte-parity) viewangles[ROLL] *= 0.5f + AI_DMState_SetIdealViewAngles, and always return success. This mirrors the already-correct pattern our own code uses at src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:7275-7289 for the Seek nodes, and simultaneously repairs the Battle Chase call site at line 8496. (2) In BotAI_RunBattleRetreatMovement, hoist AI_DMState_ChangeViewAngles(state->dm_state, state, thinktime) out of the `if` at line 8661 so it runs unconditionally after the helper call, matching HLIL 26699 (and keeping it before AI_DMState_CheckAttack, which is retail's order at 0x100209a6 then 0x100209af).

### LOW BotRecordNodeSwitch is not implemented, so the node-overflow dump prints only its header line

- status: **open**
- retail: `BotRecordNodeSwitch / BotDumpNodeSwitches` @ `0x1001d3a0 / 0x1001d2d0`
- HLIL: 24570-24579; 24509-24565
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_dmnet.c:69-91
- ours: `src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:9050`

**Divergence.** The entire node-switch recording subsystem is absent from our reconstruction, so the 50-switch overflow dump prints only its header line. Retail has three routines and two globals we do not implement at all: - numnodeswitches @ 0x100644a0 and nodeswitch[51][144] @ 0x10064a80 (ref be_ai2_dmnet.c:58-59). - BotResetNodeSwitches @ 0x1001d2b0 (HLIL 24502-24504), called once per bot per frame at 0x10028b7e (HLIL 32108), immediately before the 50-iteration node loop. - BotRecordNodeSwitch @ 0x1001d3a0 (HLIL 24570-24579). HLIL 24576 is decisive: `sub_10044c56(data_100644a0 * 0x90 + 0x10064a80, "%s at %2.1f entered %s: %s\n")` with args ClientName(bs->client), AAS_Time(), node, str, then numnodeswitches++. Called from all 13 AIEnter_* sites: "intermission"/"observer"/"stand"/"respawn"/"activate entity"/"battle fight"/"battle chase"/"battle retreat"/"battle NBG" each with "" (&data_1006294c), and "seek NBG"/"seek LTG" with BotGoalName(goal->number) (sub_1002f6a0, goal field +0x2c) or the literal "no goal". - The record-concatenation half of BotDumpNodeSwitches @ 0x1001d2d0 (HLIL 24509-24565). We implement only the header sprintf (HLIL 24518, format "%s at %1.1f switched more than %d AI nodes\n" — note %1.1f here vs %2.1f in the record format); we omit the inline-strcat loop at HLIL 24519-24561 that appends all numnodeswitches records before the single PRT_FATAL Print at 24564-24565. Retail passes the fully concatenated buffer to botimport.Print as the format string itself, one message. Our side: BotAI_RunNodeSwitchLoop (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:6647-6669) only counts iterations into state->ai_node_switches; the overflow site at bot_interface.c:9048-9054 emits BotDumpGoalStack, BotDumpAvoidGoals and the bare header. Grep across src/ finds no record buffer, no "entered %s" format, and none of the 13 node-name literals, so the behaviour is not handled in any caller either. Correction to the original claim's framing: bot_interface.c:8082-8088 (BotAI_ConsumeDeferredNodeSwitch) is a reconstruction artifact of our terminal-work split and has no retail counterpart — retail dumps at exactly one place, 0x10028bb3. Impact is diagnostic-only: nothing downstream reads numnodeswitches or the record array, so AI behaviour is unchanged. The divergence is that retail's single PRT_FATAL message contains the header plus up to 50 "<name> at <t> entered <node>: <goalname>" lines, while ours contains the header alone.

**Failure scenario.** A bot thrashes between AI nodes and hits the 50-switch limit (e.g. Seek NBG <-> Seek LTG with an unreachable goal). Retail prints one PRT_FATAL message containing the header followed by up to 50 lines naming each node entered and its goal name — the diagnostic that makes the loop debuggable. Ours prints only `"<name> at <t> switched more than 50 AI nodes"` with no trace of which nodes were entered.

**Fix.** 1. Add the two retail globals in the TU that owns the AI-node code (do not create a new source file): a `numnodeswitches` counter and a `nodeswitch[BOT_AI_MAX_NODE_SWITCHES + 1][144]` record array, matching retail's 51x144 = 7344-byte extent. 2. Add BotResetNodeSwitches (0x1001d2b0) — `numnodeswitches = 0` — and call it exactly where retail does: at the top of BotAI_RunNodeSwitchLoop next to `state->ai_node_switches = 0` (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:6656), which is the direct analogue of 0x10028b7e. Do not reset it anywhere else; note retail's AIEnter_Stand call at 0x10028b75 happens *before* the reset, so its record is intentionally discarded. 3. Add BotRecordNodeSwitch(state, node, str) (0x1001d3a0) formatting `"%s at %2.1f entered %s: %s\n"` with BotState_ClientName(state->client_number), AAS_Time(), node, str into `nodeswitch[numnodeswitches]` and incrementing. Call it from each of our AIEnter_* equivalents with the exact retail strings: "intermission", "observer", "stand", "respawn", "activate entity", "battle fight", "battle chase", "battle retreat", "battle NBG" all with "", and "seek NBG"/"seek LTG" with BotGoalName(goal->number) when a goal was selected and the literal "no goal" otherwise. 4. At the overflow site in bot_interface.c:9050, build the retail message as one string: sprintf the existing header `"%s at %1.1f switched more than %d AI nodes\n"` (ClientName, AAS_Time(), 50) into a buffer, strcat every nodeswitch[i] for i in [0, numnodeswitches), then a single BotInterface_Printf(PRT_FATAL, "%s", buf). Use "%s" rather than passing the buffer as the format string — retail passes it directly and would misinterpret a '%' in a goal name, but no retail goal name contains one, so this is behaviour-preserving and safer. Size the buffer generously and bound the concatenation: retail's is only 1400 bytes (var_578) against up to 50 x 143 bytes of records, so retail can smash its own stack; do not reproduce that. 5. Leave bot_interface.c:8084 as-is or route it through the same helper, but do not treat it as a retail parity point — it has no retail counterpart. Severity is low and the change is diagnostic-only; if the 13 call-site edits are judged not worth the churn, the honest minimal alternative is to leave the code alone and record the gap rather than half-implement it.

## `src/botlib/interface/bot_state.c`

### LOW ClientName/ClientSkin accept client == num_clients and skip the out-of-range warning

- status: **applied**
- retail: `ClientName / ClientSkin` @ `0x10028f30 / 0x10028f80`
- HLIL: 32245-32254 and 32255-32265 (deciding lines `10028f3e  if (arg1 s>= 0 && arg1 s< data_10064028)` and `10028f8e  if (arg1 s>= 0 && arg1 s< data_10064028)`)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_main.c:114-133
- ours: `src/botlib/interface/bot_state.c:805`

**Divergence.** Retail `ClientName` (0x10028f30, HLIL L32247) and `ClientSkin` (0x10028f80, HLIL L32257) use a STRICT bound `client >= 0 && client < botstate.num_clients`; anything else prints `"ClientName: client %d out of range\n"` / `"ClientSkin: client %d out of range\n"` at PRT_WARNING (2) and returns the empty string at 0x1006294c. This is deliberately stricter than `ValidClientNumber` (0x10037900, HLIL L43371), which is inclusive (`arg1 s<= data_10064028`), because the settings table is allocated at exactly `num_clients * 0x90` bytes (HLIL L32746). Our `BotState_ClientName` / `BotState_ClientSkin` (src/botlib/interface/bot_state.c:805, :826) delegate the whole range test to `BotState_SettingsSlot` (:119-135), which is INCLUSIVE: for `client == g_bot_client_capacity` it returns `&g_bot_client_settings_sentinel` instead of NULL, so the warning is never printed and whatever `BotClientSettings` last stored in the sentinel is returned as the name/skin. No caller re-imposes the strict bound. Reachable scenario (corrected): with maxclients = 4, a host calls `BotSetupClient(4)`. Our inclusive `BotInterface_ValidateClientNumber` (bot_interface.c:2285) accepts it, matching retail's `ValidClientNumber` at 0x10037f1e, and bot_interface.c:2918 stores `state->client_number = 4`. Every subsequent `BotState_ClientName(state->client_number)` / `BotState_ClientSkin(state->client_number)` (bot_interface.c:481, 3492, 3518, 3702, 5446, 5706, 8086, 9052) then silently returns sentinel data, where retail prints `ClientName: client 4 out of range` at PRT_WARNING and returns "". Note this is unreachable from Mr. Elusive's own game.dll, which maps client edicts through `DF_ENTCLIENT(x) = (x - 1 - g_edicts)` and so never exceeds maxclients-1; it is reachable only via the inclusive range botlib itself advertises.

**Failure scenario.** maxclients = 4. The game calls BotClientSettings(4, settings) - retail's Export_BotClientSettings guard (0x1003809d, ValidClientNumber, inclusive) accepts client 4 - and some later path asks for the name of client 4 (e.g. BotAI_ConsoleEasyClientName / team-leader formatting on a teammate index of 4). Retail prints `ClientName: client 4 out of range` at PRT_WARNING and returns "", so the formatted chat/console line carries an empty name. We silently return the sentinel's netname, emit no warning, and the chat line carries a real name retail would never produce.

**Fix.** In `BotState_ClientName` and `BotState_ClientSkin` (src/botlib/interface/bot_state.c:805, :826), test retail's strict bound directly before touching the slot, instead of inferring out-of-range from `BotState_SettingsSlot` returning NULL: if (client < 0 || client >= BotState_ClientCapacity()) { BotLib_Print(PRT_WARNING, "ClientName: client %d out of range\n", client); return ""; } then fetch the slot and, if it is NULL (pool not yet allocated — a state retail cannot reach because it would dereference a NULL base and crash), return "" WITHOUT printing, so the warning fires on exactly the inputs retail warns on and no others. Same for `BotState_ClientSkin` with the `ClientSkin:` text. Leave `BotState_SettingsSlot`, `BotState_StateSlot`, `BotState_ClientInRange`, and `BotInterface_ValidateClientNumber` untouched — `ValidClientNumber` (0x10037900), `BotSetupClient`, and `BotClientSettings` must keep the inclusive endpoint and its sentinel backing.

## `src/botlib/interface/botlib_interface.c`

### MEDIUM Library setup never seeds the CRT PRNG (srand missing)

- status: **applied**
- retail: `BotSetupLibrary` @ `0x10029c90`
- HLIL: 32726-32753 (deciding line 32728: `10029c99  sub_1004517a(sub_100457bc(nullptr))`)
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_main.c:571 (`srand(time(0));`)
- ours: `src/botlib/interface/botlib_interface.c:192`

**Divergence.** Retail `BotSetupLibrary` @ 0x10029c90 (HLIL 32726-32748) seeds the CRT PRNG as its very first act — HLIL 32728: `10029c99 sub_1004517a(sub_100457bc(nullptr))`, i.e. `srand(time(NULL))` — before BotSetupWeaponAI/BotSetupGoalAI/BotSetupChatAI. `sub_1004517a` (HLIL 54982-54986) writes its argument into the CRT per-thread slot at +0x14; `sub_10045187` (HLIL 54989-54994) is `rand`, reading that same slot as `seed*0x343fd + 0x269ec3` and returning `>>16 & 0x7fff`; `sub_100457bc` (HLIL 55673-55714) is the CRT `time()` (GetLocalTime/GetSystemTime/GetTimeZoneInformation). 0x10029c99 is the sole call site of `sub_1004517a` in the whole DLL, so this is retail's only seeding, and it is on the live setup path: the exported BotSetupLibrary `sub_10037bb0` reaches it at HLIL 43458 (`10037c78 *sub_10044f86() = j_sub_10029c90()`). Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_main.c:571 (`srand(time(0));`, annotated `// gladiator.dll: 10029C90..10029D59`). Our counterpart of 0x10029c90 is `Botlib_SetupAISubsystem` at src/botlib/interface/botlib_interface.c:184 (identified by the repo's own comment at :404 on the call site in `BotSetupLibrary`), which starts straight at `BotState_ResetClientSettings()` (:190) and `BotSetupWeaponAI()` (:192). Neither it, nor its caller `BotSetupLibrary` (:362, our 0x10037bb0 counterpart), nor the outer `BotSetupLibraryWrapper` (src/botlib/interface/bot_interface.c:2339) ever calls srand; `grep -rn srand src/` returns nothing, srand appearing only under tests/. Our code does consume the CRT `rand()` (src/botlib/ai/ai_dm.c:273, src/botlib/ai_chat/ai_chat.c:277 and :1142, src/botlib/ai_move/bot_move.c:114, src/botlib/ai_weight/ai_weight.c:993, src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:3081), so it runs the entire process off the implicit seed 1. No host masks this: Mr. Elusive's verbatim game/ source in ref/gladiator-bot-restored contains no srand — the botlib owned the seeding. Concretely: launch the same map with the same bot characters twice; retail's bots differ per launch, ours emit the identical chat lines, roll the identical console-chat probability gate (retail 0x100287fa `sub_10045187() & 0x7fff`), take the identical aim error and roam directions every single session.

**Failure scenario.** Start a server with the same map and the same bot characters twice. Retail: the seed differs per launch, so every rand()-driven decision diverges between runs - console-chat probability gate (0x100287fa `sub_10045187() & 0x7fff`), chat-message selection, aim spread/error, roam-goal direction, long-term-goal random. Ours: rand() emits the identical sequence from seed 1 on every launch, so the same bot on the same map says the same lines, rolls the same chat gate, and roams in the same directions every single session. Any host that itself calls srand() before GetBotAPI masks this; a host that does not (retail game.dll does not - the botlib owned the seeding) gets fully deterministic bots.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/interface/botlib_interface.c, add `#include <stdlib.h>` and `#include <time.h>`, then seed at the 0x10029c90 entry point. Retail has no already-initialised guard and seeds unconditionally on entry, so put the call in `Botlib_SetupAISubsystem` (line 184) *before* the `if (g_subsystem_state.ai_initialised)` early return, as the first statement of the function: srand((unsigned int)time(NULL)); (Equivalently, place it in `BotSetupLibrary` immediately before the `Botlib_SetupAISubsystem()` call at line 408 — that is the exact retail call site 0x10037c78. Both are behaviourally identical today because `Botlib_ResetSubsystemState()` at line 379 clears `ai_initialised` first, but seeding ahead of the guard matches retail's unconditional sequencing.) Do NOT seed any earlier than this point: retail runs `BotSetupMoveAI`, AAS setup (`j_sub_1000edc0` @ 0x10037c48) and the libvar lookups *before* the srand, so any rand() consumed during those (e.g. src/botlib/aas/aas_route.c:3081, retail 0x1001a43f) legitimately runs off seed 1 in retail too. Our existing ordering already puts `Botlib_SetupAISubsystem` after those, so no other change is needed. Matches ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/be_ai2_main.c:571. No test regression risk: the `srand(seed)` calls under tests/ (e.g. tests/parity/test_bot_interface.c:4838, :5096) all run mid-test after setup has completed, not before `BotSetupLibrary()`.

## `src/botlib/precomp/l_precomp.c`

### MEDIUM PC_EvaluateTokens uses fixed 64-entry stack heaps for values/operators; retail heap-allocates each one with no cap

- status: **open**
- retail: `PC_EvaluateTokens` @ `0x1003b9e0`
- HLIL: 46709, 46798, 47373, 47384 (dev_tools/gladiator.dll.bndb_hlil.txt)
- ours: `src/botlib/precomp/l_precomp.c:2431`

**Divergence.** PC_EvaluateTokens (retail 0x1003B9E0, HLIL 46667-47398) allocates every value_t with `GetClearedMemory(0x20)` (HLIL 46709, plus the same call on the `defined(...)` path) and every operator_t with `GetClearedMemory(0x14)` (HLIL 46798), and releases both lists with FreeMemory (`j_sub_100390b0`, HLIL 47373 and 47384) — `sub_10039000` is GetClearedMemory (HLIL 44276-44280) and `sub_100390b0` is FreeMemory (HLIL 44303-44314). Retail imposes no bound on the number of values or operators in an expression, and the strings "out of value space" / "out of operator space" do not exist in the DLL. Our src/botlib/precomp/l_precomp.c:2424-2449 replaces both allocators with the Quake III fixed-heap form: 64-entry stack arrays `pc_operator_t operator_heap[MAX_OPERATORS]` / `pc_value_t value_heap[MAX_VALUES]` (:2467-2470), bump-allocated by AllocValue/AllocOperator, which raise an invented SourceError and set error=1 once the counter reaches 64; FreeValue/FreeOperator are no-ops (:2439, :2449, used at :2875, :2883, :2894, :2900) and do not decrement the counters, so the cap is on cumulative allocations across the whole expression rather than on live nodes. Trigger: any #if/#elif or $evalint/$evalfloat expression with more than 64 operands or more than 64 operators built from numeric literals or defined() terms — e.g. `#if defined(A) || defined(B) || ...` with 65 terms, or `$evalint(1+1+...+1)` with 65 literals. Retail evaluates it and the directive succeeds; ours trips at the 65th node, prints `file <f>, line <n>: out of value space` (or `out of operator space`), sets error=1, PC_EvaluateTokens returns 0, and the source load is aborted. Note the claim's `$evalfloat(w1+w2+...+w65)` example is not a valid trigger: retail rejects any TT_NAME other than "defined" with "undefined name %s in #if/#elif" (HLIL 46875-46877) before any cap could matter.

**Failure scenario.** A conditional or $-directive whose expression contains more than 64 operands (or more than 64 operators) -- e.g. `#if defined(A) || defined(B) || ... ` with 65 terms, or a generated `$evalfloat(w1+w2+...+w65)` weight expression. Retail: GetClearedMemory keeps allocating, the expression evaluates normally and the directive succeeds. Ours: AllocValue trips at the 65th operand, prints `file <f>, line <n>: out of value space` (a diagnostic retail never emits), sets error=1, PC_EvaluateTokens returns 0, PC_Evaluate returns 0, the directive handler returns 0 and PC_ReadToken aborts the whole source load.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/precomp/l_precomp.c, restore the per-node allocation that is already sitting commented out at :2424-2427. Delete the MAX_VALUES/MAX_OPERATORS defines (:2429-2430), the two guarded macros (:2431-2438, :2441-2448) and both invented SourceError strings, and delete the `operator_heap`/`value_heap` arrays and the `numoperators`/`numvalues` counters at :2467-2470. Define instead: #define AllocValue(val) val = (pc_value_t *) GetClearedMemory(sizeof(pc_value_t)); #define FreeValue(val) FreeMemory(val) #define AllocOperator(op) op = (pc_operator_t *) GetClearedMemory(sizeof(pc_operator_t)); #define FreeOperator(op) FreeMemory(op) The existing call sites need no other change: the three AllocValue sites (:2490, :2516, :2561), the AllocOperator site (:2690), the in-reduction FreeValue/FreeOperator (:2875, :2883) and the two teardown loops (:2894, :2900) already sit exactly where retail's allocations and FreeMemory calls sit (HLIL 46709, 46798, 47373, 47384; ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_precomp.c:1425, :1462, :1552, :1770, :1778, :1819, :1830). Note the macros must remain statement-shaped without a trailing `break`, since AllocValue is used inside switch cases and the old macro's `break` was part of the invented overflow path. GetClearedMemory (not GetMemory) is required — retail zero-fills the block (HLIL 44279).

### MEDIUM PC_EvaluateTokens invents divide-by-zero and modulo-by-zero guards that retail does not have

- status: **open**
- retail: `PC_EvaluateTokens` @ `0x1003b9e0`
- HLIL: 47172-47181 (case 9 / case 0xa)
- ours: `src/botlib/precomp/l_precomp.c:2779`

**Divergence.** src/botlib/precomp/l_precomp.c:2779-2792 adds a divide-by-zero guard to the P_DIV and P_MOD arms of PC_EvaluateTokens that retail (0x1003b9e0) does not have, together with a diagnostic string that does not exist in the DLL. Retail evidence: HLIL 47172-47181. case 9 (P_DIV) is "1003bf1d *i_11 = divs.dp.d(sx.q(*i_11), *ecx_16)" followed by "1003bf22 *(i_11 + 8) = fconvert.d(x87_r7_7 / fconvert.t(*(ecx_16 + 8)))"; case 0xa (P_MOD) is "1003bf2f *i_11 = mods.dp.d(sx.q(*i_11), *ecx_16)". Both fall straight through to "goto label_1003c1cb" with no conditional branch and no SourceError call. The DLL's complete, contiguous #if/#elif diagnostic string block (HLIL 76043-76131, 1005f8d0..1005fba8) contains every message this routine can emit and has no "divide by zero" entry; grepping the whole HLIL for /zero/i finds only the unrelated "negative value set to zero\n" at 1005e474. ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_precomp.c:1632-1639 independently shows the same unguarded arms. Two observable consequences: (a) Divisor zero in the integer lane. `#if 1/0`, `#if 5%0`, or `$evalfloat(1/0.5)` (the token 0.5 gets intvalue = (unsigned long)0.5 = 0 via src/botlib/precomp/l_script.c:1091). Retail executes the integer lane FIRST at 1003bf1d, so the idiv raises EXCEPTION_INT_DIVIDE_BY_ZERO before the float lane at 1003bf22 runs at all. Ours short-circuits at the guard, emits "file <f>, line <n>: divide by zero in #if/#elif", and PC_EvaluateTokens returns qfalse (error propagates via l_precomp.c:2861 -> 2902-2905), so the enclosing file fails to load instead of faulting. (b) The P_DIV guard is additionally over-broad: `!v2->intvalue || !v2->floatvalue` rejects a divisor whose integer lane is fine but whose float lane happens to be 0.0.
