Because the integer-only operators leave the float lane untouched in both retail ("1003c163 case 0xe: *i_11 |= *ecx_16", likewise cases 0xd &, 0xf ^, 6 >>, 7 <<) and ours (l_precomp.c:2814-2823), an expression such as `#if 6/(0|3)` produces a divisor with intvalue==3 and floatvalue==0.0. Retail computes 6/3 == 2 in the integer lane, and 6.0/0.0 yields +inf in the float lane without trapping (MSVC's default x87 control word masks all FP exceptions), so the #if evaluates true. Ours aborts the entire source read with the invented error. This is a retail-succeeds / we-fail divergence with no crash on either side. Both guards are verbatim Quake III l_precomp.c code, added years after Gladiator 0.96.

**Failure scenario.** `$evalfloat(1/0.5)` (integer=0 evaluation): the token `0.5` has intvalue==0, floatvalue==0.5. Retail executes both lanes -- the float division yields 2.0 and the integer division by zero raises a hardware divide fault. Ours short-circuits at the guard, emits `file <f>, line <n>: divide by zero in #if/#elif`, returns 0 from PC_EvaluateTokens, and PC_DollarDirective_evalfloat fails, so the enclosing weight/character file fails to load rather than faulting. Same for `#if 1/0` and `#if 5%0`.

**Fix.** In src/botlib/precomp/l_precomp.c, delete both invented guards (lines 2779-2784 and 2787-2792), leaving the arms unconditional, matching 0x1003bf17 (case 9) and 0x1003bf2c (case 0xa): case P_DIV: v1->intvalue /= v2->intvalue; v1->floatvalue /= v2->floatvalue; break; case P_MOD: v1->intvalue %= v2->intvalue; break; The claim's proposed fix is correct as written. Note the operand-ordering detail is cosmetic and needs no special handling: HLIL 47173 shows retail loading v1->floatvalue into x87 (fld at 1003bf18) BEFORE the idiv at 1003bf1d, matching ref l_precomp.c:1633-1635 (`v24 = v->floatvalue; v->intvalue /= v2->intvalue; v->floatvalue = v24 / v2->floatvalue;`). Since v2 is always v1->next and therefore a distinct node, and the integer divide never touches the float member, `v1->floatvalue /= v2->floatvalue` is behaviourally identical to the pre-loaded form. Write it either way. Consequence to accept deliberately: after this change `#if 1/0`, `#if 5%0`, and `$evalfloat(1/0.5)` will divide by zero exactly as retail does. If the build must not fault on hostile botfiles, gate the guard behind an explicitly non-retail compile-time option (e.g. `#ifdef PC_SAFE_DIVIDE`) that is off in the parity build, rather than leaving it unconditionally in the retail path. Do not keep the `!v2->floatvalue` disjunct in any form on the retail path even if the integer check is retained for crash-safety, since that half rejects divisors retail divides by successfully (see case (b) above). Also drop the string "divide by zero in #if/#elif\n" entirely -- it has no counterpart in the DLL's diagnostic block at 1005f8d0..1005fba8, so any parity check that diffs emitted SourceError text will flag it.

## `src/botlib/precomp/l_script.c`

### MEDIUM PS_ReadNumber does not parse 0b/0B binary literals — the branch is behind an undefined BINARYNUMBERS macro

- status: **applied**
- retail: `PS_ReadNumber` @ `0x1003ecd0`
- HLIL: 49569-49604
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_script.c:516-532
- ours: `src/botlib/precomp/l_script.c:1165`

**Divergence.** PS_ReadNumber (retail 0x1003ecd0, HLIL 49569-49604) unconditionally implements a 0b/0B binary-literal branch: HLIL 49575 `if (ebx.b != 0x62 && ebx.b != 0x42) goto label_1003ee23` admits '0' followed by 'b'/'B', 49578-49584 emit string[0]='0'/string[1]=prefix with len=2, 49586-49602 accumulate 0/1 digits with a dedicated "binary number longer than MAX_TOKEN = %d" error, and 49590 sets subtype |= 0x400 (TT_BINARY). NumberValue's matching arm is live at 0x1003ec27 (`arg2 & 4` -> string += 2 -> `<<1` accumulation). Retail gates none of this on a script flag. Our equivalent branch at src/botlib/precomp/l_script.c:1165-1187 is inside `#ifdef BINARYNUMBERS`, and BINARYNUMBERS is defined nowhere in src/ or the build system (only in dev_tools/Quake-III-Arena-master/code/botlib/l_script.h:33), so the branch is compiled out and NumberValue's TT_BINARY arm at l_script.c:1119 is unreachable. Nothing compensates one level up: PS_ReadToken (l_script.c:1435-1439) dispatches on the leading digit alone, and SCFL_NOBINARYNUMBERS (l_script.h:55) is a dead enumerator with no consumer. For input `0b101` retail produces token.string="0b101", subtype 0x1400, intvalue 5, floatvalue 5, script_p past the literal; we produce "0", subtype 0x1200 (TT_OCTAL|TT_INTEGER), intvalue 0, and leave script_p on 'b' so the next PS_ReadToken yields a spurious TT_NAME "b101" that desynchronises the token stream. Scope note: no stock Gladiator data file in the repo uses 0b/0B literals, so this is reached via mod- or user-authored scripts, not a default install.

**Failure scenario.** Input token `0b101`. Retail: token.string = "0b101", subtype = TT_BINARY|TT_INTEGER (0x1400), intvalue = 5, floatvalue = 5.0, script_p advanced past the whole literal. Ours: the decimal/octal else-branch stores just `0`, breaks on 'b', sets TT_OCTAL|TT_INTEGER (0x1200), intvalue = 0, and leaves script_p on 'b' so the next PS_ReadToken returns a TT_NAME token "b101" — a bogus extra token that desynchronises every downstream parser (weight expressions, struct readers, #if evaluation).

**Fix.** Make the branch unconditional, matching retail, which has no gate. Cleanest option: delete the `#ifdef BINARYNUMBERS` / `#endif //BINARYNUMBERS` pair at src/botlib/precomp/l_script.c:1165 and :1187 so the 0b/0B else-if is always compiled. If the repo prefers to keep the historical macro spelling, instead add `#define BINARYNUMBERS` to src/botlib/precomp/l_script.h alongside the existing `#define DOLLAR` (line 6) and `#define NUMBERVALUE` (line 8) — that also makes NumberValue's TT_BINARY arm at l_script.c:1119 live. Either way, do NOT add a runtime check of SCFL_NOBINARYNUMBERS: retail never consults a flag here, and that enumerator (l_script.h:55) has no consumer.

### MEDIUM LoadScriptFile prints PRT_ERROR diagnostics on every failed open; retail returns NULL silently

- status: **applied**
- retail: `LoadScriptFile` @ `0x100401a0`
- HLIL: 50598-50656
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_script.c:1057-1066
- ours: `src/botlib/precomp/l_script.c:2027`

**Divergence.** LoadScriptFile @ 0x100401a0 returns NULL silently when fopen fails — HLIL 50601-50604 is the whole failure path (`eax = sub_10044936(esi, "rb"); if (eax == 0) return eax`), and the routine contains no botimport.Print outside the CRC/__squatt branch after `j_sub_10037850` (HLIL 50655; ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_script.c:1100-1104). Our src/botlib/precomp/l_script.c:2027 emits BotLib_Print(PRT_ERROR, "LoadScriptFile: failed to open %s (%s)"), which is ungated (src/botlib/common/l_log.c:271-284) and renders as "Error: ..." via gi.dprintf (ref/gladiator-bot-restored/game/bl_main.c:766-771). Retail treats a failed open as ordinary control flow: PC_Directive_include @ 0x1003a7a0 probes LoadScriptFile three times — HLIL 45712 (raw name), 45763 (includepath+name), 45777 (pak-resolved, with offset/length from j_sub_10041f60 at 45772) — and only reaches `SourceError(source, "file %s not found")` at 45781 if all three miss. Our ladder at l_precomp.c:1657/1686/1689/1699 mirrors that, so a pak-backed include that ultimately succeeds prints two spurious "Error: LoadScriptFile: failed to open ..." lines that retail never emits; the same happens once per optional file probed through PC_LoadSourceFile -> LoadSourceFile (l_precomp.c:3979) that does not exist on disk. Note the claim's "every #include, three prints per include" is too broad: our LoadScriptFile prefixes basefolder (l_script.c:1989-1996) where retail does not, so the first probe often hits. Note also that only the fopen print is a pure added-diagnostic divergence — retail has no empty-file guard and does not survive a short read at all (50645-50650 frees and NULLs the script, then dereferences `*(s_1 + 0x104)` and faults), so l_script.c:2034 and 2066 diverge in control flow, not merely in output.

**Failure scenario.** Every `#include "inv.h"` in a Gladiator bot file. l_precomp.c's PC_Directive_include probes LoadScriptFile up to three times (l_precomp.c:1657, 1686, then PC_LoadResolvedScriptFile at 443), and PC_LoadSourceFile probes again via LoadSourceFile (l_precomp.c:3979). Retail's failed probes are silent and only the final `SourceError(source, "file %s not found")` reaches the console; we print one `LoadScriptFile: failed to open <path> (No such file or directory)` line at PRT_ERROR severity per probe, so a normal successful bot load spams the Q2 console with errors that retail never emits and that make a healthy load look broken.

**Fix.** Remove the BotLib_Print(PRT_ERROR, ...) at src/botlib/precomp/l_script.c:2027 and return NULL silently, matching 0x100401c5; if the diagnostic has debugging value, demote it to Log_Write/BotLib_LogWrite so it never reaches botimport.Print. Do the same for the two basefolder path-composition prints at 2000 and 2008/2016 — they guard a shim retail does not have, and retail's fixed-buffer strcpy has no truncation diagnostic. Leave the caller's SourceError(source, "file %s not found") (l_precomp.c:1796) as the only user-visible message, matching HLIL 45781. Do NOT fold the empty-file (2034) and short-read (2066) prints into this change: retail has no empty-file guard and faults on a short read (HLIL 50645-50650 frees the script, sets it NULL, then dereferences *(s_1 + 0x104)), so those two sites are a separate parity question about whether to reproduce or deliberately harden against that fault, and should be filed on their own rather than silently un-printed here.

### LOW StripDoubleQuotes strips only one leading and one trailing quote; retail loops until none remain

- status: **applied**
- retail: `StripDoubleQuotes` @ `0x1003fcb0`
- HLIL: 50272-50331
- reference: ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_script.c:892-910
- ours: `src/botlib/precomp/l_script.c:1761`

**Divergence.** StripDoubleQuotes (retail 0x1003fcb0, HLIL 50272-50331) strips ALL leading and ALL trailing double quotes; our src/botlib/precomp/l_script.c:1761 strips at most one from each end. HLIL 50274 `while (*arg1 == 0x22)` is a loop around the inlined `strcpy(string, string+1)` (rep movs at 1003fcd5/1003fcdc), and 1003fcf6/1003fd17 form an if + do/while, i.e. `while (string[strlen(string)-1] == '"') string[strlen(string)-1] = 0;`. Our code uses two plain `if`s. Corroborated by ref/gladiator-bot-restored/botlib/l_script.c (`gladiator.dll: 1003FCB0..1003FD1B`), which uses `while` for both and comments that the outer `while` — not Q3's single `if` — matches the original's loopback. StripSingleQuotes (retail 0x1003fd40, HLIL 50333+, `1003fd48 while (*arg1 == 0x27)`) has the IDENTICAL divergence at src/botlib/precomp/l_script.c:1776 and must be fixed in the same commit; it is reached from l_struct.c:232 (FIELDTYPE_CHAR). Two reachable triggers, not one: (a) Escaped quotes in a quoted value, e.g. `name "\"Grunt\""`. PS_ReadString (l_script.c:942) stores the surrounding quotes and PS_ReadEscapeCharacter resolves `\"` to a literal `"` (retail: ref l_script.c:268); escapes are on by default, SCFL_NOSTRINGESCAPECHARS being set only at src/botlib/aas/aas_map.c:6047 for map-entity parsing. StripDoubleQuotes therefore receives `""Grunt""`: retail yields `Grunt`, we yield `"Grunt"`, leaving stray quotes in the struct string field. (b) The stringize operator: PC_StringizeTokens (l_precomp.c:1113) wraps concatenated token text in quotes without escaping, so stringizing a TT_STRING token produces `""inner""`, which PC_ReadTokenHandle (l_precomp.c:4181) hands to StripDoubleQuotes. Reachable call sites: l_struct.c:232/253, ai_character.c:338/460/714, ai_weight.c:703, bot_goal.c:2845/3191, l_precomp.c:1655/4181. No caller pre- or post-strips, so the behaviour is not handled one level up. Impact in practice is limited: no stock asset (bots.cfg, script.c, default/defaul_[cimw].c) contains an escaped quote or a stringize, so only user-authored configs expose it.

**Failure scenario.** A script value written with escaped quotes, e.g. `name "\"Grunt\""`. PS_ReadString resolves the escapes and hands StripDoubleQuotes the buffer `""Grunt""`. Retail returns `Grunt`; we return `"Grunt"`, so the bot name / struct string field keeps stray quote characters. Reachable from l_struct.c:253, ai_character.c:338/460/714, ai_weight.c:703, bot_goal.c:2845/3191 and l_precomp.c:1655 (an `#include` spelt with escaped quotes then resolves to a filename with quotes in it).

**Fix.** In src/botlib/precomp/l_script.c, change both `if`s to `while`s in StripDoubleQuotes (line 1761) and, identically, in StripSingleQuotes (line 1776): void StripDoubleQuotes(char *string) { while (*string == '\"') { strcpy(string, string+1); } //end while while (string[strlen(string)-1] == '\"') { string[strlen(string)-1] = '\0'; } //end while } //end of the function StripDoubleQuotes and the same shape with '\'' for StripSingleQuotes. Keep the `strlen(string)-1` read unguarded — retail performs the same underflowing access once the leading loop consumes the whole string (input `""` leaves it empty and retail then touches string[-1]); the reference reconstruction leaves it unguarded for the same reason. Add a short comment noting the deliberate retail-faithful underflow so a later reader does not "fix" it. Do not add an emptiness guard: that would be an invented guard changing behaviour retail exhibits. Note on the overlapping strcpy: our tree already calls strcpy(string, string+1) here, which is UB under a modern SIMD strcpy. If the build targets anything other than vintage i386, prefer memmove(string, string + 1, strlen(string)) inside the loop — byte-identical result, defined behaviour — matching the guarded pattern the reference uses.
